# A CHEMICAL DICTIONARY <note>original Latin title: "Lexicon Chymicum"</note>.

->With<-

## obscure words,

->And<-

## Hermetic matters <note>Hermeticism refers to the philosophical and alchemical tradition following the teachings of the legendary Hermes Trismegistus</note>,

->As well as<-

## Paracelsian phrases <note>relating to Paracelsus, the influential 16th-century Swiss physician who combined medicine with alchemical principles</note>,

->Found in his writings:<-

->And those of other *chemists* <note>original: "Chymicorum"; in the 1600s, this term included both what we now call chemists and alchemists</note>, occurring everywhere,<-
->containing a plain<-
->explanation.<-

---

->By *William Johnson, Chemist*.<-

->Printed by *G. D.* at the expense of *William Nea*-<-
->land*, at whose shop they are offered for sale under the Sign<-
->of the Crown, in the street commonly called<-
->*Duck Lane* / 1657.<-

# TO THE MOST LEARNED MAN,
## FRANCIS PRUJEAN, <note>Sir Francis Prujean (1593–1666) was an eminent English physician who served as the President of the College of Physicians from 1650 to 1654.</note>

->*Accomplished in medicine and other fine literature, President of the Society of Physicians of London <note>Now known as the Royal College of Physicians.</note>, and to the rest of the most distinguished colleagues of that same Society, these tender blossoms of his talent,*<-

->William Johnson,<-
->Practitioner in *Chemistry*,<-
->Dedicates and devotes.<-

Since, because of your singular kindness toward me (most learned men), as you were zealously striving for the common health and public benefit, you had arranged for a *Chemical* laboratory <note>original: *officinam Chymicam*; at this time, chemistry was closely tied to the preparation of refined medicines (galenicals and spagyrics).</note> to be built separately in the garden of your College, and you had summoned me (though unworthy) to be your Practitioner, and by the help of this work— <note>The sentence is interrupted by the page break; the catchword "ris" indicates the next word is "operis" (of the work).</note>

<summary>This is the dedication page of a chemical or pharmaceutical text by William Johnson, dedicated to Sir Francis Prujean and the Royal College of Physicians. It marks Johnson's appointment as the College's chemist and the establishment of their laboratory in London.</summary>

of the work, you had appointed the most skilled of your colleagues as Overseers <note>original: *Isostates*; a term derived from Greek meaning "standing equal" or "supervisors" appointed to ensure quality.</note> (to use your own words), so that medicines might be compounded as much more cautiously as they are more effective; as much more diligently as they are more difficult; and as much more purely as they are more precious. I could not help but strive, with all my strength, not only to show evidence of my gratitude toward you, but also to render to you and to the public some account (though I confess it is a meager one) of my stewardship <note>original: *villicationis*; a Latin term for the management of an estate or office, here referring to his role as the College chemist.</note>. # Preface to the Rea-
# der.

I cannot help but won-
der that the adversaries of *Paracelsus* <note>Philippus Aureolus Theophrastus Bombastus von Hohenheim (1493–1541), a Swiss physician and alchemist who pioneered the use of chemicals and minerals in medicine.</note>,
since they have no just slanders that they might
justly thrust upon him, only accuse him of writ-
ing obscurely and of concealing his <term>secrets</term> <note>original: *arcana*; in alchemical terms, these are the hidden "mysteries" or powerful medicinal essences of substances.</note> concerning his studies with invented words;
just as the adversaries of *Jan Baptist van Helmont* <note>A 17th-century Flemish chemist and physician who was deeply influenced by Paracelsus but also criticized established medical dogmas.</note>
stimulate him by continually agitating and irri-
tating him to draw out his secrets—acquired with
much sweat and expense—to bring them into the
light, and to openly reveal them, whether willing
or unwilling, even without gratitude, to those
who gape with envy. But those spears which they
have hurled at this eminent man should rightly
be turned back against themselves. Nor can I
fail to accuse *Weyer the Physician* <note>original: *Wierum Medicum*; referring to Johann Weyer (1515–1588), a Dutch physician known for his opposition to the persecution of witches and his work on medicine.</note>, who,
observing that *Paracelsus* had cured his sick
patients almost to the point of miracles,

<summary>In this preface, William Johnson defends the alchemists Paracelsus and Van Helmont against critics who claim their writing is intentionally obscure. He argues that such secrecy is a necessary protection for their hard-won discoveries and that their detractors are driven by envy of their miraculous cures.</summary>
<keywords>Paracelsus, Jan Baptist van Helmont, Johann Weyer, arcana, alchemy, medical controversy, secrets, defense of alchemy</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the defense of Paracelsus against his critics, specifically addressing the physician Johann Weyer (mentioned on the previous page) and the influential letter written by Paracelsus’s former secretary, Johannes Oporinus. cured <note>The OCR "raſſe" completes the Latin word *curasse* from the previous page, referring to the patients Paracelsus healed.</note> those whom he himself could neither cure nor wished to; he shamefully begged for a **Letter** from *Johannes Oporinus*, the **amanuensis** <note>A secretary or literary assistant; Oporinus (1507–1568) was a famous Basel printer who served Paracelsus but later wrote a disparaging account of his master’s private life.</note> of *Theophrastus Paracelsus*, which he afterwards brought to light. He did this so that, deliberately and on purpose, by invidiously twisting the words of that **Letter** and interpreting them in a sinister way, he might defile the honor of that incomparable man.

Nevertheless, there are more things in that **Letter** that pertain to the praise of *Theophrastus* than to his blame; and those things which he censures there are to be understood far differently than either *Oporinus* or others have interpreted them. Indeed, *Oporinus* himself—while walking in a meadow by the *Rhine* with *Michael Toxites*, the Physician <note>A German-born physician and poet (1514–1581) who became a major editor and defender of Paracelsus’s works.</note>, as they were sailing together from *Basel* to *Strasbourg* <note>original: *Argentoratum*</note>—acknowledged this with repentance, as *Toxites* himself testifies.

Even our own *Fuller* <note>Thomas Fuller (1608–1661), an English churchman and historian who had written a skeptical account of Paracelsus in his book *The Holy State and the Profane State* (1642).</note>, a man of prodigious memory but here forgetting himself, followed in the same path and handed down these stories without investigation. If he had consulted with those who wrote about *Paracelsus*, or with the man's own books, he would have perceived with a slight glance the mutual correspondence between *Erasmus of Rotterdam* <note>The famous humanist scholar who sought medical advice from Paracelsus in 1527.</note>—his peer, if I am not mistaken—and many other noteworthy physicians of that same time, and *Paracelsus* himself. But the **Epitaph** of *Para-*

<meta>This page continues the defense of the physician Paracelsus. The first word "celsi" completes the name "Paracelsus" from the previous page. The text describes Paracelsus's tomb in Salzburg and argues that his "obscurity" is actually a lack of understanding by his critics, who favor ancient Greek medical theories over the "light of nature" and Christian scripture.</meta>

...celsus, we may view his monument on the wall near **St. Sebastian’s**, in the hospital, erected by the *Archbishop of Salzburg*. It bears these words: 

> *Here is buried* **Philippus Aureolus Paracelsus**, *Distinguished Doctor of Medicine, who, with miraculous art, removed those dreadful afflictions,* **Leprosy, Gout, Dropsy,** <note>original: *Hydropisin*. An old medical term for edema or swelling caused by fluid retention.</note> *and other incurable contagions of the body, and honored his legacy by distributing and allotting his goods to the poor. In the Year of Our Lord **1541**, on the **24th day of September**, he exchanged life for death.*

But let these things concerning **Paracelsus** suffice for now. If **Paracelsus** is somewhat obscure, and his writings more intricate than those of meager talent are able to follow—as they are forced to admit—it must be attributed to their own ignorance; otherwise, they would not call his works obscure and unintelligible. Indeed, the criticism of things not understood might well be called blindness and stupidity. Nor should the doctrine of **Paracelsus** be rejected simply because it does not please the schools of the **Greeks** <note>This refers to the traditional medical establishment based on the ancient Greek teachings of Galen and Hippocrates, which Paracelsus famously challenged.</note> and the infidels. For we should no more agree with the fictions of the **Greeks** than with the sure **Word of God**, upon which alone Christians ought to lean. Rather, we should embrace the light of nature, supported by the **Word of God**, than the darkness of the **Greeks**...

<summary>This page describes the epitaph of Paracelsus at St. Sebastian’s in Salzburg and defends his complex writing style against critics who favor traditional Greek medicine over Paracelsus's "light of nature."</summary>

## God sells every excellence at the price of labor.
<note>original: "Quicquid præclarum est vendit Deus omne labore." This is a Latin rendering of a Greek maxim by Epicharmus, emphasizing that divine wisdom and great achievements require hard work and dedication.</note>

*If the adversaries of **Paracelsus** strive to overcome him, let them do so not by the authorities of men—especially those who are unfaithful—but by the **Holy Scriptures** of God, upon which true **Philosophy** rests. Nor do we agree with **Paracelsus** merely because he is a most learned man: **Plato** is my friend, **Socrates** is my friend, but **Truth** is a greater friend.* <note>original: "Amicus Plato, amicus Socrates, sed magis amica Veritas." This famous proverb asserts that a scholar's highest loyalty is to the truth itself, rather than to any specific teacher or school of thought.</note> *Nor should **Paracelsus** be read haphazardly; for just as the entrance to the inner sanctum of the temple was opened to no one but the **Priests**, so no admission is granted to the secrets of **Paracelsus** except to learned and upright men. Whoever reads and considers these few hidden things here collected, seriously and diligently, let them provide him an entrance to the **Temple of Apollo**.* <note>In this context, the "Temple of Apollo" symbolizes the height of medical and prophetic wisdom, as Apollo was the god of healing.</note> *These are the veils of the **Hermeticists** <term>Hermeticists: Followers of the Hermetic tradition, an ancient system of spiritual philosophy, alchemy, and science attributed to the legendary Hermes Trismegistus.</term>, in which the truth of the sealed matter is wrapped and entwined—like **Falernian** wine <note>A prestigious, high-quality wine highly praised in ancient Rome.</note> in the grape, and gold in the dross. I confess these things are not my own, but have merely been reduced into a small bundle and organized by me, so that to*

He positions himself as a compiler who has organized these hidden truths for the benefit of diligent readers.</summary>

The learner ought to believe the teacher; <note>original: "Oportet discentem docenti credere." ## Upon the Book.

> The secrets hidden beneath obscure words,
> Through which **Paracelsus** <note>Philippus Aureolus Theophrastus Bombastus von Hohenheim (1493–1541), known as Paracelsus, was a pioneer of the "medical revolution" who emphasized observation and chemistry over traditional ancient authorities.</note> brings aid to the suffering:
> Here, the path is shown clearly, and hidden things are unlocked;
> You will find the veils removed from your own eyes.
> What was once difficult, you will now see as accessible and clear,
> So that your mind might be better prepared for your studies.
> Read this little handbook; <term>handbook: original "fasciculum," literally a small bundle or packet of papers.</term> it is brief, yet it reveals deep mysteries.
> Let this one book serve you as many others would.

->N. H.<-

## A.

<margin>Andrea Bacci, <note>Andrea Bacci (1524–1600) was an Italian physician and philosopher, famous for his works on natural history and wines.</note> book concerning the Elk.</margin>
**Alces** [Elk], from <note>original: "ἀλκή"</note> **strength**, a Great Beast: it is a social animal, cloven-hoofed, resembling a **Deer** in shape though it is larger; it is timid by nature and subject to the <term>falling sickness: a historical term for epilepsy, known in Latin as "morbus comitialis" because public assemblies (comitia) were suspended if someone suffered a seizure.</term> They report that not every animal of this species is seized by the epileptic evil, and that the hoof of the female possesses no power in this regard, but only that of the **Male**; nor the young, but the adults, and specifically at that time when they begin to be driven by <note>original: "in Venerem ferri," a poetic way of describing the mating urge or rutting season.</note> desire (between the feast of the Assumption of Mary <note>August 15th.</note> and her birth <note>September 8th.</note>). They even attribute more power to the rear hooves than the front ones, and of these, some prefer the right foot (which is more common), others the left. There are those who maintain these hooves must be cut from the animal while it is still alive at the aforementioned time.

Physicians use the **Hoof** and **Sinews** in a dose from a <term>half-scruple: a small unit of weight used by apothecaries, roughly 0.65 grams.</term> to one **scruple**, administered with a specific <term>vehicle: a liquid substance, such as water or wine, used to deliver a medicine.</term>

The **Hoof** is celebrated for its specific virtue against epi-

HEADER: CHEMICAL DICTIONARY.

...epilepsy, both in prevention and in cure, using it both internally and externally.

Internally, shavings and the specific preparations listed below are administered.

Externally, a small piece is set in a ring and worn on the (commonly called) ring finger—the one next to the little finger—so that it faces the palm; or worn on that same finger in a ring specifically carved for this purpose. It is also placed in the hand and applied to the pulse, put into the (left) ear, and hung from the neck so that it touches the skin.

It is reported to be recognized by its scent, which it gives off as a pleasant incense when burned.

**Acte**, Dwarf Elder, or Ground Elder <note>original: "Ebulus, seu Sambucus humilis."</note>. A common and well-known plant. *Quercetanus* <note>Joseph Duchesne (c. 1544–1609), a French physician and alchemist who promoted Paracelsian medicine.</note> highly praises the *Tragea* <note>A medicinal powder or confection.</note> made from the berries of this plant according to his recipe for stopping all kinds of flows <note>original: "fluxus," referring to bodily discharges like diarrhea or hemorrhaging.</note>, and rightly so, above many other medicines.

**Abesamum**, Wheel-mud <note>original: "Lutum rotæ," the grease or grime that collects on a wagon wheel axle, sometimes used in folk medicine.</note>.

**Abesum**, Quicklime <note>original: "Calx viva."</note>.

**Abicum**, A lid or cover.

**Ablutio** [Washing], This is a process of refinement through repeated infusions, washing away unclean things and restoring them to purity. It includes <term>Imbibition: the process of soaking a solid in a liquid until it is saturated</term> and <term>Cohobation: a redistillation process where the distilled liquid is poured back onto the remaining solid matter.</term>

**Ablatio** [Removal], This is the drawing away of matter from the upper part. It is performed in various ways: for dry materials that adhere lightly, cleaning can be done with a **Hare’s foot** or similar tools. Some things we remove with a **feather, a knife, or spatulas**; others we wipe away with a tight cloth or with **brushes** made of bristles, iron, or wood.

**Abric, Kibrit, Chybur**, This is **Sulfur**.

**Abessi**, The same as **Rebis**. Specifically, the final matter of food <note>In the Paracelsian tradition, this refers to the waste or excrement left after the body's internal "alchemist" (the Archeus) has extracted the nutrition.</note>.

**Achahi**, This is **Alum water**.

# CHEMICAL DICTIONARY.

*Acalai* is Salt.
*Acartum* is Red Lead <note>original: "minium," a bright red pigment made from lead tetroxide</note>.
*Acaid* is vinegar, or something acidic.
*Achamech* is the dross of silver <note>original: "superfluitas argenti," referring to the impurities or "excess" removed during refining</note>.
*Iron Acacia* is an iron spoon <note>original: "Cochlear ferreum"; in this context, "Acacia" likely refers to a tool shape rather than the plant</note>.
*Acazdir*, *Alkani*, and *Alomba* are Tin.
*Accatum* is Brass <note>original: "Aurichalcum," a golden-colored alloy of copper and zinc used in antiquity</note>.

**Agate** <note>original: "Achates"</note>, first discovered in *Sicily* near the river of the same name, and later in other places. They are effective against the bites of spiders; and eagles place them in their nests to defend their chicks against venomous creatures; they also quench thirst and strengthen the eyesight.
In **Agate**, various impressions appear: Forests, Rivers, Beasts of burden, and the tracks of a Wolf or Lion, all carved by nature so clearly as if a Gem-cutter had made them with great effort, according to *Pliny* <note>Pliny the Elder (23–79 AD), whose "Natural History" was a primary source for mineral lore</note>.

**Diamond** <note>original: "Adamas"</note>, originates both outside of gold and within the most noble gold.
1. Outside of gold, the *Indian* variety is twofold, related in color to translucent Crystal, with six-sided facets, tapering at the end like a spinning top, or consisting of two opposite parts joined together, about the size of a hazelnut kernel.
2. The *Arabian* variety, originating outside of gold, is smaller than the previous one.

->In the most noble gold.<-

1. The size of a millet grain, which they call *Cenchron* <note>from the Greek word for millet seed</note>.
2. The *Macedonian* variety, found in the gold of Philippi, equal to the size of a cucumber seed.
3. The *Cypriot* variety, found in Cyprus, tending toward a brassy color; it is effective in medical remedies.
4. *Sideritis* <note>meaning "iron-like"</note>, possessing an iron-like brilliance and exceeding the others in weight, but different from them in nature: this one can be broken by blows and can be perforated by another Diamond.

# CHEMICAL DICTIONARY.

[epi]lepsy, both in prevention and cure, and this through both internal and external use.
Internally, filings <note>original: "scobs"</note> and specific preparations listed below are administered.
Externally, a small piece is set in a ring and worn on the (commonly called) wedding finger, which is next to the little finger, positioned so that it faces the palm; or it is worn on that same finger in a ring specially turned for the purpose. It is likewise held in the fist and applied to the pulse; it is even placed into the (left) ear and hung from the neck so that it touches the skin. It is reported to be recognized by its scent, which provides a pleasant odor when used as incense.

*Actē*, Dwarf Elder, or Ground Elder <note>original: *Ebulus*, or *Sambucus humilis*</note>. A common and well-known plant.
Quercetanus <note>Joseph Duchesne (c. 1544–1609), a prominent French physician who blended alchemy with medicine</note> rightly and frequently praises the medicinal powder <note>original: *Tragæa*</note> made from the berries of this plant according to his own prescription, for stopping all kinds of fluxes, preferring it over many other medicines.

*Abesamum*, Wheel-mud <note>original: *Lutum rotæ*; the greasy grime found on wagon axles, often used in old remedies</note>.

*Abesum*, Quicklime <note>original: *Calx viva*</note>.

*Abicum*, A lid or cover.

*Ablutio* [Washing], is an exaltation <note>a process to improve a substance's potency</note> achieved through frequent infusions, washing away impure things and reducing them to a state of purity. It involves Imbibition <note>soaking</note> and Cohobation <note>repeated distillation of a liquid over its solid residue</note>.

*Ablatio* [Removal], is extraction through the upper part. It is performed in various ways for dry substances that adhere loosely: cleaning can be done with a hare’s foot and similar tools; some things we remove with a quill, a small knife, or spatulas; others we wipe away with a tightened cloth, or with brushes made of bristles, iron, or wood.

*Abric*, *Kibrit*, *Chybur*, are all names for Sulfur.

*Abessi*, the same as *Rebis* <note>In this context, referring to the "excrement" or final waste of digestion</note>. Specifically, the final matter of foods.

*Achahi*, is alum water.

# CHEMICAL DICTIONARY.

H 2

**Acalai** is Salt.
**Acartum** is Red Lead <note>original: *Minium*; a bright red pigment made from lead tetroxide</note>.
**Acaid** is vinegar, or something acidic.
**Achamech** is the dross of silver <note>original: *superfluitas argenti*; referring to the impurities or waste material separated from silver during refining</note>.
**Iron Acacia** <note>original: *Acacia ferrea*</note> is an iron spoon.
**Acazdir**, **Alkani**, and **Alomba** are terms for Tin <note>original: *Stannum*</note>.
**Accatum** is Brass <note>original: *Aurichalcum*; a golden-colored alloy of copper and zinc</note>.
**Agate** <note>original: *Achates*</note> was first discovered in Sicily, near the river of the same name, and later in other places. They are beneficial against spider bites: And eagles place them in their nests to defend their chicks against venomous creatures; they also quench thirst and strengthen the eyesight.
In **Agate** various impressions appear: Forests, Rivers, Beasts of burden, and the tracks of a Wolf or Lion, so clearly engraved by nature as if a Gem-cutter had created them with focused effort, according to Pliny <note>Pliny the Elder, Roman author of *Natural History*</note>.
**Diamond** <note>original: *Adamas*</note> is born both outside of gold and within the most noble gold.
1. Outside of gold, the Indian variety is twofold, related in color to translucent Crystal, hexagonal on its sides, tapering at the point like a spinning top, or like two opposite parts joined together, the size of a hazelnut kernel.
2. **Arabian**, born outside of gold, is smaller than the preceding.

->Within the most noble gold.<-

1. The size of a millet grain, which they call **Centhron**.
2. **Macedonian**, found in Philip's gold <note>gold from the mines of Philip II of Macedon</note>, equal in size to a cucumber seed.
3. **Cyprian**, found in Cyprus, leaning toward a copper color, and effective in medicinal Healings.
4. **Sideritis**, possessing an iron-like brilliance, surpassing the others in weight, but unlike them in nature: this one is broken by hammer blows, and can be pierced by another **Diamond**.

<meta>This page continues the detailed entry on the Diamond (Adamas) from the previous page, which had begun listing the different varieties of the stone. # CHEMICAL DICTIONARY

These last two types are degenerate, and possess authority in name only.

The best **Diamonds** <note>original: *Adamantes*</note> are tested on anvils; for they refuse the blow, so that the iron leaps away on both sides, and the anvils themselves fly apart; indeed, their hardness is unspeakable. The diamond is the scorner and victor of fire, never growing hot in the flame.

This stone is broken by warm, fresh goat’s blood, especially when the goat has first drunk wine, or eaten parsley and mountain seseli <note>original: *sisely*; an aromatic herb used in ancient medicine</note>.

**Diamonds** are sought after by sculptors for carving and shaping gems and other materials, and then they ought to be enclosed in iron alone.

They do not suffer other metallic substances near them, and they are dissolved by lead.

The **Diamond** <note>original: *Adamas*</note> binds the Magnet <note>original: *Magnetem*; the lodestone</note> and deprives it of its powers; for if placed near iron, it will not allow the iron to be drawn away, or if the Magnet is moved near and has grabbed the iron, the diamond snatches and carries the iron away.

The **Diamond** renders poisons harmless and drives away madness <note>original: *lymphationes*; a state of being "seized by nymphs" or driven insane</note>, ghosts, and night-demons, both incubi and succubi. It makes people spirited and brave, for which reason it is called *anachitis* <note>an ancient Greek name for the diamond when used as an amulet to ward off evil</note>. It is effective against quarrels and brawls, and it cures loose gums. *Serapion* <note>original: *Serapius*; likely referring to the 12th-century medicinal writer Serapion the Younger</note>.

**Diamonds** are seen in **Bohemia** that are so excellent that they surpass the oriental ones in elegance and splendor. Astrologers have dedicated the **Diamond** to the Moon.

**Adarnech** is orpiment <note>original: *auripigmentum*; a deep orange-yellow arsenic sulfide mineral once used as a pigment and in alchemy</note>.

**Adarces** is a substance growing in maritime places, especially in **Cappadocia** or **Galatia**: it is like a congealed saltiness, emerging in damp and marshy places, and by the process of drying, it becomes glued to reeds and herbs. Native Indian salt grows spontaneously on the reeds of **India** in the same way; or like Indian salt found in a Cane.

**Adarces** is similar in color to the "flower of the Asian stone" <note>original: *flori lapidis Asii*; a powdery efflorescence found on stones from Assos, used to treat wounds</note>, and in form it resembles the "honey of the kingfisher" <note>original: *halcyonio melli*; likely referring to a type of sea-foam or porous marine substance</note>, and is sometimes very nearly hollow.

# CHEMICAL DICTIONARY

...so that it may appear as a lake-dwelling kind of sea-foam <note>original: *halcyonium*; a spongy, calcified sea-growth often used in ancient medicine</note>, and because it hangs from reeds in this way, it was once called *pericalamitum* and *calmochium*; today, if I am not mistaken, they call it *balla marinum*.

**Dioscorides** and **Pliny** say that **Adarce** forms around thin reeds from the foam of fresh and salt water where they mix, and that it possesses a caustic <note>burning or corrosive</note> quality.

**Adarris** is the "flower" of seawater [sea foam].

**Adec** is sour milk. It is also called **Adhebe**.

**Ader** is fresh milk without butter. It is also called **Adho** or **Adoc**.

**Adehem** or **Alhohonec** is a thin plate <note>original: *Lamina*; usually a thin sheet of metal</note>.

**Adarige** is Sal Ammoniac <note>original: *Armoniacum*; a salt used in smelting and cleaning metals</note>.

**Adabisi** or **Adebezi** is a tortoise.

**Adibat** is Mercury <note>the metal quicksilver</note>.

**Ados** is water in which iron has been quenched.

**Adorat** is a weight of four pounds.

**Adram** is rock salt <note>original: *sal gemmae*; literally "gem-salt," high-purity crystalline salt</note>.

**Adraragi** is garden saffron <note>original: *Crocus hortulanus*</note>.

**Adsamar** is urine <note>original: *Lotium*; frequently used in alchemical processes for its ammonia content</note>.

**Air** <note>original: *Aër*</note> is a breeze, a vapor, a spirit, or wind.

**Aminnean vinegar** <note>original: *Acetum aminneum*</note> is white vinegar.

**Vinegar distilled from the dregs of vinegar** is of an extremely sharp and fiery nature, such that it consumes all metals, stones, and similar things just as strong water <note>original: *aqua fortis*; a term usually referring to nitric acid</note> does. It is called **Radical Vinegar** <note>original: *acetum radicatum*</note> by some; it is that most pungent liquor of vinegar which remains at the bottom once the phlegm <note>the watery, tasteless part of a distillate</note> has been drawn off. **Paracelsus, Book 7, *On the Nature of Things*, page 456.** It is made from the crystals of vinegar dregs by distilling through a retort with redistillations <note>original: *cohobiis*; the alchemical process of repeatedly pouring a distillate back over its own residue to concentrate it</note>: or, place good wine vinegar in a retort, distill the phlegm with a slow fire, and pour this back over the "dead head" <note>original: *capiti mortuo*; the solid residue remaining in the flask after distillation</note> quite often, and digest it in dung <note>placing the vessel in a pile of fermenting manure to provide a steady, gentle heat</note>; finally, distill it: and take that which comes first separately, but that which comes out later, forced by the strongest fire, is **Radical Vinegar**.

**Acordina** is Indian Tutty <note>original: *Tuthia Inda*; a zinc oxide mineral collected from the flues of smelting furnaces, used in eye salves and metallurgy</note>.

<page-num>8</page-num>

# CHEMICAL DICTIONARY

***Acortinus*** is ***Lupine*** <note>original: *Lupinus*; a type of flowering plant in the legume family whose seeds were used in ancient medicine</note>.

***Acureb*** is ***glass*** <note>original: *vitrum*</note>.

***Acusto*** is ***nitre*** <note>original: *nitrum*; likely saltpeter or potassium nitrate</note>.

***Alaurat*** is ***salt nitre***.

***Vinegar of the Philosophers*** <note>original: *Acetum Philosophorum*</note> is ***mercurial water***, which they otherwise call ***virgin’s milk*** <note>original: *lac virgineum*</note>, with which they claim metals are dissolved; it is also called the ***water of the wise*** <note>original: *hydor sophorum*</note>.

***Radical vinegar*** <note>original: *Acetum radicale*</note> is ***vinegar distilled*** from its own root or matrix <note>the fundamental source material or "mother" substance from which it originates</note>; it is also called ***solvent water***.

***Adamite*** <note>original: *Adamita*</note> is a certain species of ***Tartar*** <note>the potassium bitartrate crust found in wine barrels, frequently used in alchemical experiments</note>.

***Adech*** is our internal and invisible man, who prefigures the patterns of all individual things in our mind, which he afterwards shapes and imitates with his hands. This visible outer man of ours and that invisible one both operate according to their own nature: the invisible upon invisible things, the sensible upon those things which fall under the senses, yet both work under the same form <note>this reflects the Paracelsian idea of the "archeus" or internal creator that directs physical growth and action</note>.

***Copper (or Bronze)*** <note>original: *Aes, seu Cuprum*</note> is attributed to **Venus** by the Chemists. It is a bright metallic body, possessing a dark redness, and is both ignitable <note>reactive to fire</note> and fusible <note>able to be melted</note>.

It is, as it were, a medium between gold and silver, between the **Sun** and the **Moon**, as the Chemists say. It is made from impure **quicksilver** <note>original: *argento vivo*</note>, which is not fixed, but earthy, burning, red, and not clear; and from a similar sulfur. It lacks fixation <note>stability under heat</note>, purity, and weight. A bad and weak sulfur—like a father of an ill temperament joining with a noble mother (that is, with quicksilver of good substance)—begets copper, a son so livid and of a dark redness. We have two types of copper: 1. Pure, which is found in copper and silver mines, and which is such even without smelting. Entangled veins are sometimes discerned, and sometimes even entire thin plates <note>original: *bracteae*</note> which embrace the stone. 2. That which is smelted from pyrite, that is, **Marcasite** <note>an iron sulfide mineral often mistaken for gold or copper ore</note>; likewise from other species, such as the fissile stone <note>shale or slate</note> of **Eisleben**; and from several types of yellow and purple earths, such as **Chrysocolla** <note>a green/blue copper carbonate mineral</note> and blue-stone, just like gold.

<meta>catchword: Copper (Aes),</meta>

<summary>This page defines various alchemical terms, most notably "Adech" (the internal invisible human archetype) and "Aes" (Copper/Bronze), explaining the latter's planetary association with Venus and its theoretical composition from sulfur and mercury.</summary>

<keywords>Acortinus, Acureb, Nitre, Vinegar of the Philosophers, Radical Vinegar, Adamite, Adech, Copper, Venus, Quicksilver, Marcasite, Chrysocolla</keywords>

The entries shift from chemical substances to the nature of minerals and volcanic phenomena, blending physical observation with moralizing anecdotes about ancient philosophers.</meta>

# CHEMICAL DICTIONARY
<page-num>7</page-num>

->**B 2**<-

***Aes*** <note>original: *Æs*; usually meaning copper or bronze, but used here as a general term for pure native metal</note> does not always denote copper, but sometimes **gold** or **silver** [produced] by nature without the admixture of another metal or stone—a pure metal generated by itself, from which money was once coined, just as it is now [coined] from others separated by human art. It was first born in **Galilee**, but in small quantities. In our times, something similar has been seen, but rarely, and it happens to be found only as a miracle.

***Aethna*** <note>Mt. Etna or volcanic fire</note> is said to be a subterranean fire, invisible and sulfurous, which burns stones beneath mountains into coals very similar to ***jet*** <note>original: *gagates*; a dark, organic, coal-like gemstone</note>; they are filled in some way with resin and bitumen, so that some nations use them in place of charcoal from wood, and especially blacksmiths. In some places, it reveals itself more at one time than another, as in the Kingdom of **Sicily**, on that mountain which has this very name; likewise in the Kingdom of **Naples** at ***Pozzuoli*** <note>original: *Puteolis*; a volcanic coastal town near Naples</note>, not far from Naples. Once, the ancients wondered at the cause of this kind of burning, and being unable to explain it after much investigation, they finally reached such a point of desperation that among the most famous philosophers, physicists, and physicians, ***Empedocles*** <note>a Greek philosopher who, according to legend, leaped into the crater of Mt. Etna</note> alone threw himself to be devoured into the midst of the flames of **Etna**, overcome by shame rather than ignorance. Likewise, ***Gaius Plinius*** <note>Pliny the Elder, who died while investigating the eruption of Vesuvius</note> allowed himself to be suffocated by the smoke of that same fire. O the insane characters of men, who while they wish to be ignorant of nothing, find they have sustained many labors to know nothing, except those things which bring about a harmful death, thinking it better not to live than not to know, even though they will not be ignorant that these things are transitory. Indeed, when the cooking is finished, simple women know how to shut up burning coals in a pot, so that the access of air is not open through even the smallest crack; in this way, the fire is completely extinguished and suffocated, which lives only through and within the air itself. If, however, before all is extinguished, so that even a spark remains, a vent is made, it revives and returns to its former state. Not differently regarding...

<meta>catchword: æthneo</meta>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of "Aethna" (subterranean or volcanic fire) from the previous page. ### CHEMICAL DICTIONARY
<page-num>8</page-num>

...must be judged as **Etnean fire** <note>original: *æthneo*; referring to the fire of Mt. Etna or volcanic fire</note>, which takes its origin in the center of the world, while the earth contains it like a cooking pot. Yet it does not lack its vents <note>original: *spiraculis*; breathing holes or volcanic fumaroles</note> gaping around the globe of the earth, through which—now through this one, now through another—it draws life and nourishment from the air. Indeed, if it were to lack its breathing holes, it would necessarily happen just as it does with a woman’s cooking fire <note>referring to a fire being smothered or a pot exploding if not vented</note>. See, I pray, what old wives' tales moved the faithless Philosophers and tormented them even unto death <note>A likely reference to the philosopher Empedocles, who allegedly leapt into Mt. Etna to prove his divinity</note>. You will find a broader account of the nature of this natural fire where I have interpreted **Paracelsus** <note>The famous Swiss-German physician and alchemist (1493–1541)</note> regarding the *Great Uncreated Mystery* <note>original: *mysterio magno increato*; a central Paracelsian concept regarding the primordial matter of the universe</note> in his book titled *On the Light of Nature*. Thus far has been said of **Aethna**, the mineral fire and "general father," as much as the nature of this space allows.

***Ethnici*** <note>from the Greek 'ethnikos,' but here referring to 'heathen' or 'fiery' spirits</note> are said to be fiery spirits, or spiritual men fragrant with fire, who appear in various modes and forms, such as fiery flames, firebrands, globes, and spherical burning coals, and this occurs in sulfurous mountains.

***Alkali*** <note>original: *Alcali*</note> is the name given to every salt that is extracted from the ashes or lime of any material by boiling in lye; it is common to all things, whether liquid or solid.

***Copper*** <note>original: *Æs*; while it often means copper or bronze, in this context it refers more broadly to metallic ores</note>, when it is in this state:
1. It is in its vein in the mines—namely, where there is a great deal of **pyrite**, which is **marcasite** <note>In early mineralogy, pyrite and marcasite were often used interchangeably for various sulfide minerals</note>; it causes those native species of vitriol <note>original: *vitrioli*; sulfate minerals</note> known as *Colchitis*, *misy*, *sory*, and *diphryges* <note>These are classical Greek terms for different forms of weathered copper/iron sulfates</note>.

2. It is smelted, and then it becomes: 
    1. Artificial **cadmia** <note>zinc-rich furnace deposits</note> with its various types. 
    2. **Pompholix** <note>"Bird’s-nest" or white zinc oxide soot</note>. 
    3. **Spodium** <note>A heavier metallic residue or soot</note> is burnt, and becomes **burnt copper** <note>original: *æs ustum*</note>. 
    4. It is quenched and becomes **scale of copper** <note>original: *squama æris*</note>. 
    5. It is hammered out and becomes **scale of copper**. 
    6. Finally, on the surface of the smelted metal, **scraped verdigris** <note>original: *ærugo rasilis*</note> is formed.

***Copper*** is of two kinds:
1. **Pure**, which is found in copper and silver mines; this is found as such without smelting. Sometimes tangled veins are discerned, and sometimes entire plates <note>original: *bracteæ*</note> which embrace the stone.
2. That which is **smelted** from pyrite, that is, marcasite; likewise from other species, such as from that fissile stone of **Eisleben** <note>original: *Islebiæ*; referring to the famous copper-shale (Kupferschiefer) deposits in Eisleben, Germany</note>, and furthermore from...

### CHEMICAL DICTIONARY
<page-num>9</page-num>

...with some types of earth, yellow and purple: **Chrysocolla** <note>literally "gold-glue"; in this period, it referred to green copper minerals like malachite or substances like borax used in soldering</note>, and even blue <note>original: *æruleo*; cerulean or azure</note> just like gold.

**Burnt Copper** <note>original: *Æs ustum*; copper oxide produced by heating the metal</note> in our time is made from the copper itself being burnt with sulfur and salt. That from Memphis <note>in Egypt</note> and Cyprus is most highly praised by **Dioscorides** <note>A famous Greek physician and author of *De Materia Medica*</note>. It binds, dries, restrains, thins out, draws, and cleanses ulcers: and whenever it is applied to ulcers with vinegar, it consumes those things which are an impediment <note>likely referring to necrotic tissue or "proud flesh"</note> in the ulcers.

**Burnt copper** is also the **Saffron of Venus** <note>original: *Crocus Veneris*; a refined copper oxide named after Venus, the planetary and alchemical symbol for copper</note>. *Paracelsus, Book 5 of "On the Nature of Things," page 420.*

**The Copper of Hermes** <note>original: *Æs Hermetis*; a symbolic term in Hermetic philosophy</note> is, truly and properly, the imperfect body not yet prepared, just as it is at the beginning: It is Mercury, the powder of the sun, the raven's head <note>original: *caput corvi*; a symbol for the *nigredo* or blackening stage of the alchemical process</note>, our copper, yellowed earth, the container and the contained, our leaden substance, gold extracted from metals, **Venus**, **Vitriol** <note>sulfate minerals</note>, **Orpiment** <note>arsenic trisulfide</note>, **Arsenic**, the Coin, the Soul, the **Green Lion**, green water—because it sprouts—permanent water, wine, blood, etc.

26 species of Copper <note>original: *Æris* or *Cupri*</note> are enumerated by **Ruland** <note>Martin Ruland the Elder (1532–1602), author of a famous alchemical lexicon</note> on pages 10 and 11, if you desire to know more; and he distinguishes **verdigris** <note>original: *æruginem*; the green pigment or crust formed on copper</note> into 65 species from page 15 to page 20.

<margin>**Verdigris**, or the green of copper, is:</margin>
<image-desc>A logical branching diagram categorizing types of Verdigris (Ærugo). Brackets connect "Scrapable" and "Cleavable" to the main heading, which then branch further into natural and artificial varieties.</image-desc>

1. **Scrapable** <note>original: *Rasile*; verdigris that can be scraped off the metal</note> is:
    1. **Natural**: found in Cyprian metals, or in stones containing some copper, upon which the verdigris effloresces <note>blooms on the surface</note>: these are few but of the best quality, and are found in copper mines.
    2. **Artificial**: produced on the surface of copper, and is of three types:
        1. **Scraped**, because it is shaved off.
        2. **Worm-like** <note>original: *Vermiculare*</note> from *scolecia* <note>original: *scolierme*, likely a corruption of the Greek *skolekia*, meaning "worm-shaped"</note>, which grows into a worm-like form.
        3. **Goldsmiths'**, which is an artificial **Chrysocolla**.

2. **Cleavable** <note>original: *Scissile*; verdigris that can be split or flaked</note>:
    1. **Natural**: which is true **Chrysocolla**, that is, the "glue of gold."
    2. **Artificial**: the goldsmiths' variety, green, scraped, or imitation **Chrysocolla**.

<meta>This page continues the "Lexicon Chymicum" (Chemical Dictionary) from page 9. It picks up after the discussion of "Copper of Hermes" and the stages of the alchemical process, transitioning into mineralogy and a list of alchemical synonyms beginning with the letter 'A'.</meta>

### CHEMICAL DICTIONARY
<page-num>10</page-num>

**Eagle-stone** <note>original: *Ætites*; from the Greek *aetos*, meaning eagle</note>, so called by the Germans either because of its color or because of the power it is said to hold, that eagles cannot give birth without it: for it alleviates labor and prevents miscarriage in pregnant women when the womb is slippery <note>likely a reference to a "weak" or "relaxed" cervix in modern medical terms</note>; it is a gemstone of various types.

**Eagle-stones** come in six species.

1. The male **Eagle-stone**, from Africa, reddish-black in color, very hard, which contains within itself a white, crumbly earth.

2. The female, from Hildesheim <note>a city in Germany known for its mineral deposits</note>, yellow-colored and soft, to which natural ochre adheres, and which contains a hard yellow earth.

3. Another in which a stone lies free and loose, and when shaken, it rattles <note>these are geode-like stones with a smaller pebble or sand trapped inside</note>.

4. A reddish-hard stone, to which a stone of iron-like color adheres.

5. The **Motteschan** stone <note>likely referring to specimens from the Mottesch region described by the physician Martin Ruland</note>, similar to a human head, round and very hard, to which are attached crystalline flowers in the shape of four-angled diamonds.

6. A hollow stone of iron-like color, found in a vein of iron, in which nothing but air is contained: whoever desires to know more, let him read **Ruland** <note>Martin Ruland the Elder, author of the 1612 *Lexicon Alchemiae*</note>, pages 21 and 22.

**Flower of Nitre** <note>original: *Affronitrum*; a "foam" or efflorescence of saltpeter</note> is the froth of nitre, which is salty, or it is **Cappadocian salt** <note>a high-quality rock salt from central Turkey</note>.

**Affeos & Afros** is foam <note>from the Greek *aphros*</note>.

**Affroton** is foamy.

**Affrodina** is **Venus** <note>The goddess Aphrodite, used here to represent the metal copper or the planet Venus</note>.

**Affenicum** is the **soul** <note>original: *anima*; in alchemy, the "soul" of a substance is its volatile essence or "sulfur"</note>.

**Afformas** is glass.

**Affax & Affaris** is **ink** <note>original: *atramentum*; used broadly for dark pigments or vitriol</note>.

**Affrengi** is **Red Lead** <note>original: *Minium*; lead tetroxide, a bright orange-red pigment</note>.

**Affidra & Almat** is **White Lead** <note>original: *Cerusa*; lead carbonate, the most common white pigment in history</note>.

**Afrob** is our **lead** <note>a term for the alchemical "Lead of the Wise," which is often not common lead but a starting material for the Great Work</note>.

**Alahabar, Alchonor, Alastrob, Alomba, Agar, Algit, Algerit** is **Quicklime** <note>original: *Calx*; calcium oxide, produced by burning limestone</note>.

**Ahusal** is **arsenic sulfur**. **Akibot, Alchibit, Alchimit** is **Sulfur**.

**Alafor** is **alkali salt**.

<meta>This page continues the "Lexicon Chymicum" (Chemical Dictionary). It transitions from the previous page's discussion on the "Flower of Nitre" into alphabetical entries for various alchemical substances, tools, and minerals, specifically focusing on the letter 'A'.</meta>

### CHEMICAL DICTIONARY
<page-num>11</page-num>

**Alatan** is the litharge of lead <note>litharge is a natural mineral form of lead(II) oxide, often a byproduct of separating silver from lead</note>.

**Ahenum** <note>literally "brazen" or "made of copper"</note> is a metallic vessel made of copper or iron, forged to a height of two feet and nearly the same width. A cover, shaped in the following manner and properly sealing its edges, is to be placed on its top. A round copper plate is made to the capacity of the cauldron, in the center of which—since it must serve the purpose of a gourd-shaped flask <note>original: *cucurbitæ*; a rounded alchemical vessel</note>—a hole is excavated to the size of the vessel to be inserted, so that the vessel may protrude through it. On one side or the other, near the edge of the cauldron, another small hole is bored, through which hot water is poured and the loss of evaporated water is replenished. The use of this cauldron with its lid is very frequent in water baths <note>original: *balneis*; the "bain-marie" used for gentle, indirect heating</note>.

**Akon** <note>from the Greek *akonē*</note>, or **Cos**, is a stone with which knives and other instruments are sharpened; a whetstone. There are various species of it, in colors of black, white, yellow, and deepest black. A species of whetstone is what they call the **Heraclean** and **Lydian** stone, which is known to goldsmiths and is called a touchstone <note>original: *coticula*; used to test the purity of precious metals by the color of the streak they leave behind</note>.

**Alabastrites** <note>Alabaster</note> is a stone from the species of marble well known in Venice.

There are three species of it:

The first is white, from which the statues of Saints and the monuments of the buried were formerly made.

The second has black spots.

The third is sometimes white amidst reddish tones, and for that reason is called **Onyx** <note>from the Greek word for "fingernail"</note>, because it represents the color of a human fingernail; and **Dioscorides** <note>a famous 1st-century Greek physician and botanist</note> specifically means this species when he says: **Alabaster**, which is also called **Onyx**, when burnt, disperses hardness <note>referring to hard tumors or swellings</note> if used with pitch or resin.

**Alafalet** is Sal Ammoniac <note>original: *Armoniacum*; ammonium chloride, used as a flux in metallurgy</note>.

**Alartar** is burnt copper <note>original: *æs ustum*; copper that has been heated with sulfur to create an oxide or sulfide</note>.

**Albanum** is the salt of urine.

**Albor** is urine.

**Alborca** is Mercury.

<page-num>12</page-num>

*Albaras* is arsenic.

*Alberick* is the "white of copper" <note>likely referring to a white copper alloy or copper whitened through the addition of arsenic</note>.

*Albetud* is Galbanum <note>a bitter, aromatic gum resin derived from certain Persian plant species, used in medicine and incense</note>.

*Albi* means sublimates <note>original: *sublimati*; substances that have been heated into a vapor and then solidified again for purification</note>.

*Albir* is pitch from the bark of the yew tree; it is supposed to be the substance from which ink <note>original: *incaustum*</note> is made.

*Albot* is a crucible <note>original: *crucibulus*; a ceramic or metal container used for melting materials at very high temperatures</note>.

*Albotar* or *alfidas* is Ceruse <note>white lead, historically used as a pigment and in medicinal plasters</note>.

*Albotim*, or *albotai*, or *Albotra Helcalibat*, is Turpentine.

*Alcady* is white ink <note>likely white vitriol or a specific mineral sulfate</note>.

*Alzegi* is Ink <note>or vitriol; a term often used for iron or copper sulfates used in making black pigments</note>.

*Alcaol* is soured milk, or Mercury.

*Alcanna* or *Alcona* are slender, long trees having nodes like a reed <note>original: *Canna*</note>, which the Arabs use in place of spears. It is taken to mean Guaiac wood <note>a hard, resinous wood from the Americas, famously used in the 16th century to treat syphilis</note>.

*Alcebris vivum* <note>literally "living alcebris"</note> is Sulfur.

*Alchitram* is oil of Juniper, liquid pitch, or arsenic prepared for washing.

*Alchitran* is the dregs of distillation. Also oil of Juniper.

*Alcolismus* is the reduction to powder <note>comminution</note> or corrosion.

*Alcol* is Vinegar.

*Alcohol* is Antimony, or stibnite <note>original: *stibium*; in this period, the word "alcohol" referred to an extremely fine powder, such as the kohl used for eye makeup, rather than a liquid spirit</note>.

*Alcone* is Brass <note>original: *Aurichalcum*; literally "gold-copper," a yellow alloy of copper and zinc</note>.

*Alcer* is burnt copper <note>original: *æs ustum*</note> in grain-like form. Also *Alkalid*, *Alcoh*, and *Altimar*.

*Alcore* is a certain stone having leaves similar to silver <note>likely a reference to mica or a similar foliated, shiny mineral</note>.

*Alcubd* or *Alumbair* is raw butter.

*Alech* is vitriol <note>a metal sulfate, such as copper or iron sulfate</note>.

*Alecharith* is Mercury.

*Alechil* is a tripod <note>original: *tripes*; a three-legged stand used to support vessels over a fire</note>.

*Alectoria* is a gemstone similar to crystal or clear water. It displays a crystalline appearance and is the size of a bean, found in the gizzards of roosters <note>original: *ventriculis gallinaceorum*; also known as a "cock-stone," it was historically believed to have magical or medicinal properties</note>, as [Albertus says...]

### CHEMICAL DICTIONARY
<page-num>13</page-num>

**Alectorius** <note>original: *Alectorius*; a mythical stone said to be found in the gizzards of roosters</note> is taken from a castrated cock; it is found after its fourth year. It is said to make those who carry it wealthy and courageous. For this reason, it is reported that Milo of Croton was invincible in his contests. It wins favor with girls and quenches thirst.

*Alefantes* is the "flower of the Sun" <note>likely referring to the sunflower or a solar-aligned mineral</note>.

*Alembic* is Mercury <note>while usually meaning a still-head, here it is used as a synonym for the metal itself</note>.

The **Liquor Alkahest** <note>original: *Altahest*; the legendary "universal solvent" sought by alchemists</note> of Paracelsus resolves every visible body into its own "primary matter," while the power of its seeds is preserved. Concerning this, the Chemists say: the common people burn by fire, but we burn by water. **Van Helmont** <note>Jan Baptista van Helmont, a 17th-century chemist who claimed to have discovered the Alkahest</note>.

The **Liquor Alkahest** is of great virtue for preserving the liver from dropsy <note>original: *hydrops*; an accumulation of fluid in the body</note> and all its varieties that originate from the liver. See the preparation in *Book 2 of The Nature of Things, chapter 3, page 482*. Precipitated Mercury for the liver.

**Alcohol of wine** <note>original: *Alcohol vini*; refers here to highly rectified spirit or ethanol</note> is when all the superfluity of the wine is separated from the wine, so that when ignited it burns until it is entirely consumed, and nothing of the dregs or phlegm <note>the watery residue of distillation</note> remains at the bottom.

**Alcohol of Paracelsus**: the *Alcohol* of any things or bodies whatsoever is nothing other than the purer and cleaner substance separated from the impure. For example, **Alcohol of Antimony** for Paracelsus means nothing other than this: the Author intends that the Antimony should not only be ground with a pestle and mortar, but also turned into its volatile substance, unchanged from its natural color. And it is necessary that this be done solely by the benefit and governance of fire and heat, so that after it has been ground in the common way, it is finally "altered philosophically" by sublimations, which are the "pestles and mortars of the Philosophers" <note>meaning that heat and vapor perform a finer purification than any physical tool</note>. He teaches in another place that this sublimation ought to be done without any *caput mortuum* <note>literally "dead head"; the worthless residue left in a flask after distillation or sublimation</note>. Thus it is established that no dregs were present before the sublimation; those who work in the common way cannot elicit the yellow or red "flowers" <note>powdery sublimates</note>.

<page-num>14</page-num>

...by grinding they sublimate antimony into a white soot. But this kind of sublimation must be done with a very moderate fire, so that the powder of the antimony does not melt at all, yet still rises, just as the fine flour is seen adhering to the walls of mills.

**Alembics** <note>original: *Alembicus*; a specialized cap or "head" used in distillation</note> is the small cap which is placed upon the lower vessels to receive vapors.
1. A **Beaked Alembic** <note>original: *Alembicus rostratus*</note> is one which sends the resolved liquids through a channel or "beak" to the receiving vessel.
2. Another is the **Blind Alembic** <note>original: *Cœcus*</note>, that is, one without a beak, serving for sublimations <note>the process where a solid turns into vapor and then back to solid</note>. In sublimations, this is sometimes perforated slightly at the peak to release rising vapors.

**Alkahest** <note>original: *Alcahest* or *althahest*</note>, or *althahest*, is said to be prepared **Mercury**; some maintain it is tartar, yet the intent of the author is easily grasped from the description of its preparation.

**Alchemy** <note>original: *Alchimia*</note> is the separation of the impure from the purer substance.

**Alcohol** <note>original: *Alcol*, *alcool*, or *alcohol*</note>, is a powder made into the finest dust; when nothing is added to the word alone, it refers to this. Otherwise, the meaning is restricted by an accompanying term.

<note>Error: see the other page.</note>

**Alcohol of wine** is rectified "burning water" <note>original: *aqua ardens*; a term for high-proof spirits or ethanol</note>.

**Alcubrith** <note>original: *Alcubrith*, *alazer*, or *alcur*</note>, or *alazer*, or *alcur*, is the same as **Sulfur**.

**Alkibrie** <note>original: *Alkibrie*, *alchabrick*, or *algibich*</note>, or *alchabrick*, or *algibich*, is living sulfur <note>original: *sulphur vivum*; naturally occurring sulfur that has not been treated by fire</note>.

**Alcosol** (or as some prefer, *alcosol*) is stibium or **Antimony**; likewise, *alcimad* is stibium.

**Alandahal** is colocynth <note>original: *colocinthis*; a bitter Mediterranean plant used as a powerful purgative</note>.

**Alembroth** is the salt of mercury, or the **Salt of the Philosophers** and of the art; the key of the art.

**Purified Alembroth** <note>original: *defecatum*</note> is salt of tartar and the "magistery of masters" <note>a "magistery" refers to a substance prepared to its highest state of purity and power</note>.

**Almizadir** is verdigris <note>original: *viride æris*; the green pigment or crust that forms on copper, brass, or bronze</note>.

**Alteyplumbi** is a sweet material derived from lead. Also called *Alkyplumbi* <note>likely referring to "sugar of lead" or lead acetate</note>.

<meta>This page continues the "Lexicon Chymicum" (Chemical Dictionary) from page 14, following the alphabetical sequence of terms beginning with "A." It moves from apparatus descriptions into a glossary of substances, minerals, and Paracelsian spiritual-chemical terms.</meta>

->### CHEMICAL DICTIONARY<-
<page-num>15</page-num>

**Alufar** is Manna <note>original: *Alufar, est Manna*; manna was a sweet, resinous secretion from trees used as a mild laxative</note>.

**Amalgam** <note>original: *Amalgama*</note> is a paste made of gold, silver, or any other metal mixed with quicksilver <note>mercury</note>. **Amalgamation**, see page 121.

**Amianthus** <note>original: *Amianthus*; a form of asbestos</note> is a stone similar in nature and condition to split or feathery alum; it is not consumed by fire, and for this reason, it is also called the **salamander** <note>after the mythical lizard believed to be fireproof</note>.

**Starch** <note>original: *Amidum* or *amilum*</note> is the whitest flour, hardened into loaves in the sun.

**Tonsils** <note>original: *Amigdalæ*; literally "almonds"</note> are what surgeons call the superfluous flesh that grows at the base of the tongue.

**Alkalized stream** <note>original: *Amnis alcalisatus*</note> is water passing through the "limes" or ashes of the earth from which alkali has been extracted.

**Andena** is a type of steel <note>original: *calybs*</note> imported from Eastern regions, which melts in fire just like any other metal and allows itself to be cast into molds.

**Anathron** or *anachthron* is a type of salt that grows on rocks like white, stony moss <note>original: *usneæ*</note>; some call it saltpeter <note>nitre</note>. The ancients mistakenly thought it was the "gall of glass" <note>impurities that rise to the top of molten glass</note>, whereas it is more accurately the "gall of stones."

**Anatrum** is glass melted into various colors, which is commonly called **smalt** <note>a blue pigment made from cobalt glass</note> or Saracen earth.

**Anthos** <note>Greek for "flower"</note>, beyond its botanical meaning referring to rosemary, is used in reference to metals to mean an **elixir** made from gold, or its **quintessence** <note>the most purified essence or "fifth element" of a substance</note>.

**Anatomia essata** <note>a Paracelsian term referring to the inherent "internal anatomy" or essential blueprint of a person</note> is the parent of diseases.

**Anticar** is **Borax**.

**Anatris** or *antaris* is **Mercury**.

**Aniaday** signifies an eternal springtime, the new world to come, and paradise.

**Aniadum** is the **Celestial body** which is planted within us Christians by the Holy Spirit through the Holy Sacraments; or, it is the spiritual man reborn within us.

<meta>catchword: Aniadus,</meta>

<summary>This page defines various chemical and mineral terms, transitioning from physical substances like steel (Andena) and asbestos (Amianthus) to Paracelsian metaphysical concepts regarding the spiritual body (Aniadum).</summary>

<meta>This page continues the alphabetical sequence of the "Lexicon Chymicum" (Chemical Dictionary), moving from the terms "Alufar" and "Anatrum" on page 15 into further definitions under the letter "A." ->### CHEMICAL DICTIONARY<-
<page-num>16</page-num>

**Aniadus** is the efficacy or vital power of things <note>original: *rerum efficacia*; in Paracelsian philosophy, this refers to the internal spiritual strength or "virtue" of a substance</note>.

**Aniada** are the fruits and powers of Paradise and Heaven; they are also the Sacraments of Christians. In the physical realm, however, they signify astral and celestial powers—such as those which, through influence, imagination, estimation, and fantasy, lead us toward a long life and, by the gift of God, urge us toward eternity.

**Anodynes** <note>original: *Anodina*</note> are medicines that induce sleep <note>pain-relievers or sedatives</note>.

**Anodus** is that which is separated from nourishment by the kidneys.

**Anontagius** is the Philosopher's Stone.

**Soul of Saturn** <note>original: *Anima saturni*</note> is **Altey**, the sweetest essence of lead, which is extracted by means of vinegar <note>lead acetate, known for its sweet taste, though it is highly toxic</note>.

**Philosophical Year** <note>original: *Annus Philosophicus*</note> is a common month <note>alchemical texts often used coded timeframes; a "year" in a recipe might only mean four weeks</note>.

**Anotaster**, **Aliocab**, **Alemzadar**, and **Amizader** are terms for **Sal ammoniac** <note>ammonium chloride, used as a flux in metallurgy</note>.

**Alfol**, **Alcob**, **Azonec**, **Alacap**, **Aracab**, and **Aquila** <note>original: *Aquila*; literally "The Eagle," a common alchemical name for sal ammoniac or mercury sublimate</note>.

**Ales** is a compound salt.

**Alexir** is a medicine prepared by chemical means. Also known as **Elixir** or **Elci**.

**Alezaram** is a wash or lotion of lead.

**Agallium** is thorny columbine <note>original: *Aquilegium spinosum*</note>, also called "rest-ox" <note>original: *resta bovis*</note> or "plow-stopper" <note>original: *aratri remora*; likely referring to the plant Restharrow, which has tough roots that could snag a plow</note>.

**Agresten** signifies a drink made from apples or barberries, cider, or an artificial wine made from apples.

**Alexanthi** or **Altingat** is the "flower of copper" <note>original: *flos æris*; verdigris or copper acetate</note>.

**Alfacta** is distillation.

**Alfadidam** is iron dross <note>original: *scoria ferri*; the waste material left after smelting iron</note>.

**Alfatida** is burnt copper, or the filings of Venus <note>original: *Laminatura Veneris*; "Venus" is the alchemical name for copper</note>.

**Alfusa** is Tutty <note>original: *Tutia*; zinc oxide that collects as a crust in the chimneys of smelting furnaces</note>.

**Algali** is nitre <note>saltpeter</note>.

**Algamet** means coals.

**Algeria** or **Algerie** is lime <note>original: *Calx*</note>.

<meta>This page continues the alphabetical "Lexicon Chymicum," picking up from the end of the entry for "Alexir" on page 16. <page-num>17</page-num>

*Aligulus* is a confection <note>a medicinal preparation, usually a paste or pill made by mixing drugs with honey or sugar</note>.

*Alcafiel* is antimony.

*Alkalia* is a vessel.

*Alkale* is the oil of a hen.

*Alkali* is fossil or fusible vitriol <note>a sulfate mineral, usually of iron or copper</note> burnt <note>original: *calcitura*</note> in an *Aludal*.

*Aludel* and *Alutel* is a glass vessel used for sublimation <note>the process where a solid is heated into a vapor and then collected as a solid again on a cooler surface</note>.

*Alkali* is, according to others, a small stone of salt, extracted from ashes that have been reverberated <note>heated in a furnace where the flame is deflected downward onto the material</note> by means of moisture, and then coagulated once the moisture has been dissipated. But it is specifically called *Alkali* when the essence of the form of this little stone is worked out, after the impure body has been left behind and separated. However, when whole things are calcined and dissolved and then reduced back to a solid consistency—such as when common salt is dissolved by moisture and coagulated again, or when pearls are entirely calcined, dissolved, and coagulated in turn—these are "magisteries" <note>preparations where the whole substance is kept, rather than just an extract</note> by themselves, though they are called *Alkali* due to their similarity.

*Alkautum* is burnt copper <note>original: *æs ustum*</note>, or an aromatic species; according to some, it is arsenic.

*Alkaut* and *Almarkasita* is mercury.

*Alkaranum* is green *Duenec*. *Duenege* is vitriol.

*Alcara* is a cucurbit <note>a gourd-shaped glass vessel used in distillation</note>.

*Alkin* and *Alkal* is potash <note>original: *cinis clavellatus*; literally "clubbed ashes," produced by burning hardwood</note>.

*Alkir* is smoke or coals.

*Alkitram* is liquid pitch.

*Alkoel* is lead refined from ore, Lapis Lazuli, or antimony.

*Alcol* is Tartar <note>the potassium bitartrate deposit found in wine barrels</note>. Theophrastus <note>a reference to Paracelsus, whose full name was Philippus Aureolus Theophrastus Bombastus von Hohenheim</note> says this: *Alcol* is the purer substance of a thing separated from its impurity. Thus, *Alcol of wine* <note>original: *Alcol vini*</note> is rectified "burning water" <note>original: *aqua ardens*; a term for concentrated alcohol or brandy</note>.

*Alkofor* is camphor.

*Alkimia* is the powder of a basilisk <note>in alchemical lore, "basilisk powder" was sometimes used as a code for a potent catalyst or a specific red tincture</note>. *Alchimilla* is Sanicle <note>a common healing herb, also known as "Lady's Mantle"</note>.

<page-num>19</page-num>

*Alsech* is Yemeni Alum <note>original: *Alumen Jameni*; a high-quality alum sourced from Yemen</note>.

*Alum* <note>original: *Alumen*</note> is known to everyone. There are various species of it. The best is Gem Alum. It is also called Mercury because it dissolves things. There is Yemeni, Rock, Split <note>schistose</note>, Round, Sugar-alum, Alum of Bergamo, Genoese nitre, Fossil, Fusible, Plumose <note>feather-like, often referring to asbestos</note>, Liquid, Capillary <note>hair-like</note>, Common, Plate-like <note>original: *Placodes*</note>, Burnt, Liparian, etc. You may read more in **Agricola** and **Kentmann** <note>Georgius Agricola and Johannes Kentmann, famous 16th-century mineralogists</note> regarding metallic and fossil matters. *Albertus* <note>Albertus Magnus, 13th-century philosopher</note> posits four types of alums in his book *On Minerals*: 1. Simple common alum. 2. Black. 3. White. 4. Written <note>original: *Perscriptum*; possibly a transcription error for *Persicum* or Persian alum</note>. *Ruland* <note>Martin Ruland the Elder, author of a famous alchemical lexicon</note> counts different types of alums on pages 33 to 36, numbering over 32; see more in *Ruland*, pages 72 to 74.

*Alum of Sugar* <note>original: *Alumen Sacchari*</note>: how it is made can be found in **Paracelsus**, *The Nature of Things*, Book 7, page 451.

The various preparations of different *Alums* are found in *On Preparations*, Book 4, pages 656 and 657, for putrid ulcers, paralysis, flux of the bowels <note>diarrhea</note>, etc.

*Raw Ales* <note>original: *Ales crudum*</note> are little droplets often falling at night in the month of June <note>likely referring to "May Dew," which alchemists believed contained a celestial spirit</note>.

*Alunibur* is the Moon <note>the alchemical name for Silver</note>.

*Alunsel* is a Drop <note>original: *Stilla*</note>.

*Alusen* is a Sulphurated substance.

*Alzemafor* is Cinnabar <note>mercury sulfide</note>.

*Alzilat* is a weight of three grains.

*Amalgamation* is the calcination <note>the reduction of a substance to powder by heat or chemical means</note> of common metals by means of mercury <note>original: *hydrargyrum*</note>. Although we do not always strive for the end result of a lime <note>original: *calcis*; a fine powder</note>, in that way it is sufficient for the metal to be dissolved so that it can be worked like a paste, or the *malagma* <note>a soft, pliable mass</note> of goldsmiths. Nevertheless, corrosion occurs through this process, turning the metal into a very fine powder, which they likewise call a "lime."

Or, *Amalgamation* is the Calcination of a Metal through quicksilver; it occurs when the metal is drawn into thin parts, leaves, or plates, and combined with six, nine, or

<meta>The catchword "duodecim" (twelve) indicates the sentence continues on the next page, likely specifying the ratio of mercury to metal.</meta>

...is mixed and joined with twelve parts of quicksilver <note>original: *argenti vivi*; mercury</note>, so that a mass is formed that is uniform throughout; in this way, the metal is broken down and calcined <note>reduced to a powder through heating</note>. For once the quicksilver is washed away by evaporation over a slow fire, the metal is left behind in the form of a fine powder <note>original: *calcis*</note>.

*Amandivus Stone* <note>original: *Amandivus Lapis*</note> is a gemstone of various colors. It extinguishes and represses all poisons, which is why it is not a gem to be looked down upon; consult **Albertus** <note>Albertus Magnus, a 13th-century philosopher and theologian known for his writings on minerals</note>.

*Amber* <note>original: *Ambra*</note> is the "Sperm of the Whale" <note>original: *Sperma Ceti*; historically, ambergris (found in the digestive system of sperm whales) was often confused with the waxy substance found in the whale's head</note>.

*Amene* is common salt.

*Amentum* is split alum <note>original: *alumen scissum*; likely schistose or layered alum</note>.

*Amethyst* is an Indian gemstone of a violet color. **Pliny** <note>Pliny the Elder, Roman author of *Natural History*</note> says the best ones come from there. **Ruland** <note>Martin Ruland the Elder, author of an influential alchemical lexicon</note>, on page 37, counts six species that are of less value than the oriental ones.

*Amianthus Stone* <note>original: *Amianthus lapis*</note> is found in Cyprus. **Dioscorides** <note>A 1st-century Greek physician and botanist</note> describes it as similar to split alum; but *Amianthus* is undoubtedly our "feathered alum" <note>original: *alumen plumosum*; a form of asbestos</note>, and from this an "eternal candle" <note>A wick that does not burn away</note> can be made. It does not have great medicinal power, but it is drying; refer to **page 120**.

*Asbestos* is the color of iron and is found in the mountains of Arcadia; once it is set on fire, it is hardly ever—if ever—extinguished, as it is oily and moist.

*Amogabriel* is Cinnabar <note>mercury sulfide</note>.

*Anfir-son* <note>original: *Anfir-filius*</note> is Mercury.
*Anfirarto-spirit* <note>original: *Anfirarto-spiritus*</note> is Salt.
*Anaton*, that is *Amatron*, is the salt of nitre.
*Anatron* is the dross of glass.
*Anatron* is *Bauraç* <note>Borax or a similar alkaline salt</note>.
*Anatron* is the grease of glass.
*Anatron* is the foam of glass.

<meta>This page continues the "Chemical Dictionary," proceeding through terms starting with "An-" and "Ap-." It follows the previous page's discussion on minerals and stones, moving into medicinal inflammations, specific alchemical substances like Antimony, and the properties of Nitre.</meta>

->## CHEMICAL DICTIONARY<-
<page-num>21</page-num>

*Saint Anthony’s Fire* <note>original: *Antonii sancti ignis*; historically a term for ergotism or erysipelas, skin conditions that cause a painful, burning sensation</note> is a natural inflammation.

*Annora* is the lime <note>original: *calx*; a powdery residue left after heating</note> of eggshells, or quicklime <note>original: *calx viva*; calcium oxide</note>.

*Anthos* is properly rosemary; but when applied to metals, it signifies the quintessence <note>the purest, most concentrated essence of a substance</note> or the Elixir of gold. Sometimes it refers to medicine extracted from beryls <note>a type of gemstone</note> from their own body, according to **Paracelsus** <note>a famous 16th-century Swiss physician and alchemist</note>.

*Athonor* <note>also spelled Athanor</note> is a furnace <note>specifically a self-feeding furnace designed to maintain a constant temperature for long periods</note>.

*Anticar*, *anucar*, or *ancinar* is Borax <note>a mineral salt used as a flux in soldering and to help melt metals</note>.

*Antimony* <note>original: *Antimonium*</note>, or *Alhofol*, is a stone from a vein of lead. It is called Saturnine Marcasite <note>original: *Marchasita Saturnii*; Saturn is the alchemical name for lead</note>, Saturnine Magnesia, or Stibium. A red variety is found in Indenburg <note>possibly Idrija, known for its mines</note>.

*Antimony*, when prepared spagyrically <note>using alchemical methods of separation and recombination to purify a substance</note>, is the supreme antidote for all poisons; for the poisons of natural things contain their own antidote. **Basil Valentine** <note>a legendary alchemist, often cited as an authority on antimony</note>, page 14.

The highest secret <note>original: *arcanum*</note> of *Antimony*, which he calls the "Stone of the Fire of Antimony," is of such great power and energy that it almost mimics the Philosophers' Stone in the curing of all diseases and the transmutation of metals. **Basil Valentine**, page 15.

**Basil Valentine** concludes that there are such great secrets and natural hidden wonders lying within Antimony that no mortal is capable of investigating them all; for the more we investigate, the more hidden and precious the things we find.

->page 16<-

*Aphebrinck* is Sulfur.

*Aphrodisia* is the age of Venus <note>original: *ætas Venerea*; referring to puberty or the age of sexual maturity</note>.

*Aphronitrum*: Just as salt and the "froth of salt" relate to one another, so do nitre <note>saltpeter</note> and the "froth of nitre" <note>original: *spuma nitri*; likely the efflorescence found on walls</note>. There is a great kinship between the salt and the nitre, and vice-versa; likewise between the froth of salt and the froth of nitre. The froth of nitre—*aphronitrum*, "flower of the rock," or "flower of the wall" (just like the froth of salt)—is a very light, clumpy, and crumbly substance. It is frothy, sharp to the touch, and of a nearly purple color, which is the best kind. We [call] the froth of nitre and *aphronitrum*...
