--- Page 44 ---
<meta>This page continues the entries under "A," specifically refining the definitions of "Aphronitrum" (saltpeter foam) and moving into various "Aquae" (waters) and substances like Ambergris. It maintains the alphabetical dictionary format established on the previous page.</meta>

->## CHEMICAL DICTIONARY<-
<page-num>22</page-num>

we say, and the foamy *aphronitrum* <note>original: *aphronitrum*; literally "foam of nitre," it refers to the efflorescence of saltpeter or soda found on walls</note>, which is born on walls and rocks. The foam of nitre varies according to the rocks and walls where it is born; its virtue is similar to burnt salt. These three are nearly the same thing: *aphronitrum* (that is, foam of nitre), flower of the stone <note>original: *flos petræ*</note>, and flower of the Asian stone <note>original: *flos Asiæ petræ*</note>. But they differ slightly in substance, for if it is dissolved by fire, it is the flower of the Asian stone; if it is not dissolved, it is our foam of nitre. In Jena, in Thuringia <note>a region in central Germany famous for its university and mineral deposits</note>, a most beautiful kind of *aphronitrum* is found, a variety which corresponds closely to the flower of the Asian stone described by **Dioscorides** <note>a 1st-century Greek physician and botanist</note>.

*Aquala* is Arsenic or Sulfur.
*Aquila* <note>literally "The Eagle"</note> is gold in the form of drops.
*Water of the Brain* <note>original: *Aqua Cerebri*</note> is water of Tartar <note>a substance collected from wine barrels, often used in alchemical purifications</note>.
*Aqua Elsabon* is the common water of the sun <note>likely referring to a solution of gold</note>.
*Stinking Water* <note>original: *Aqua foetida*</note> is the water of Mercury.
*Strong Water* <note>original: *Aqua fortis*; historically Nitric Acid</note> is that which is distilled by violent fires from sharp and corrosive materials, combined in a certain proportion, having a fiery power strong enough for corroding. The strongest is called *Stygian* <note>original: *stygia*; named after the River Styx in the underworld</note>, which, when mixed with sal ammoniac, rules over the rest, and in it gold may be dissolved <note>this refers to *Aqua Regia*, the "royal water" capable of dissolving gold</note>. Mixtures may be made with various increased virtues, according to advice, so that it may be suitable for specific tasks. That which is used for intensifying colors is called "Graduated Water." These are made like the strong waters from the same material, but with dyeing agents added, such as **cinnabar** <note>mercury sulfide, a red pigment</note> and **alum**. In these, it must be noted that nothing dyes unless at least the eighth or tenth cohobation <note>original: *cohobio*; the process of repeatedly redistilling a liquid over its solid residue to concentrate its power</note> is performed over the dregs; otherwise, they are not "fixed" <note>rendered stable or non-volatile</note>.
*Amber* <note>original: *Ambra*; referring here to Ambergris</note>: Some call this the sperm of the whale <note>original: *sperma ceti*</note>; others the fruit of a tree or a gum growing under the sea; others the liver of a fish; others the dung of a certain fish; others a kind of sea foam; others say it is born in the form of mushrooms under the sea. Consult **Serapion** <note>an influential 9th-century Arabic medical scholar</note> and **Almanso** <note>likely Al-Mansur, often associated with Rhazes, the Persian physician</note>.
*Aqua Holsobon* is the water of common salt.
*Water of the Lily* <note>original: *Aqua Lilii*</note> is orpiment <note>original: *auripigmentum*; a bright yellow arsenic sulfide mineral</note>.

*Water of Mercury* <note>original: *Aqua Mercurii*</note>.
*Sea Water* <note>original: *Aqua Marina*</note>.
*Water of nitre* <note>original: *Aqua nitri*</note> is alkali salt, or strong water <note>original: *aqua fortis*; historically nitric acid</note>.
*Permanent water* <note>original: *Aqua permanens*</note> is Gold and Silver <note>original: *Sol & Luna*; the Sun and Moon are common alchemical symbols for these metals</note> dissolved together in a liquid. It is also called celestial water and the Mercury of the philosophers.
*Philosophical water* <note>original: *Aqua philosophica*</note> is named by some as sublimated vinegar; by others, circulated wine; by others, perennial water that does not wet the hands.
*Palestine water* <note>original: *Aqua Palaestina*</note> is the flower of copper <note>original: *flos aeris*</note> or verdigris <note>original: *viride aeris*; the green crust that forms on copper or brass</note>.
*Rain water* <note>original: *Aqua pluvialis*</note> is fresh water.
*Reddish water, water of Megi, water of Segi* are names for water of vitriol <note>vitriol refers to sulfate minerals, often used to create sulfuric acid</note>.
*Salmatine water* <note>original: *Aqua Salmatina*</note> is made from salt.
*Saturnine water* <note>original: *Aqua Saturnia*</note> is water generated from the "three primes" <note>the alchemical principles of Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury</note> dissolved in the passages of the earth within transparent stones, such as radical acidic waters, etc. *Water of glass.*
*Water of life* <note>original: *Aqua vitae*</note> is Mercury; sometimes it is understood as distilled wine <note>ethanol or brandy</note>, and for a water composed of various species distilled from wine.
*Antimony* <note>original: *Antimonium*</note>: The greatest cordial <note>a medicine for the heart</note> of all waters.
*The Mortification of Antimony* is its sublimation <note>a process of heating a substance until it becomes vapor and then solidifies again</note>, as described in *Book 5 of the Nature of Things, page 429.*
*Oil of Antimony* is found in the *Great Surgery* <note>original: *Magna Chirurgia*; a major work by Paracelsus</note> *page 62.*
*Water of Caesar* or the composition of the King <note>likely referring to *aqua regia*, a mixture of acids capable of dissolving gold</note>, in the *Book on Persicaria, page 741.*
*Theriacal water* <note>original: *Aqua Theriacalis*; a medicinal water derived from Theriac, an ancient herbal compound used as an antidote</note>, in the *Book on Persicaria, page 742.*
The method of making *water of saltpeter* and *ammoniac* is in *Book 5 of the Nature of Things, page 417.*
*Distilled water* <note>original: *Aqua stillatitia*</note> is a specific extract of a certain consistency and division produced by the condensation of vapors drawn out of substances through the process of distillation. It is of two types.

...we say, and foamy saltpeter <note>original: *aphronitrum*; an efflorescent salt found on walls</note>, which is born on walls and rocks. The foam of nitre varies according to the rocks and walls where it is born: its power is similar to burnt salt. They are almost one and the same: *aphronitrum*, that is, foam of nitre, flower of stone, and flower of *Asian* stone: But they differ slightly in substance, for if it is dissolved by fire, it is the flower of *Asian* stone: If it is not dissolved, it is our foam of nitre. At Jena in Thuringia <note>a region in central Germany famous for its salt mines and university</note>, a very beautiful species of *aphronitrum* is found, a species which corresponds most closely to the flower of *Asian* stone described by Dioscorides <note>A 1st-century Greek physician and the father of pharmacology</note>.

*Aquala* is Arsenic or Sulfur.
*Aquila* <note>The Eagle</note> is gold in droplets <note>original: *aurum guttando*</note>.
*Water of the Brain* <note>original: *Aqua Cerebri*</note> is water of Tartar <note>a potassium salt deposited during wine fermentation, used as a solvent</note>.
*Aqua Elfabon* is common water of the sun <note>original: *aqua solis*; often refers to a solution of gold or a highly purified "solar" solvent</note>.
*Stinking water* <note>original: *Aqua fœtida*</note> is the water of Mercury.
*Strong water* <note>original: *Aqua fortis*; historically nitric acid</note> is that which is distilled from sharp and corrosive substances, combined in a certain proportion, by violent fires, having a fiery power strong in corroding. The strongest is called Stygian water <note>original: *stygia*</note>, which governs sal ammoniac along with the rest, and in it gold may be dissolved <note>This mixture of nitric acid and sal ammoniac is known today as *aqua regia*, the only acid that can dissolve gold</note>. Let the mixture be increased with various virtues/powers by design, so that it may be suited to certain things. It is called "gradated" <note>original: *gradatiora*</note> when it is used for improving the color or grade of tinctures. And these are made like the "strong waters" from the same material, but with dyeing agents added, such as cinnabar <note>mercury sulfide</note> [or] alum. In these it must be noted that nothing dyes unless at least an eighth or tenth cohobation <note>the process of repeatedly distilling a liquid off its solid residue to concentrate its power</note> is performed over the dregs, because otherwise, they will not be fixed <note>stabilized or made non-volatile</note>.

*Ambergris* <note>original: *Ambra*</note>. Some call this "whale seed" <note>original: *sperma ceti*</note>, others the fruit of a tree, or a gum growing under the sea; others the liver of a fish, others the dung of a certain fish, others a certain foam of the sea; others say it is born in the form of mushrooms under the sea. Consult Serapion <note>a 9th-century Arab medical scholar</note> and *Almansor* <note>likely referring to the Persian physician Rhazes, who wrote for the Caliph Al-Mansur</note>.

*Water of Holsobon* <note>original: *Aqua holsobon*</note> is water of bread salt <note>common table salt</note>.
*Water of the Lily* <note>original: *Aqua Lilii*</note> is orpiment <note>original: *auripigmentum*; a yellow arsenic mineral used as a pigment and in alchemy</note>.

...we call it, and foamy saltpeter <note>original: *aphronitrum*; literally "foam of nitre," a salt crust found on walls</note>, which grows on walls and rocks. The foam of nitre varies according to the rocks and walls where it grows: its power is similar to burnt salt. Foam of nitre, the flower of the rock <note>original: *flos petræ*</note>, and the flower of Asian rock <note>original: *flos Asiæ petræ*; likely a reference to the "stone of Assos" in Lycia</note> are almost the same thing. However, they differ slightly in substance, for if it is dissolved by fire, it is the flower of Asian rock; if it is not dissolved, it is our own foam of nitre. In Jena, in Thuringia, a very beautiful species of saltpeter foam is found, a species that corresponds closely to the flower of Asian rock described by Dioscorides <note>a famous Greek physician and botanist of the 1st century AD</note>.

*Aquala* is Arsenic or Sulfur.
*Aquila* <note>literally "The Eagle"</note> is gold obtained by dropping <note>perhaps referring to granulated gold or a specific refining process</note>.
*Water of the Brain* <note>original: *Aqua Cerebri*</note> is water of Tartar <note>a solution made from the crusty potassium bitartrate deposits found in wine barrels</note>.
*Aqua Elsabon* is common water of the sun <note>original: *aqua solis*; in alchemical shorthand, the "Sun" always refers to Gold</note>.
*Stinking Water* <note>original: *Aqua fœtida*</note> is the water of Mercury.
*Strong Water* <note>original: *Aqua fortis*; historically nitric acid</note> is that which is distilled by violent fires from sharp and corrosive substances mixed in certain proportions; it possesses a powerful fiery strength for corroding. The most powerful is called Stygian water <note>original: *stygia*; named after the River Styx in the underworld</note>, which governs sal ammoniac <note>ammonium chloride</note> along with the rest, and in it gold is dissolved <note>this mixture of nitric acid and sal ammoniac creates "Aqua Regia," the only acid known to dissolve gold</note>. Mixtures may be increased with various powers by design, so that they are suited to specific tasks. It is called "graded" <note>original: *gradatiora*</note> when it is used for intensifying colors or tinctures. These are made like strong waters from the same material, but with the addition of coloring agents like cinnabar <note>mercury sulfide, used for red pigment</note> or alum. Regarding these, it should be noted that nothing provides a color unless at least an eighth or tenth cohobation <note>original: *cohobio*; the alchemical process of repeatedly distilling a liquid by pouring it back onto the solid matter left behind</note> is performed over the dregs; otherwise, the colors will not be fixed.

*Amber* <note>original: *Ambra*; referring here to ambergris, a valuable substance found in the ocean</note>. Some call this "whale sperm" <note>original: *sperma ceti*</note>, others the fruit of a tree, or a gum growing under the sea; others call it the liver of a fish, others the dung of a certain fish, others a kind of sea foam, and others say it grows in the form of mushrooms under the sea. Consult Serapion <note>an influential 9th-century Arab physician</note> and Almansor <note>likely referring to the Persian physician Rhazes, who dedicated works to the ruler Al-Mansur</note>.
*Aqua holsobon* is the water of common salt <note>literally "bread salt"</note>.
*Water of the Lily* <note>original: *Aqua Lilii*</note> is orpiment <note>original: *auripigmentum*; a deep yellow arsenic sulfide mineral</note>.

<page-num>24</page-num>

->#### CHEMICAL DICTIONARY<-

1. *Distilled waters* <note>original: *Stillatitia*</note>. Simply distilled waters are those that are milder in power and thinner in consistency, produced by a simpler method of distillation. For the phlegmatic <note>watery or inactive</note> element is coarser and less subtle, differing very little from ordinary external water. Simply distilled water has less fire and more wateriness than a solvent; but it has more fire and less coarseness than "phlegm" <note>the watery residue of a distillation</note>.

2. *Solvent water* <note>original: *Aqua solvens*</note> differs from a simple distillate by its penetrating fiery sharpness or acidity, and by a more laborious distillation, so that it contains as little as possible of the simple element of water, but rather a fiery flow. It is as if they were watery fires; they are called "burning waters" <note>original: *aquæ ardentes*; often referring to alcohols or strong acids</note>. Geber <note>an influential early alchemist</note> calls them "sharp waters" <note>original: *aquæ acutæ*</note>, and others call them the "keys of the philosophers." Mercury <note>original: *hydrargyrus*</note> attains the power of these solvents in some respects through its whole substance. Common people usually distinguish between what are simply called distilled waters and those that are corrosive.

*Aquarius* is Iron <note>original: *Ferreum*</note>.

*Aracon* is Brass <note>original: *Laton*</note>. See *Brass* <note>original: *Laton*</note>.

*Aquilegia*: Theophrastus <note>likely the ancient Greek "father of botany"</note> calls the Broom with yellow flowers *Aquilegia*.

*Aquilena* is Royal Consound <note>original: *consolida regalis*; likely a species of Larkspur</note>.

*Arbores* <note>literally "Trees"</note> is a spotted defilement of the skin; there is no ulceration in the beginning, but it develops over time. It is the same as *Morphea* <note>a condition causing discolored patches on the skin</note>.

*Arcos* is burnt copper <note>original: *Æs ustum*; copper that has been heated until it scales</note>, <note>original: *aycophes azuphora*</note>.

*Aremaros* is Cinnabar <note>mercury sulfide, a bright red mineral</note>.

*Area* belongs to the mines of the opposite head <note>Likely referring to a specific anatomical location on the skull or a localized skin patch in a medical context</note>.

*Arenamen*, *arenarmei*, is Armenian bole <note>a reddish medicinal clay used as an astringent</note>.

*Antera* is a medicine extracted from hyacinths, named for the resemblance of the yellow or lemon color occurring in the flowers of lilies and similar plants.

*Aphorism* <note>original: *Aphorismus*</note> is used in its common sense, namely as a general rule in medicine.

*Celestial water* <note>original: *Aqua cælestis*</note> is rectified wine <note>highly concentrated alcohol</note>, so that in a certain way of heaven...

<page-num>25</page-num>

->#### CHEMICAL DICTIONARY<-

provided that it assumes the nature and likeness [of something else], after many revolutions <note>cycles of distillation or circulation</note> have been completed.

*Celestial water* <note>original: *Aqua cælestina*</note> is mercurial water. *The Mercury of the Philosophers*.

*Corroding water* <note>original: *Aqua corrodens*</note> is vinegar and every corrosive liquid.

*Water of wine dregs* <note>original: *Aqua fæcum vini*</note> is that which is made from the lime <note>the calcined residue</note> of wine dregs, dissolved upon marble in the manner of Oil of Tartar <note>original: *oleum Tartari*; a concentrated solution of potassium carbonate</note>.

*Lubricated water* <note>original: *Aqua lubricata*</note> is that which is made with mucilaginous substances, such as sugars, juleps <note>sweet medicinal syrups</note>, and similar things.

*Permanent water* <note>original: *Aqua permanens*</note> is that which is made from the two most perfect metallic bodies <note>usually gold and silver</note> through philosophical solution.

*Saturnian water* <note>original: *Aqua Saturnia*</note> is that which retains within itself the nature of the first three <note>referring to the *tria prima*: Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury</note>, such as the waters of hot springs naturally medicated by the nature of those things through which they pass.

*Solvent water* <note>original: *Aqua solvens*</note> is vinegar distilled as mentioned above, and *aqua fortis* <note>nitric acid</note>.

*Aquaster* is a vision sometimes presenting something to our eyes that truly is not there; it exists only as a certain appearance, not in reality itself <note>A Paracelsian term for a deceptive or astral entity/illusion</note>.

*Aquila* <note>literally "the Eagle"</note>, which is the queen of birds, is used as a name for sal ammoniac <note>original: *sal armoniaco*; ammonium chloride</note> because of its lightness in sublimations <note>the process of turning a solid directly into vapor</note>. However, Paracelsus wants it to be understood in many cases as precipitated mercury. *Aquila* is the spirit of Mercury.

*Eagle of the philosophers* <note>original: *Aquila philosophorum*</note> is the mercury of metals—that is, a metal reduced into its primary matter.

*Tree of the sea* <note>original: *Arbor maris*</note> is coral, which grows and flourishes in the sea like a shrub. <note>original: *Gorgonion, Dendritres, Lithodendron, Curallion*; these are Greek-derived synonyms meaning "the Gorgon-thing," "tree-stone," and "stone-tree"</note>.

*Arcanum* in general signifies something most hidden. Likewise for Paracelsus, it is an incorporeal virtue hidden in natural things, and a participant as if in a perpetual and immortal life derived into it from heaven, which...

--- Page 51 ---
<meta>This page continues the alphabetical dictionary of chemical and medicinal terms. The opening sentence completes the entry for "Arcanum" from the previous page, discussing the potential of substances to be enhanced through alchemical processes.</meta>

-># CHEMICAL DICTIONARY<-

<page-num>26</page-num>

->#### CHEMICAL DICTIONARY<-

<meta>catchword: Aridura</meta>

can be multiplied by the <term>Spagyric art</term> <note>The alchemical process of separating and recombining the essential components of a substance to purify it</note> beyond its prior condition. The virtue <note>The inherent power or essence</note> of each thing operates a thousand times more effectively than the physical thing itself.

**Arcaltes** is, according to <term>Paracelsus</term> <note>The influential 16th-century Swiss-German physician and alchemist</note>, the foundation or column of the earth, which does not appear to be supported by its own companions but by another "magnal" <note>a great work or wonder</note> and secret of God; namely, he calls this very thing **Archaltem**. **Archates**, according to **Rulandus** <note>Martin Ruland the Elder, a physician and author of a famous alchemical dictionary</note>.

***Archeus*** is the highest, exalted, and invisible spirit which is separated from bodies, is exalted, and ascends; it is the hidden power of nature common to all things, acting as both an artisan and a physician. Likewise, the **Archiatros** <note>Original: *Archiatros*, meaning "Chief Physician"</note> is the supreme physician of nature, who distributes to each thing its own specific *Archeus*, and to every limb secretly through the **Ares** <note>The force that grants specific form</note>. Furthermore, the first *Archeus* in nature is the most hidden force producing all things from the **Iliaste** <note>The primordial, unformed matter of the universe</note>, supported solely by divine power.

***Ares*** is the hidden dispenser of nature within the three first principles <note>The *tria prima*: Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury</note> from which all individual things consist. It assigns to all things their own form, species, and substance, so that each may take on its own specific nature rather than that of another. One should therefore note the beautifully ordered, divinely ordained difference in nature between these three, to speak according to the custom of the schools: **Iliastes** is the substance of the most general category, consisting of the first universal matter of all things. The ***Archeus***, the first dispenser of nature, initially arranges this into three categories—namely, sulfur, mercury, and salt—and from there produces all things into their next-closest categories. Finally, the ***Ares***, the second dispenser of nature, arrives and produces forms and species into individual things from those categories. Each of these individuals has its own unique root, stem, flower, and leaves.

***Ardentia*** <note>Burning things</note> are those substances which, while not taken as food or drink, are by their own nature liable to burning, such as **amber** <note>original: *carabe*</note>, **turpentine** <note>original: *terebinthina*</note>, **jet** <note>original: *gagates*; a dark gemstone derived from fossilized wood</note>, and similar things.

<summary>This page defines key Paracelsian concepts of the vital forces of nature, specifically the Archeus (the inner physician/artisan) and the Ares (the force of differentiation), explaining how they transform primordial matter into specific living things.</summary>

<keywords>Archeus, Ares, Iliastes, Arcaltes, Paracelsus, Spagyric art, Archiatros, Tria Prima, Ardentia, amber, alchemy, cosmology</keywords>

--- Page 52 ---
<meta>This page continues the alphabetical dictionary of chemical and medicinal terms, following the entry for "Ares." This section covers terms starting with "Ar-," focusing heavily on the vitalistic concepts of Paracelsus and the alchemical composition of silver.</meta>

-># CHEMICAL DICTIONARY<-

<page-num>27</page-num>

*Aridura* is the total wasting away of the body or limbs, or "blasting" <note>original: *syderatio*, a term used for a sudden stroke or withering as if struck by a star</note>, as they call it. Gangrene <note>original: *sphacelus*</note>, total decay <note>original: *telia*</note>, and tissue death <note>original: *necrosis*</note>.

*Arles crudum* (Raw Arles) are drops that fall in the month of June, similar to the dew that drips in May.

*Aromata* (Aromatics) are all things that give off a sweet and very pleasant scent and fragrance, which are otherwise generally called aromatics.

*Arcanum Materiale* (Material Secret) is a specific extract very close to the matter of the physical body. Since the body's matter is made of a twofold element—moist and dry (for Air and Fire are formal rather than material, and represent the active principle)—this arcanum is therefore twofold: a distilled essence and a specific <term>coagulum</term> <note>a thickened or solid substance</note>. The **Specific Arcanum** is an extract of the interior nature that more closely reflects the substance of each species, so that it can be recognized within it.

*Archeus of Paracelsus* is the vital aura, the director of seeds and life; it is the "impelling force" <note>original Greek: *τὸ ἔνορμον* (*to enormon*)</note> of <term>Hippocrates</term> <note>the ancient Greek "father of medicine"</note> and <term>Van Helmont</term> <note>Jan Baptist van Helmont, a 17th-century physician-chemist who expanded on Paracelsian ideas</note>.

*Archeos* is an invisible essence wandering about and separating itself from bodies; the medicinal power and virtue of nature.

*Archeus* is the power producing things from the <term>Iliaste</term> <note>In Paracelsian philosophy, the primordial, unformed matter of the universe</note>, the distributor and arranger of all things.

*Arena* (Sand). <term>Rulandus</term> <note>Martin Ruland the Elder, author of a famous 1612 alchemical dictionary</note> lists the species of this on page 54; let those who are interested look there.

*Arfar* and *arsag* are <term>Arsenic</term>.

*Argentum* (Silver) is called **The Moon** <note>original: *Luna*</note> by the Chemists, to whom it is also attributed. It is the metal closest to gold: white with a pure whiteness, clean, hard, and resonant. The <term>tincture</term> <note>the essential coloring principle or quality</note> of its whiteness comes from pure <term>quicksilver</term> <note>mercury</note>, which is nearly fixed, clear, and white; and from such a <term>sulfur</term> <note>In alchemy, sulfur represents the active, combustible principle, while mercury represents the metallic essence</note> that is clean, fixed, white, and clear, which has fallen upon the substance of the quicksilver. It lacks only a small amount of fixation, color, and weight [to become gold]. It is, as it were, the daughter of nature (who prioritizes gold) when pure quicksilver and white, non-burning sulfur combine.

This section defines core Paracelsian concepts regarding the vital forces and the formation of matter.</meta>

-># CHEMICAL DICTIONARY<-

<page-num>26</page-num>

...can be multiplied by the <term>Spagyric art</term> <note>the alchemical method of separating and recombining substances to concentrate their virtues</note> beyond its former condition. The virtue of each thing operates a thousand times more effectively than the thing itself.

***Arcaltes***, according to <term>Paracelsus</term> <note>Theophrastus von Hohenheim, a 15th-century physician-alchemist who revolutionized medicine</note>, is the foundation or column of the earth. It does not appear to be supported by its own companions, but by another "magnal" <note>a Paracelsian term for a great or miraculous power</note> and secret of God; namely, he calls this very thing the *Archaltem*. <term>Rulandus</term> <note>Martin Ruland the Elder, author of a famous alchemical lexicon</note> calls it *Archates*.

***Archeus*** is the supreme, exalted, and invisible spirit which is separated from bodies, becomes exalted, and ascends. It is the hidden power of nature common to all things—the craftsman and the physician. Likewise, the *Archiatros* is the supreme physician of nature, who distributes to each thing its own peculiar *Archeus*, and to every limb secretly through the *Ares*. Furthermore, the first *Archeus* in nature is the most hidden force producing all things from the <term>Iliaste</term> <note>the primordial, unformed matter from which everything is created</note>, supported only by divine power.

***Ares*** is the hidden steward of nature within the three first principles <note>the *Tria Prima*: Sulfur, Mercury, and Salt</note> from which individual things consist. He uniquely assigns to all things their own form, species, and substance, so that each may take on its own specific nature and not that of another. Therefore, one must note the beautifully ordered difference within nature, established by God, to speak in the manner of the schools: *Iliastes* is the substance of the most general genus, consisting in the first universal matter of all things. The *Archeus*, the first steward of nature, first arranges this into three genera—namely into <term>sulfur</term>, <term>mercury</term>, and <term>salt</term>—and from there produces all things into their nearest categories. Finally, the *Ares*, the second steward of nature, approaches and produces specific forms and species from these categories into individual things, each of which has its own unique root, stem, flower, and leaves.

***Ardentia*** (Burning things) are substances which are not taken as food or drink but are by their own nature liable to burning, such as <term>amber</term> <note>original: *carabe*</note>, turpentine, <term>jet</term> <note>original: *gagates*, a type of fossilized wood like coal</note>, and similar items.

<meta>This page continues the alphabetical definitions of alchemical and Paracelsian terms, following the previous discussion of Ares and the Archeus. The catchword "Aridura" from page 26 matches the first entry here on page 27.</meta>

-># CHEMICAL DICTIONARY<-

<header>CHEMICAL DICTIONARY</header>

<page-num>27</page-num>

***Aridura*** is the total consumption of the body or limbs, or "blasting" <note>original: *syderatio*; a term used for limbs that appear struck by an evil star or sudden stroke</note>, as they call it. It is also known as gangrene <note>original: *sphacelus*</note>, corruption <note>original: *telia*</note>, or the death of tissue <note>original: *necrosis*</note>.

***Arles crudum*** (Raw Arles) are drops falling in the month of June, much like the dew that drips in May.

***Aromata*** (Spices) are all those things that give off a sweet and very pleasant odor and fragrance from themselves, which are otherwise generally called aromatics.

***Arcanum Materiale*** (Material Secret) is a specific extract closer to the physical matter of the body. Since the matter of the body is composed of a double element—the moist and the dry (for air and fire are "formal" <note>acting as the shape or soul of the thing</note> rather than material, and have the role of the efficient cause)—this *arcanum* <note>a hidden, powerful essence</note> is twofold: a distilled essence and a specific coagulant. The specific *arcanum* is an extract of the inner nature that more closely reflects the substance of each species, so that it can be recognized within it.

***Archeus Paracelsi*** (The Archeus of Paracelsus) is the vital aura, the director of seeds and of life. It is the "indwelling force" <note>original Greek: *τὸ ἐνορμῶν* (*to enormon*)</note> mentioned by Hippocrates, according to <term>Van Helmont</term> <note>Jan Baptist van Helmont, a Flemish chemist and physician who expanded on the concept of the Archeus as a vital governing spirit</note>.

***Archeos*** is an invisible species wandering about and separating itself from bodies; it is the power of the physician and the virtue of nature.

***Archeus*** is the force producing things from the <term>Iliaste</term> <note>the primordial, unformed matter from which all elements were created</note>; the dispenser and composer of all things.

***Arena*** (Sand). <term>Martin Ruland</term> <note>author of a famous 1612 alchemical dictionary</note> lists its types on page 54; anyone who wishes may look there.

***Arfar, arsag*** is Arsenic.

***Argentum*** (Silver) is called the Moon <note>original: *Luna*</note> by the Chemists, to whom it is also attributed. It is the metal closest to gold: white with a pure whiteness, clean, hard, and resonant. Its white tint comes from pure, almost "fixed" <note>stable and resistant to fire</note>, clear, white quicksilver <note>mercury</note>, and from a similar sulfur—clean, fixed, white, and clear—which has fallen upon the substance of the quicksilver. It lacks only a little fixation, color, and weight. It is, as it were, the daughter of nature who was intending to produce gold, through the union of quicksilver and a white, non-burning sulfur.

***Arcaltes*** is, according to <term>Paracelsus</term> <note>The famous 16th-century Swiss-German physician and alchemist who revolutionized medicine with chemistry</note>, the foundation or column of the earth, which does not appear to be sustained by its fellow elements, but by another greatness and secret of God; specifically, he calls this very thing *Arcaltes*. According to <term>Rulandus</term> <note>Martin Ruland the Elder, author of a famous 1612 alchemical dictionary</note>, it is also called *Archates*.

***Ares*** is the hidden dispenser of nature within the three first principles <note>the *Tria Prima*: Sulfur, Mercury, and Salt</note> from which all things consist. The *Ares* uniquely assigns to all things their own form, appearance, and substance, so that each may take on its own specific nature rather than that of another. One must therefore note the beautifully ordered difference within nature among these three—to speak in the manner of the schools <note>referring to Scholastic or academic terminology</note>. ***Iliastes*** is the substance of the most general category, consisting of the first universal matter of all things. The first dispenser of nature, the ***Archeus***, first arranges this matter into three categories—namely into sulfur, mercury, and salt—and from there produces all things in their most immediate categories. Finally, the ***Ares***, the second dispenser of nature, approaches and produces specific forms and species from these categories into individual things. Each of these has its own singular root, stalk, flower, and leaves.

***Ardentia*** (Burning things) are those substances which, though not taken as food or drink, are by their own nature liable to burning <note>combustible</note>, such as amber <note>original: *carabe*</note>, turpentine, jet <note>original: *gagates*; a minor gemstone and fossilized coal</note>, and similar materials.

<meta>This page continues the entry for Silver (Argentum) and begins the entry for Quicksilver (Mercury), referencing Martin Ruland’s Lexicon Alchemiae. It maintains the alphabetical dictionary format found on previous pages.</meta>

-># CHEMICAL DICTIONARY<-

<header>CHEMICAL DICTIONARY</header>

<page-num>28</page-num>

Silver is found in mines. 1. That which is immediately itself <note>original: *suum*; meaning native silver found in its pure metallic state</note>, which is produced without the labor of furnaces, and is found pure in the veins. Such silver exists without smelting, having been generated as such from its origin. It possesses its own natural color, though some rock almost always adheres to it, having been pulled away in the valley, as we see it dug out from the mines. This pure silver embraces the stone in very thin plates <note>original: *bracteis*</note>; sometimes it displays the appearance of hair, sometimes of small twigs; sometimes it takes the form of a sphere, as if wound with white or red threads; and sometimes it displays the appearance of a tree, a tool, mountains, herbs, and other things.

<margin>page 56.</margin>
**Rulandus** <note>Martin Ruland the Elder (1522–1602), author of a famous alchemical dictionary</note> affirms that he has seen a whole fish, **Christ** crucified with the **Blessed Mary**, and even a serpent and a scorpion, etc., formed in this way out of pure silver in the bowels of the earth.

2. Raw silver <note>original: *Rude*</note>, as called by the Germans, of various species and colors: red, lead-colored, purple, black, ash-gray, reddish, etc.

3. That which is smelted <note>original: *excoquitur*</note> from metals, such as from pyrite or lead-ore <note>original: *plumbagine*</note> by the Germans. He who desires to know more concerning silver should read **Ruland** from page 56 to 66.

**Quicksilver** <note>original: *Argentum vivum*</note>, which Chemists call **Mercury**, is a viscous water in the bowels of the earth, of a subtle substance, from a white earth, united by a most tempered heat in a total union, through its smallest parts, so long as the moisture is tempered by the dry, and the dry by the moisture, in equal measure.

And **Quicksilver** is the other part of the **philosophers' stone** <note>The legendary substance capable of turning base metals into gold and granting eternal life</note> according to the Chemists. It is also the second principle and the mother of all metals; and according to how it joins with its father and male, **sulfur**, it causes perfection or imperfection in metals. Metals also (like a fetus) take more from the mother, so to speak, than from the father. From this they largely draw their origin, and into this they are mostly resolved. Quicksilver is of two kinds: 1. Native, 2. Artificial.

--- Page 58 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of Quicksilver (Mercury) from the previous page, detailing its natural and artificial forms, followed by alphabetical entries for clays, silver-related terms, and various alchemical substances like dragon's blood and vitriol.</meta>

-># CHEMICAL DICTIONARY<-

<header>CHEMICAL DICTIONARY</header>

<page-num>29</page-num>

1. Native quicksilver, in its natural color, produced without smelting, which is found in its pure state within metals in pools into which it has flowed out of the mineral fibers; it is the mother and the element of Metals. There is also a stone found in these veins, whose pocket <note>original: *vomica*; literally an abscess or cavity, here referring to a pocket of liquid within the ore</note> of eternal liquid is called quicksilver.

2. Artificial quicksilver, which is made or "cooked" <note>smelted</note> from minium <note>the native stone, often cinnabar or red lead</note>, or from a vein of minium; it is also found in mines. **Dioscorides** <note>An ancient Greek physician and pharmacologist</note> in Book 5, chapter 6, teaches the preservation, use, poisonous nature, and antidotes of this substance. The Greeks call both the native and the artificial varieties *hydrargyron* <note>original: *hydrargyron*; from the Greek for "water-silver"</note>.

**Note.** While quicksilver is liquid in our workshops, it is called "crude" quicksilver. When it has been "killed" <note>original: *mortificatum*; chemically altered or oxidized so it is no longer liquid</note>, it is called "burnt" in the workshops, or "sublimated" by some. Alchemists categorize quicksilver as cold and moist in the fourth degree, liquid in the third, white in the second, and dark in the first.

**Quicksilver**, that is, the Spirit, or **Mercury**, the "fugitive servant," or "white smoke." **Ruland** <note>Martin Ruland the Elder (1522–1602), author of the *Lexicon Alchemiae*</note> enumerates various species of quicksilver from page 68 to 70, listing up to 23 types.

**Clays** <note>original: *Argillæ*</note> are so called because they are similar to the clays used by potters. There are various types of these clays found in mines, of which **Ruland** (page 7) records 16 kinds. Pigments could be made from all of these if they were not so damp; excellent jars could be made from them if the clays were correctly worked by potters into vessels in the usual manner.

***Argistata*** is a substance treated with wax <note>original: *Incerata*</note>.

***Argyros*** is Silver; hence ***Lithargyros*** <note>litharge, a lead oxide</note> is the "silver-stone"; for *Lithos* <note>Greek: *lithos*</note> means stone.

***Asabon*** is Soap.

***Asagen*** is dragon's blood <note>a bright red resin obtained from certain plants, used in medicine and alchemy</note>. Also written as ***Asegen*** and ***Azogen***.

***Asagi*** or ***Azegi*** is vitriol <note>a sulfate of a metal</note> or red ink <note>original: *atramentum rubeum*</note>.

Silver is found in mines. 1. That which is immediately itself <note>meaning native or pure silver</note>, which exists without the work of furnaces, which is found pure in the veins; such silver requires no smelting because it was generated that way at its origin. It possesses its own natural color, though some rock usually still clings to it when it is brought up from the valley, as we see when it is dug out of the mines. This pure silver clings to the stone in very thin plates <note>original: *bracteis*</note>: sometimes it displays the appearance of hair, sometimes the appearance of little rods or twigs; occasionally it takes the form of a globe, as if wound with white or red threads; at other times it displays the appearance of a tree, a tool, mountains, herbs, and other things. **Ruland** <note>Martin Ruland the Elder (1522–1602), a physician and alchemist</note> asserts that he saw an entire fish, Christ crucified with **Lady Mary**, and even a serpent and a scorpion, etc., formed this way out of pure silver in the bowels of the earth. page 56.

2. Raw silver <note>original: *Rude*; likely referring to silver ore</note>, called so by the Germans, of various species and colors: red, leaden, purple, black, ash-colored, reddish, etc.

3. That which is smelted from metals, such as from pyrite or lead-glance <note>original: *plumbagine*; a term used for graphite or galena/lead ore</note> by the Germans. Whoever desires to know more concerning silver should read **Ruland**, from page 56 to 66.

*Quicksilver, which the Chemists call **Mercury**, is* a viscous water in the bowels of the earth, of a subtle substance, a white earth, united by a most temperate heat into a total union, through its smallest parts, until the moisture is equally tempered by the dry, and the dry by the moisture.

And **Quicksilver** is the second part of the philosopher's stone according to the Chemists. It is the other principle, and the mother of all metals; according to how it joins with the father and its masculine sulfur, it causes both perfection and imperfection in metals, and the metals themselves. (Like a fetus), they have more—so to speak—from the mother than the father: for this reason they draw most of their origin from it, and into it they are mostly resolved. Quicksilver is twofold. 1. Native, 2. Artificial.

-># CHEMICAL DICTIONARY<-

<header>CHEMICAL DICTIONARY</header>

<page-num>29</page-num>

<sig>C 5</sig>

1. Native <note>occurring naturally in the earth</note>, in its own color, without the need for smelting; that which is found within metals in pools into which it has flowed out from the fibers [of the ore] is the mother and element of Metals. There is also a stone in those veins whose "blister" of eternal liquid is called quicksilver <note>original: *argentum vivum*; literally "living silver"</note>.

2. Artificial, which is made or "cooked" either from the stone *minium* <note>here referring to cinnabar, the ore of mercury</note> itself or from a vein of *minium*; it is found in mines. *Dioscorides* <note>Pedanius Dioscorides, a 1st-century Greek physician</note> *Book 5, Chapter 6* teaches the preservation, use, poison, and antidotes of this substance. The Greeks call both the native and the artificial forms *hydrargyron* <note>literally "water-silver"</note>.

Note: While quicksilver is liquid, in our workshops it is called "crude quicksilver"; but when it is "mortified" <note>original: *mortificatum*; meaning chemically stabilized or killed so it no longer flows</note>, the workshops call it "burnt-together," or by some, "sublimated." Chemists categorize quicksilver as cold and moist in the fourth degree, liquid in the third, white in the second, and dark in the first.

*Quicksilver*, that is, the Spirit, or Mercury, the "fugitive servant," the "white smoke." **Ruland** <note>Martin Ruland the Elder</note> enumerates on pages 68 to 70 various species of quicksilver, up to twenty-three types.

*Clays* <note>original: *Argillæ*</note> are so called because they are similar to those clays which potters use.

There are various kinds of these clays found in mines, of which **Ruland** (page 7) records sixteen types. Pigments could be made from all of these if they were not so damp. Excellent pots could be made from them if the clays were properly processed by potters into jars in the usual manner.

*Argistata* is treated with wax <note>original: *incerata*</note>.

*Argyros* is Silver. From this comes *Lithargyros* <note>Litharge or lead monoxide</note>, meaning "silver stone"; for *Lithos* means "stone."

*Asabon* is Soap.

*Asagen* is Dragon’s Blood <note>a bright red resin used in alchemy and medicine</note>. Also written as *Asegen* and *Azogen*.

*Asagi* or *Azegi* is vitriol <note>a sulfate mineral</note> or red ink.

<vocab>Argentum vivum, Mercurius, Rulandus, Dioscorides, Hydrargyron, Argillæ, Argyros, Asabon, Asagen, Asagi, Vitriolum</vocab>

<header>CHEMICAL DICTIONARY</header>

<page-num>30</page-num>

*Asamar* is the green of copper <note>original: *viride æris*; verdigris</note>. *Asingar*, or *Asugar*.

*Aseb* or *asep* is alum <note>a naturally occurring mineral salt used as a mordant in dyeing and as an astringent</note>. It is a metallic thing, or a vein of the earth that possesses a substance halfway between vitriol <note>original: *calcanthum*; blue or green copperas</note> and salt, and is found in mines. It is a "salt-silt" of the earth, as **Pliny** says in *Book 35, Chapter 15*. It is formed from water and mud—that is, from the nature of overflowing earth. Once gathered in winter, it is ripened by the summer suns. It is a vein of earth which, through excessive heat, is transformed into a white color; every vitriol contains alum. *Azub, azel*.

*Ased* is the Lion <note>In alchemy, the "Lion" often refers to a powerful acidic solvent, like *aqua regia*, or to the fixed part of a substance</note>.

*Asdenigi* is Hematite <note>original: *Æmatites*; an iron ore known as bloodstone</note>. *Azedegim*.

*Asenec* is the Sun <note>original: *Sol*; the common alchemical name for Gold</note>.

*Asmaga* is a mixing or commingling of certain metals with one another.

*Asoper* is Soot.

*Asseres* [Beams] are those things placed continuously upon the sides; also the outermost parts of a cut tree.

*Afubedegi* is a stone that cuts other stones <note>likely referring to a diamond or emery</note>.

*Atac* is *tath*, or nitre <note>saltpeter</note>.

*Atebras* is a sublimation vessel <note>a piece of laboratory glassware used to heat a solid until it vaporizes and then condenses back into a solid state</note>.

*Attingat* is the flower of copper <note>original: *flos æris*; the oxide or patina formed on copper</note>. *Atincar, Borax*.

*Attingir* is an earthen capsule <note>a small ceramic container or box</note>.

*Atureb* is glass.

*Aurancum* and *aurantum* are eggshells.

*Auraric, azoch, azog, azet, Besec* is Mercury <note>Quicksilver</note>.

*Bird of Hermes, Copper of Hermes, the Flying Bird*—so called because it flies up into the heights, yet descends again to the earth for its nourishment; hence the earth is the nurse of all things <note>This is a direct reference to the *Emerald Tablet of Hermes Trismegistus*, describing the volatile nature of Mercury which rises as vapor and returns to the bottom of the flask</note>.

*Auripigmentum* [Orpiment] is yellow Arsenic. It is called *Operment* by the Germans, and painters are accustomed to use it. It is crusty and shimmering with a golden color. It is related to a certain sulfur, being of similar material and odor. It is a "terrestrial dung-heap" (if I may call it so) in the caverns of the earth, which...

...through long boiling it passes into the substance of orpiment <note>original: *auripigmenti*; a yellow arsenic sulfide mineral used as a pigment</note>. See more in Ruland <note>Martin Ruland the Elder, author of the *Lexicon alchemiae*</note>, pages 87 to 90, if you please.

*Azarnet.* <note>An alchemical name for arsenic</note>

*Aurichalcum* is Copper <note>specifically referring to brass or a gold-colored copper alloy</note>.

*Aurum Philosophorum* [Philosophers' Gold] is lead.

*Aurum vivum* [Living Gold] is quicksilver <note>original: *argentum vivum*; mercury</note>.

*Auver* is pure or gentle water.

*Azaa* is Magra, a Red Earth <note>a type of red silicate or clay</note>.

*Azci* is ink <note>alchemically, this often refers to metallic salts used in making pigments</note>. *Azec* is green ink <note>likely green vitriol or copper acetate</note>.

*Azeg, azegi, azeci* is vitriol <note>a sulfate of a metal, such as iron or copper</note>. *Azuric* is red vitriol.

*Azem* is cooked butter <note>in an alchemical context, this usually refers to a substance with a fatty or oily consistency reached through heat</note>. *Azemasor* is Red Lead <note>original: *Minium*</note> or Cinnabar <note>mercury sulfide</note>.

*Azuc* is red coral. *Azubo* is a chemical vessel.

*Azimar* and *azamar* is Vermilion or Red Lead. *Azimar* is also burnt copper <note>original: *æs ustum*; copper oxide created by roasting copper with sulfur</note>.

*Azungia Vitri* <note>original: "Glass Fat"; commonly known as Sandiver or glass gall, the scum that forms on the surface of molten glass</note>.

*Azoch* is Latten <note>a brass-like alloy</note>; it is copper tinted with a golden color by calamine stone <note>zinc ore</note>, etc.

*Aroph* is mandrake. The *Aroph* of **Paracelsus** <note>a renowned 16th-century Swiss-German physician and alchemist</note> cures the stone <note>kidney or bladder stones</note>.

*Arfaneck* is the name for sublimated arsenic.

*Arsenicum* [Arsenic] is the "lightning of metals"; it is also the salt of those metals and of Saturn <note>the alchemical name for lead</note>. In several places, it is also found written as *artanec* or *artanech*.

*Artetiscus* is the name given to one who suffers from a defect of some limb.

*Arthoicum* is a red oil drawn by art from the roots of herbs digested together with bread in dung <note>animal dung provided a steady, gentle heat for long-term digestion or fermentation in the laboratory</note>.

*Asphaltum* is a bitumen drawn from the silt of earth and water in the likeness of pitch. It is also the "flower of copper" <note>original: *flos æris*; the green or reddish patina/oxide found on copper</note>.

<meta>This page continues the alphabetical glossary of alchemical and medical terms, picking up where the previous page left off with the letter 'A'. # CHEMICAL DICTIONARY

*Ascendentia* [Ascendants] are said to be the signs or stars of the firmament of heaven, and especially the sidereal spirits <note>the vital or celestial forces believed to influence human health and the properties of materials</note>.

*Asaphatum* is a creeping skin eruption, or impetigo, or a scabies under the skin generated in the pores; like little worms, if the skin is squeezed, they leap out as long threads with black heads <note>a vivid historical description of comedones or blackheads</note>.

*Assala* is the name for the nutmeg <note>original: *nux myristica*</note>.

*Assaliæ* are worms born in wood or between planks, otherwise called shipworms <note>original: *teredones*</note>. *Aselli* [Little Donkeys] are Woodlice <note>original: *Millepedes*</note>.

*Asthma* is a disease of the lung, or pneumonia <note>original: *peripneumonia*</note>, which causes heavy sighing.

*Astrum* [Star] is used in this context to mean the virtue and power of things acquired through chemical preparations. For example, the *star of sulfur* is its ignition, by which it is turned into a most excellent oil. Likewise, the *star of salt* is its resolution into water or oil, through which it takes on greater powers than it had previously held. Similarly, the *star of mercury* is its sublimation, by which it acquires a wonderful strength and power, both greater and more subtle than its natural state.

*Athanor*, or *athanar*, is a furnace in the Spagyric <note>alchemical</note> art, most often a reverberatory one <note>a furnace that reflects heat back onto the substance being heated</note>, though sometimes adapted for other uses according to the judgment of the craftsman. See more in Ruland <note>Martin Ruland the Elder’s *Lexicon Alchemiae*</note>, pages 76 to 78.

*Atramentum* [Ink or Vitriol] signifies various things depending on what is added to it. For instance, *shoemaker’s ink* is copperas <note>ferrous sulfate</note>, internally a red vitriol <note>original: *calcanthum*</note>; *writing ink* is suited for the pen; *sooty ink* is for the printer. There are also white, green, and blue varieties, as well as that distilled from green vitriol, which they call *Roman*, or from any other kind. Ruland lists various species of *atramenti* on pages 78 to 80.

*Atractiva* [Attractives] are medicines, otherwise called magnetic, which have the power of drawing things to themselves, being composed of similar attractive substances.

*Augurista* is the name for a superstitious craftsman, who [his art...]

# CHEMICAL DICTIONARY

...exercises his art through mirrors, crystals, and in waters; especially in the song and flight of birds, so that he might prophesy from them. He also observes the rituals of others for the same reason. <note>This sentence appears to be the conclusion of a definition for a type of divination, likely *Augury* or *Hydromancy*, which began on the previous page.</note>

*Aurum potabile* [Potable Gold] is a liquor of gold without any corrosive, which very few truly understand—even among those who prepare it daily, often more to the ruin of men than to their health.

*Aurum vitæ* [Gold of Life] is gold that has been precipitated and reverberated <note>heated in a furnace that reflects the flame back onto the material to increase its "potency"</note> into a likeness of the deepest red of ground cinnabar. It is a most excellent substance produced and precipitated from its own mercury.

*Austromantia* [Austromancy] is another type of invented superstition based on observations of the winds; for instance, when the "stars of the winds" break out into a violence that is extreme and beyond the norm, idle men, more curious than wise, pronounce prophecies of the future from it.

*Axungia* [Fat or Grease], specifically "of mummy"—which is sometimes found written as "mummy of the marrow"—is the marrow of bones.

*Azoth* is quicksilver <note>mercury</note> extracted from any metallic body, and specifically refers to "corporeal mercury." To Paracelsus especially, it is a universal and neutral medicine, stripped of the appearance of all specific things and clothed in the most intense power and a certain general, central virtue. It contains within itself all other medicines, just as the "first matter" <note>original: *prima* [materia]; the fundamental substance from which all things are made</note> contains all other substances, excluding their accidental qualities. He is said to have carried this with him wherever he went, hidden within the pommel of his sword <note>a famous legend claiming Paracelsus kept his secret medicine or a familiar spirit in the hollow hilt of his sword</note>.

*Atramentum sutorium* [Shoemaker's Blacking] is vitriol, which is to say, chalcanthum <note>original: *Calcanthum*; copper or iron sulfate</note>. *See Ruland, pages 80 to 86.*

*Aurum* [Gold] is called the *Sol* [the Sun] by <term>Chymists</term> <note>practitioners of alchemy and early chemistry</note> and is attributed to the Sun. It is a metallic body: yellow, shining, and heavy, which has been equally "digested" <note>matured or 'cooked' by the earth's natural internal heat</note> in the belly of the earth and washed for a very long time by mineral water. It is formed from pure, fixed, clear, red quicksilver and from clean, fixed, red sulfur that is not

<meta>This page continues the entries under "A" in the alchemical dictionary, specifically concluding the detailed entry for Gold (Aurum), which follows the previous page's discussion of potable gold and the nature of mercury.</meta>

# CHEMICAL DICTIONARY

...by a non-burning fire. Gold is a most subtle substance of quicksilver <note>mercury</note>. For we see that quicksilver easily receives gold because it is closest to its own nature, just as a mother receives her son. Furthermore, gold consists of a small portion of the substance of pure sulfur and of pure redness; however, it possesses more quicksilver than sulfur—it takes more from the mother than from the father. When the father, the purest sulfur, unites with the mother, quicksilver, he generates the best son: gold. Briefly, gold is quicksilver coagulated by sulfur in a pure, yet non-scorching, fire. This is the "chosen son" which nature always intends to produce and toward which she always strives. It is the most balanced of all metals; they say it is hot and dry in the second degree, and fixed and red in the third degree.

->*Gold is twofold: 1. Native and pure. 2. Cooked [Processed].*<-

1. Native and pure, which is such by nature. The *Spaniards* call its masses *Palucas* <note>large gold nuggets</note>. It is found in rivers, such as the *Tagus, Elbe, Saale,* and *Schwarza*. 2. In the *Arabian* mountains, in mines and pits; its sparks sometimes cling to a type of very white stone called *Quartz* by the *Germans*. 3. In the small heads of fish which we call *Forellas* <note>Trout; medieval naturalists believed gold could be found in the heads or scales of certain fish</note>.

2. Cooked, which is smelted or boiled. 1. From pyrite of an ashen color, and galena <gloss>which is a vein of lead and silver together</gloss>. 2. From a certain purple earth, so tempered and influenced by the vapor and breath of the earth that it is fertile with gold, from which gold is smelted out in a furnace. 3. From Chrysocolla <note>term: *Chrysocolla*—literally "gold-glue," usually referring to malachite or copper minerals used in soldering</note>. 4. From *caeruleum* <note>original: "caeruleo"—a blue mineral, likely azurite or lapis lazuli</note>. The region contributes much to the quality of the gold. *Arabian, Spanish, Hungarian,* and *German* gold differ by certain fixed degrees; you may find more in *Ruland*, pages 92 to 94 <note>Refers to Martin Ruland the Elder’s *Lexicon Alchemiae* (1612)</note>.

->B.<-

**Iron rod**, is a forged instrument used for supporting vessels that have been placed [over a heat source].
***Bayda***, is a vessel over which distillation is performed.
***Bagedia***, is a pound of 12 ounces. ***Berdadia***, is also a Pound.
***Baiac***, is Ceruse <term>white lead, a common alchemical and pigment base</term>. ***Balziam***, is a bean.
***Balas*** <note>original: *Balafius*; likely the Balas ruby or spinel</note>, shines and glows with a purple or rosy color. It is called "gentle" by some; others believe it to be a Carbuncle <term>a deep red gemstone, usually a ruby or garnet</term> of diminished color and power, in the same way that the female power differs from the male power. It has often been observed that the outer part of a single stone shows as a Balas, while the interior is a Carbuncle, and for this reason they say the Balas is the "house" of the Carbuncle. It drives away various [disturbing] thoughts and restrains lust; it reconciles the disagreements of friends and brings health to the human body. When taken as a drink with water, it heals infirmities of the eyes and relieves pains of the liver.
***Balitiftera***, is Red Earth.
***Bread of Barach*** <note>original: *Barach panis*</note>, is the niter of salt <note>likely saltpeter or a form of soda</note>.
***Barcata***, is a path of fire.
***Barna***, is a glazed vessel.
***Basura***, is a seed. ***Baul***, is Urine.
**Hammering of the branch** <note>original: *Batitura rami*</note>, is the scale of Venus <term>copper flakes or oxides produced during forging</term>. **Hammering of copper**, is the scale of Metals.
***Baurac***, is rock salt, or sapphire-colored litharge <term>lead oxide</term> that has been whitened; it is also the salt of glass, the dross of glass, the foam of glass, or the gall of glass <note>the scum that forms on the surface of molten glass</note>: the shell of quicksilver <note>mercury</note>.
***Baurac***, is also *attinchar* <note>borax</note>, or any kind of salty substance.
***Bezar***, or ***Besar***, a precious green stone <note>The Bezoar, a stone-like mass found in the stomachs of animals, highly prized as a universal antidote</note>.

# CHEMICAL DICTIONARY

*Bianchetunsim pythium* is Ceruse <term>white lead, a common alchemical base and pigment</term>. *Biladen* is Steel.

**Bitumen** is a sulfurous earth or a fatty juice; there are various kinds of bitumens:
1. A liquid variety similar to oil, called petroleum.
2. A fragrant liquid from Saxony.
3. A fragrant variety of natural camphor.
4. A thick black variety which takes its name from its pitch-like color, originating from the Dead Sea <note>original: *Sodomæo*</note>, which the Moors use to fill corpses <note>a reference to ancient Egyptian mummification practices</note>. A thick black variety from Palestine.
5. A mineral variety similar to coal.

*Bodid* is an egg.

*Bof* is Quicklime <term>calcium oxide, a caustic substance produced by heating limestone or shells</term>.

**Bolus** is a specific coagulum <term>a thickened or curdled mass</term>, which is a chemical essential. It consists of a certain essence freed from impurities and foreign matter, separated and established through the "knot" of coagulation (which must necessarily be preceded by solution). It is formed in the manner that washed boluses <note>medicinal clays or earths</note> are typically made, so that they have a consistent and uniform shape, like that of a clay clod—and sometimes even in the form of nine tablets, though that matters little to the essence itself.

Ruland <note>Martin Ruland the Elder (1532–1602), a physician known for his alchemical dictionary</note> identifies six species, of which the true Armenian [bolus] is held in great value by the Turkish Emperors, who use it against fevers, the plague, and quinsy <term>an abscess of the throat</term>, page 105.

*Bonati* means glazed.

*Borades* is Filings <note>usually metal filings</note>.

**Borax** is Chrysocolla <term>literally "gold-glue," a flux used in soldering</term>. It is also called *Batrachium* and natural *attinchar* <note>from the Arabic *al-tinkar*</note>.

**Borax** is *aphronitrum* <term>the "froth of niter," or flower of saltpeter</term>, *attinchar*, or niter. It is called the "Halter of Gold" <note>original: *Capistrum auri*; so named because it "binds" or fluxes gold during the soldering process</note>.

*Botamum* is washed lead <note>The OCR reads *votum* (vowed), but in this context, it is almost certainly *lotum* (washed)</note>.

*Botus barbatus* is a vessel placed upon another vessel, in which gold is melted.

**Botus barbatus**

(They are mistaken who call it *Chelonites* <term>the "turtle-stone," usually a fossilized shell</term>, which I have described under its own heading.) Princes and noblemen ought to carry this stone with them by right; for if poisons are hidden within a man, or if they are brought near him from the outside, the stone itself changes color and emits small droplets as if it were sweating. According to *Ernestus Burgravius* <note>a 17th-century physician and writer on "magnetic" or sympathetic medicine</note>.
**Buffali**, a ring fashioned from the horn [of a buffalo] and worn on the ring finger, is said to control the contractions of a spasm. *Burgravius*.
**Balneum Mariæ** (the Bath of Mary), or "the bath of the sea" <note>original: *balneum maris*</note> as many prefer to call it, is a distilling furnace containing water, in which chemical vessels are placed while the water is hot. This is done for the putrefaction <term>the breaking down of a substance through gentle heat</term> of the material they contain, for separation, and for that [type...]

<meta>This page continues the alphabetical "B" entries from page 37, finishing a definition of chemical baths before moving into various types of balsams, metallurgical mixtures, and minerals.</meta>

### CHEMICAL DICTIONARY

...a kind of moist ascensions <note>original: *humidarum ascentionum*; referring to processes like distillation or steaming</note> for completing operations.

*The **Balneum roris** <note>original: "Bath of Dew"</note>, or "of dews," is a furnace in which the distillation vessels are suspended over the vapor of water only, so that they do not touch the body of the water; we have called it by another name: the "vaporous bath" or **vaporarium** <term>a steam-chamber used for gentle, indirect heating</term>.*

***Balsamum*** <note>Balsam</note> *is the substance of bodies which preserves them from putrefaction <term>decay or rotting</term>. It is otherwise written by some as **Balsamus**. It is both internal and external. The internal balsam in the human body is a certain most temperate substance, neither bitter, nor sweet, nor harsh; it is not a mineral salt, but a "salt of liquor" <note>original: *sal liquoris*; a Paracelsian term for the life-sustaining fluids of the body</note>, which most powerfully preserves human bodies from putrefaction. It is also called the most temperate "gluten" <note>glue or binding agent</note> of the body's nature. It is defined more briefly thus: It is the liquor of the interior salt, naturally preserving its own body most safely from corruption. The external balsam, however, is called **terebinthina** <note>Turpentine</note> by Paracelsus; it has suffered no violence of fire, but is digested <term>subjected to gentle, steady heat over time</term>. Likewise, it is everything that heals quickly; the name is adapted to surgery from the German language from two words, **baldzamen** <note>original German: *bald zusammen*, meaning "quickly together"</note>, that is, "quickly joined." It is also called an oil distilled from any body and brought to the highest degree of purity. **Bele son** is also Balsam.*

***Balsamus de mumiis*** <note>Balsam of mummies</note> *is a balsam drawn from flesh.*

***Balsamus elementorum externus*** <note>External balsam of the elements</note> *is the liquor of external mercury, otherwise known as the **mumia** of the external elements, and properly the firmamental <note>celestial or heavenly</note> essence of things, which some also call the "fifth essence" <term>the quintessence, or the purest form of a substance</term>.*

***Blachmal*** *is a material confused into one by the melting of several metals and cast into sulfur.*

***Barnabas***, *or rather **barnaas**, is a urinous salt-petre <note>potassium nitrate derived from urine</note>, which is also called the sharpest vinegar.*

***Basiliscus minor*** <note>Lesser Basilisk</note> *is a stellion <note>a type of spotted lizard, historically believed to have medicinal or magical properties</note>.*

***Berillus*** <note>Beryl</note> *is a mirror made of crystal, superstitiously* <insert>used</insert> <unclear>...</unclear>

### CHEMICAL DICTIONARY

...consecrated by the augurs <note>original: *auguristis*; ancient Roman diviners who interpreted the will of the gods</note>. *Rulandus* <note>referring to Martin Ruland the Elder, author of a 1612 alchemical dictionary</note> on pages 101 and 102 states there are eight species of **Beryl**.

**Bruta** is the influence of celestial virtue that is revealed to rational beings through "brute" animals, such as in the discovery of **celandine** <note>original: *chelidonia*; from the Greek *chelidon*, swallow</note> through the swallow <note>it was believed swallows used the herb to heal their chicks' eyes</note>, or the use of the **clyster** <term>enema</term> learned from the stork using salt water, and many other things of that kind.

**Butter of Saturn** <note>original: *Butyrum Saturni*</note> is what was previously called **altey**, as well as the "sweetness of lead" <note>likely lead acetate, which has a sweet taste but is highly toxic</note>.

**Blas of the Heart** <note>original: *Blas Cordis*; a term coined by van Helmont or Paracelsus for a vital, pulsating force</note> is the vital spirit, and consequently the fuel of its own heat: the spirit thus animated, and...

# C.

**Cabala**, **Cabalia**, or the **Cabalistic Art**, is the most secret science, which is said to have been divinely handed down together with the law of Moses. It revealed to us the doctrine of God concerning the Messiah, forms friendships with Angels for its practitioners, and transmits knowledge of all natural things. It illuminates the mind with divine light once the darkness has been driven away. The word is Hebrew; in Latin, it is called **Reception** <note>original: *Receptio*</note>, because Moses received divinely on the Mountain—along with the law that he later gave in writing to the people—a more secret and true explanation of the law, which it was forbidden to write down. Instead, through ordinary successions of revelations, one would receive it from another, as if by hereditary right, lest the divine mysteries become cheapened or violated if known by the profane multitude. The Persians were the most diligent practitioners of this and called their wise men "Magi" <term>the root of the word 'magician,' though here meaning wise sages or priests</term>, such as those three who came from the East to Bethlehem to worship Christ, and who were not kings as the common people have thought until now. Concerning this, Reuchlin the German <note>Johannes Reuchlin, a humanist who introduced Greek and Hebrew studies to Germany</note>, and Pico, the Italian lord of Mirandola <note>Giovanni Pico della Mirandola, a famous Renaissance philosopher</note>, and Pietro Galatino have written most extensively. Galatino affirms that the ancient Rabbis, through this science of the Cabala, recognized the Trinity and Christ the Son of God. Therefore, it is not a foolish art in the opinion of many, but rather a certain and celestial science, through which Theophrastus <note>referring to the famous alchemist and physician Paracelsus</note>, as he writes of himself, completed his doctrine by the grace of God; nor by [spirits...]

<meta>image quality: good; catchword: [spiriti]bus (spirits)</meta>

<summary>This page concludes the letter 'B' with terms from the chemist Van Helmont and begins the 'C' section with a lengthy definition of the Cabala (Kabbalah). It frames the Cabala as a divine, secret science passed from Moses to Renaissance thinkers like Paracelsus and Pico della Mirandola.</summary>

### CHEMICAL DICTIONARY. 41

...[with evil] things; for which reason he is falsely burdened with this slander by the envious. It is admitted that this science <note>referring to the Cabala mentioned on the previous page</note> has fallen into abuse among many, just as other sciences have. For **Theophrastus** <note>Theophrastus von Hohenheim, better known as Paracelsus (1493–1541), a famous Swiss physician and alchemist</note> writes that the **Hebrews** degenerated in their knowledge of God, and so too in this science. Therefore, the Cabala was later divided into a true science and a false art. There also arose a literal version which is rightly numbered among the uncertain arts, especially if it does not proceed from the Hebrew source. For the true Cabala has its foundation in Holy Scripture.

**Theophrastus** wrote much about this science in his books on *Sagacious Philosophy* <note>original: *Philosophia sagaci*, also known as the *Astronomia Magna*</note>; nor do I doubt that with the passage of time, foreign nations will judge **Theophrastus** more sincerely than certain Germans do now. For it is clearer than light itself from his own monuments <note>his written works</note> that he was a supreme philosopher and an incomparable physician: and one who denied no article of the Christian faith, even if he interpreted certain passages of Holy Scripture differently than is customary (with certain very learned men of more recent times agreeing with him). But concerning Christ the Savior, he taught many excellent things, as will be evident from his own books.

### CHEMICAL DICTIONARY. 42

**Cal**: **Yellow arsenic** <note>original: *Arsenicum citrinum*, likely orpiment</note>. Also **vinegar** <note>original: *acetum*</note>.

**Calamina**: This is **calamine stone** <note>an ore of zinc, historically used for making brass</note>.

**Calcadis**: This is **white vitriol** <note>zinc sulfate</note>. At other times it refers to **alkali salt**.

**Calchanthos**, or **Calchanthum**, signifies:
1. **Flower of copper** <note>original: *florem æris*, often meaning verdigris or copper oxide</note>.
2. **Chalcitis**, which is a species of **vitriol** <note>metallic sulfates</note>.
3. **Vitriol**.
4. **Burnt copper** <note>original: *æs ustum*</note>, or stony copper.
5. **Green vitriol** <note>iron(II) sulfate</note>. **Calchanthum** and **Colcothar** <note>the reddish residue left after the distillation of vitriol, essentially iron oxide</note> are generated and nourished by grains of black pigment <note>original: *atramenti*</note>. When the salt is dissolved, they themselves are dissolved, a quality found in **Venus** <note>the alchemical name for copper</note>. They freeze in the cold; Venus participates in certain minerals found within the mines. If redness is present, wateriness abounds; if greenness is present, air prevails. *See pages 123 and 124 of **Ruland** for more on Calchanthos.* <note>Refers to Martin Ruland the Elder’s *Lexicon Alchemiae* (1612), a standard reference for alchemical terms</note>

**Calchantum**, **Calcadis**: This is a species of black pigment <note>original: *atramenti*</note>.

**Calcaton**: These are **troches of arsenic** <note>medicinal lozenges or cakes containing arsenic</note>.

**Calchithios**: This is the **rust of copper** <note>verdigris</note>. Also **marcasite** <note>an iron sulfide mineral similar to pyrite</note>.

**Calcata**: **Yellow ink** <note>original: *Atramentum citrinum*</note>.

**Calcatar**, **Calcotar**, **Calcadinum**: This is **red ink** <note>original: *Atramentum rubeum*</note> or **vitriol**.

**Caldar**: This is **tin** <note>original: *stannum*</note>.

**Calcocos**: This is **copper** <note>original: *æs*</note>. **Calcithos** is **green copper**. **Calocamenon** is **burnt copper**.

**Calcitari**: This is **alkali salt**. **Alkael**. **Cali** is **potash** <note>original: *cinis clavellatus*, literally "ash from small wooden plugs"</note>.

**Calcidicum**: This is a **medicine made from arsenic**.

**Calmet**, **Cosmec**: This is **antimony**. **Cosmet**, **Cusme**.

**Calusaz**: This is **Indian oil** <note>likely a specific aromatic or medicinal oil imported from the East</note>.

**Calusa**, **Cyptas**: This is **crystal**.

**Natural Heat**: This occurs when solar rays either cook the material applied to them, or when they are collected in a **concave mirror** <note>original: *specul. concavo*</note> and applied to the object. The use of this "fire" among craftsmen is to dissolve and **calcine** <note>to heat a substance to high temperatures to drive off volatile matter or cause oxidation</note>.
