--- Page 126 ---
<meta>This page continues from the previous entry, likely defining "Elevation" (elevatio) or "Distillation," concluding the general description of how substances are separated before moving into the specific definition of Sublimation.</meta>

->## CHEMICAL DICTIONARY. ##<-

->### 86 ###<-

...from bodies, the subtle from the gross, and the non-fixed from the fixed, are raised like smoke or fire, and are condensed at the top of the head <note>the "cap" or "head" of the distilling apparatus</note>. This process is of two kinds.

**Dry Elevation**, (commonly called **sublimation**) is the process by which the dryer and more subtle parts are raised, adhering to the walls of their vessel. This is performed in the following manner:

I take some material to be sublimated, and I enclose it—either alone or mixed with other materials—in a glass or earthenware vessel that is wide or round at the bottom. I fill it such that two parts remain empty and the third part is filled. Upon this vessel, or **cucurbit** <term>cucurbit: a gourd-shaped chemical vessel used for boiling or distilling</term>, I place a "blind" **alembic** <term>alembic: the cap of a distilling apparatus; "blind" means it lacks a drainage spout</term> pierced with a small hole at the top. I coat the joints with **lute** <note>original: *luto*; a paste used to seal chemical apparatus airtight</note> and fasten it firmly so that the spirits or sublimated parts cannot escape. 

Then, I place it in a **sand bath** <note>original: *catinum arenarium*; a container of heated sand used to provide even, gradual heat</note> with three fingers of sand between the bottom and the walls, or I place it over an open fire, and I increase the heat by degrees. In the first degree of heat, it is necessary for all moisture to depart; I easily test this by placing a smooth iron plate over the glass. If any moisture still remains, it clings to the plate, but when all moisture has vanished, the "dry spirits" begin to ascend. At this point, I close the hole of the alembic with a piece of glass, well-sealed with good lute so the spirits do not escape. Finally, I maintain the fire until all the material to be sublimated has been raised. Some practitioners, instead of a cucurbit, use a dish or an earthenware shell to hold the material, with a glass cone properly fitted to the walls of the lower vessel.

**For example:** I take 2 pounds of the best-purified **yellow sulfur** <note>original: *sulphuris citrini*</note>, 5 pounds of common salt, and a little lightly calcined **Hungarian vitriol** <note>original: *vitrioli Hungarici*; a mineral salt, usually iron or copper sulfate</note>. Having powdered each and mixed them together, I place them in the cucurbit. After placing the blind alembic (pierced at the top) over it, I light a fire of the first degree until the moisture has vanished. Then, having closed the opening, I proceed by degrees of heat until all the more subtle sulfur has ascended. This sulfur (called by Chemists **flowers** <note>original: *flores*; the fine, powdered sublimate that forms on the vessel walls</note> of sulfur...

...called of sulphur) taken out of the **alembic** <term>alembic: the cap or head of a distilling apparatus</term> a second or third time. **Moist Elevation**, (commonly called **distillation**) is the refinement of moist parts, thinned into vapors, away from the dry and dense parts. It is either straight or oblique.

**Elevation through the side** occurs when the liquid is drawn out from a vessel tilted to the side.

**Elevation by retort** occurs when the liquid is driven out from a **retort** <term>retort: a glass vessel with a long, curved neck pointing downward, used for distilling substances that require high heat</term>. Both methods have their use in matters involving heavier and more tenacious spirits.

**Elidrium** is Mastic gum. **Elidrion** is Mercury.

**Eloanx** is **orpiment** <term>orpiment: a deep yellow arsenic sulfide mineral formerly used as a pigment and in alchemy</term>. **Elome** is orpiment.

**Elopitinum** is Tragacanth Vitriol <note>original: *Dragantum Vitriolum*; likely a specific preparation of vitriol mixed with gum tragacanth</note>.

**Elas maris** is burnt lead.

**Elis** is the dross of silver <note>original: *scoria argenti*; the slag or waste material left after smelting silver</note>.

**Elix** or **Elzimar** is the flower of copper <note>original: *flos æris*; likely referring to verdigris or copper oxide</note>.

**Ema** (from the Greek *haima* <note>original: αἷμα</note>) is Blood.

**Hematite** <note>original: *Ematites*</note> is bloodstone.

**Embula** is a reed or pipe.

**First Essence** <note>original: *Ens primum*</note> is an extract of minerals which have not yet reached their final perfection and are more fertile in their seminal power. It is usually called **prime matter**, which exists in every genus and possesses a power closest to it, from which the first natural motion toward a substance arises. This First Essence is sought primarily from minerals. For here, in the case of metals and gems, they are found empty of their actual form but fertile in potential. In vegetables, it is sought from plants while they are still in the green herb or in immature fruits. --- Page 128 ---
<meta>This page (86) provides the technical definition and procedural instructions for sublimation, which continues directly into the text provided for page 87. It describes the physical process of separating "subtle" parts from "gross" parts using heat.</meta>

-># CHEMICAL DICTIONARY. 86 #<-

...from the bodily parts, the subtle are raised from the coarse, and the non-fixed from the fixed, like smoke or fire, and are condensed at the top of the cap. It is of two kinds.

**Dry Elevation**, (commonly called **sublimation**) is that by which the subtler dry parts are raised, adhering to their vessel. It is carried out in the following manner.

I take some material to be sublimated, and I enclose it either alone or mixed with other substances in a glass or earthenware vessel, wide or round at the bottom, so that two parts are empty and the third is filled. On this vessel, or **cucurbit** <term>cucurbit: the lower, gourd-shaped part of a distilling apparatus containing the material to be heated</term>, I place a "blind" **alembic** <term>alembic: the cap or head of a distilling apparatus; "blind" implies it lacks a side-spout</term> perforated at the top with a small hole; I coat it with lute <note>original: *luto*; a clay or cement used to seal joints and prevent the escape of vapors</note> and secure it so that the spirits or sublimated parts cannot escape. Then I place it in a sand bath <note>original: *catinum arenarium*; a container of sand used to distribute heat evenly around a glass vessel</note> with three finger-widths of sand between the bottom and the walls, or in an open fire, and I increase the heat by degrees. In the first degree, it is necessary that all moisture departs, which I easily test by placing a smooth iron plate over the glass. For when some moisture still remains, it sticks to the plate, but when all moisture has departed, the dry spirits ascend, and hence I close the hole of the alembic with some matching glass covered with good lute, so that the spirits do not breathe out. Finally, I maintain the fire until all the material to be sublimated has been raised. Some use, in place of the cucurbit, a small dish or some earthenware shell containing the material, with a glass cone properly fitted to the walls of the lower vessel. *For example:* I take 2 pounds of yellow sulphur <note>original: *sulphuris citrini*</note> that has been very well purified, and 5 pounds of common salt and **Hungarian vitriol** <term>Hungarian vitriol: copper sulfate, traditionally sourced from Hungarian mines</term> which has been slightly calcined <note>original: *calcinati*; heated to a high temperature to drive off volatile matter</note>. I powder each of them, mix them together, and place them in the cucurbit. Having placed the blind alembic (perforated at the top) upon it, I light a fire of the first degree until the moisture has vanished; then, with the orifice closed, I proceed step by step until all the subtler sulphur has ascended. This sulphur (called by **chemists** <note>original: *Chymicis*</note> **flowers of sulphur**...

<sig>G 2</sig>
...of sulfur, as it is called) removed from the alembic for a second or third time, always adding a fourth part of salt and vitriol <term>vitriol: a sulfate mineral, usually iron or copper sulfate</term> restored through sublimation, I rectify <term>rectify: to purify a substance through repeated distillation or sublimation</term> it, and I receive the most beautiful flowers of sulfur <term>flowers of sulfur: a fine yellow powder produced when sulfur vapor is condensed</term>, which are resistant to many ailments.

**Wet Elevation**, (commonly called **distillation**) is the refining of moist parts, thinned into vapors, from the dry and coarse parts. It is either direct or oblique.

**Elevation through the side** <note>original: *Elevatio per latus*</note> occurs when the liquid is drawn out from a vessel tilted to the side.

**Elevation through a retort**, occurs when the liquid is driven out from a retort <term>retort: a glass vessel with a long, downward-pointing neck used for distilling and separating substances</term>. Both methods have their uses for substances that yield heavier and more persistent spirits.

*Elidrium* is mastic gum <note>original: *gummi Mastix*; a resin obtained from the mastic tree, used in medicines and varnishes</note>. *Elidrion* is Mercury.

*Eloanx* is orpiment <term>orpiment: a deep yellow arsenic sulfide mineral formerly used as a pigment and in alchemy</term>. *Elome* is orpiment.

*Elopitinum* is Tragacanth Vitriol <note>original: *Dragantum Vitriolum*; likely a specific preparation or mixture of vitriol with gum tragacanth</note>.

*Elas maris* is burnt lead.

*Elpis* is silver dross <note>original: *scoria argenti*; the stony waste matter separated from metals during smelting</note>.

*Elix* and *Elzimar* are "flower of copper" <term>flower of copper: the oxide or patina that forms on copper, often used in medicinal recipes</term>.

*Ema*, from the Greek <note>original: *αἷμα* (haima)</note>, is Blood.

*Ematites* is bloodstone <term>hematite: an iron oxide mineral known for its reddish-brown color, believed to stop bleeding</term>.

*Embula* is a reed or pipe.

**First Essence** <note>original: *Ens primum*</note> is an extract of minerals that have not yet reached their final perfection and are more fertile in their "seminal" power <note>Alchemists believed metals "grew" in the earth from seeds or primal vapors; the First Essence captures the plant or mineral at this high-energy, "growing" stage</note>. It is usually called the **prime matter** <note>original: *materia prima*</note>, which belongs to every genus and possesses the power closest to that from which the first natural movement toward substance arises. The First Essence is sought primarily from minerals. For here, the [essences] of metals and gems are found, though actually empty of them, yet fertile in potential. In vegetables, it is sought from plants while they are still in the herb stage or are immature fruits. Ani-

<meta>This page continues the alphabetical definitions of chemical and alchemical terms, specifically those beginning with the letter 'E'. It follows the previous page's discussion of sulfur sublimation and various types of distillation (elevation), moving into definitions of essences and specific mineral salts.</meta>

-># CHEMICAL DICTIONARY. #<-

The class of animals does not provide many substances, unless you wish to prepare it from blood and eggs. Likewise, the spawn of frogs and similar creatures are called to this use.

***Epar*** is air, or ether. <note>original: *Epar*; usually the Greek term for liver, but here defined cosmologically.</note> ***Epatum*** is Liver-colored Aloe <term>Epaticum: a variety of aloe resin used in medicine, named for its dark, liver-like color</term>.

***Epipolasis*** occurs when a sublimated substance <term>sublimation: the process where a solid turns into vapor and then back to solid, used to purify chemicals</term> rises only to the surface and sits upon it. First, the essences are extracted from the center to the surface, but sometimes this same operation serves for purification. It is of two kinds: wet and dry.

***Wet Epipolasis*** is when, from a substance placed in an excess of liquid, the sublimated part floats and emerges all the way to the top, where it remains visible. Therefore, it can be called sublimation by floating or emergence. This is aided by the natural inclination toward lightness and the separation of different substances; it is brought about by heat, not only actual <note>meaning direct fire</note> but also potential <note>meaning the internal heat of a chemical reaction</note>.

***Dry Epipolasis*** is when the sublimated part "blooms" <note>original: *efflorescit*</note> directly from the substance itself and clings closely to it in a dry state. It is achieved in various ways by the work of elevating heat and by loosening the way for its exit through opened closures.

***Epostlinga*** is iron scale <term>iron scale: the flaky oxide that forms on the surface of iron when it is heated</term>.

***Esebon, Assebbon*** is common salt.

***Essence*** is a simple extract produced from things perfected in their whole nature. It is called an essence because, born and contained within elementary compartments, it is a perfect part of the mixture of the whole substance, provided with nature and essential reason and power. It can be made from every kind of mixture—mineral, vegetable, and animal—with great yield. Essence is of two kinds: juice and mystery.

The ***Fifth Essence*** <note>original: *Essentia quinta*; the Quintessence</note> is so called because it is something beyond the elements, not arising from their physical temperament <note>original: *crasi*; the specific mixing of the four qualities of hot, cold, wet, and dry</note>, but divine in its author and its effects.

<meta>This page continues the alphabetical "E" entries from the previous page, which concluded with a definition of iron scale (epostlinga). We move now from metallurgical residues into definitions of refinement (exaltation), extraction techniques, and the philosophical nature of the Elixir as defined by Paracelsus.</meta>

-># CHEMICAL DICTIONARY. #<-

*Ethesius* is a stone; it is Chrysolite <note>A yellowish-green gemstone, often historically confused with topaze or olivine</note>.

*Exaltation* is Sublimation. It is also an operation by which a substance, through the changing of its qualities, is led to a higher dignity of essence and power. Alternatively, it is a process of refining by gradually dissolving a substance, transposing it into a purer and greater degree of its own power; this is achieved through circulation <term>circulation: a process of continuous distillation where the vapor returns to the original liquid</term> or by washing.

*Exhalation* occurs when dry spirits <note>volatile components of a substance</note>, raised into the air by heat, depart. This occurs most effectively in dry, ground materials placed upon iron plates, flat-bottomed basins, or even the floors of kilns. With fire lit beneath, or applied around or above, they are roasted until the vapors are consumed, as occurs in metallic ores.

*Expression* is extraction by means of a press, by which a compressed substance pours out its matter in a liquid form. Its use is excellent for extracting juices and certain oils.

*Extraction* is a digestion from a bodily mass, dissolving the finer and purer parts which are captured by an added solvent <note>original: *menstruo*; a term used in alchemy for a liquid that dissolves a solid</note>, leaving the dregs behind. Alternatively, it is the segregation of an essence by which it is pulled from its body; however, this does not happen without a breaking of the whole and a tearing apart. Extractions are considered more "noble" than mere "distractions" <note>likely referring to violent or mechanical separations that do not preserve the essence</note>.

*Elephas* <note>literally "The Elephant"</note> is *aqua fortis* <note>original: *aqua fortis*; "strong water," the historical name for nitric acid</note>. In other contexts, it refers to leprosy, Elephantiasis, or the "Herculean disease" <note>often referring to epilepsy or severe skin conditions</note>.

*Elixir*, properly speaking, is a ferment, the smallest quantity of which binds together a huge mass of its own kind by penetrating and turning it into a paste similar to itself. For Paracelsus <note>Philippus Aureolus Theophrastus Bombastus von Hohenheim (1493–1541), a famous Swiss physician and alchemist</note>, the "paste" in this context is the spirit of human life and the internal balsam of life. The Elixir, however, is an external balsam sought out by art from external sources and prepared into a spagyric <term>spagyric: the alchemical process of separating a substance into its components and then recombining them in a purified state</term> ferment.

*Emunctorium* is a place for the expulsion of corrupt matter, through which it finds an exit, driven by a strong natural force supported by the power of external medicines.

*Entali* is split alum <term>alumen scissum: a fibrous form of alum, possibly asbestos or plume alum</term>, and sometimes it is derived from salt...

<meta>This page continues the alphabetical "E" entries from the previous page, finishing a description of a substance (likely an elixir or essence) fused through alchemical art before proceeding to definitions of mineral vapors, medical conditions like hives and necrotic tissue, and the fundamental Paracelsian concept of the Fifth Essence.</meta>

...of a gemstone fused by the spagyric art <note>The alchemical art of "separating and recombining" components to create medicine</note>. *Entale* is a vessel.

*Enur* is the hidden vapor of water from which stones are born.

*Erodinium* is a sign of some thing, or of a future history, which remains hidden from men for a time until it comes to pass; only then is it called "known" instead of "uncertain," and a more certain prophecy.

*Escara* [Eschar], or *esphara*, refers to black and dead flesh which is separated from healthy, living flesh by surgeons and falls away, caused by a cautery <note>a hot iron or caustic used to sear tissue</note> or corrosive medicine.

*Essare* [Hives] are small pustules like the blisters of the itch <note>scabies</note>, except they have protruding flesh, like the bumps caused by a nettle.

*Essential essatum* is the force, power, and virtue that resides within vegetables and minerals.

*Essatum wine* is distilled and rectified wine infused with herbs and similar substances to extract their essential virtues—such as wine of celandine, smartweed, lungwort, lemon balm, and others—intended for immortality <note>original: *asthanate*; a term referring to life-prolonging medicines or *athanasia*</note>.

*Quinta essentia* [Fifth Essence], according to the definition by Paracelsus, is a certain matter corporally extracted from all things that nature produced and which possess life; it is separated from all impurities, mortality, or corruption, and most subtly purified from all the elements. Concerning this law, he wrote more extensively in the *Archidoxis* <note>One of Paracelsus's most influential books, detailing the extraction of essences for healing</note>, wherein lies the medicine of all things.

*Essodinum* is a certain omen of a future event based on an indication, the opposite of the *Erodinium* defined above.

*Estiomenus* is a most corrupt abscess, devouring an entire limb like a "wolf" <note>likely referring to gangrene or *lupus*</note> or a biting herpes; it is a type of "holy fire" <note>Historically known as St. Anthony's Fire, often identified as erysipelas or ergotism</note>.

*Evestrum* is the perpetual [aspect] of the firmament within the fourfold world of elements. It is also a prophetic...

-># CHEMICAL DICTIONARY #<-

...a spirit which, through a sign or some preceding omen, foretells and predicts something of the future with certainty.

***Excrementa*** [Excrements], generally speaking, are all superfluous materials ejected by nature. The "excrements of women" refer to the menses <note>menstrual discharge</note>.

***Exitura*** [Abscess], generally, is every abscess or sore that discharges pus <note>original: *saniem*; a thin, blood-stained discharge from a wound</note>.

***Exorcista*** [Exorcist] is a superstitious practitioner who summons shades and spirits by incantations and causes them to appear.

***Extractum*** [Extract] is that which is drawn out from a physical mass, leaving behind the elemental dregs <note>original: *crassitie*; the thick, coarse matter</note>. It is, as it were, the Marrow and most noble part of the entire substance produced within the elements, and as if sown there; it is preserved in their womb and nourished toward the perfection of its own nobility. It has its root, to some extent, in the elements, from which arises a kinship of shared origin and sustenance. However, it depends principally on the power of God the Creator and is maintained in nature through the hidden influences of the heavens. For this reason, extracts—especially the more noble ones—are said to rival the ethereal and celestial nature. When well prepared and applied for human health, and with God’s blessing, they have great power in the medical art. And if these things are to be believed, some follow Plato in thinking that during the separation of celestial light from the dark earth, not every ethereal part departed; or at least, that something was later recovered from it during the birth of mixed bodies. This is proven by the way the celestial bodies act upon these lower things.

***Ezezic*** is salt. ***Elixir*** is a fermented medicine made from the seven metals <note>Gold, silver, mercury, copper, iron, tin, and lead</note>. *Paracelsus.*

-># CHEMICAL DICTIONARY #<-

***Fabiola*** is the flower of beans.

***Fatum*** [Fate], *imarmeni* <note>original: *imarmeni*; from the Greek *heimarmene*, referring to the destiny or "allotted portion" decreed by the stars</note>, is a certain perpetual and imperceptible influence originating from the stars of heaven, the sun, and the moon.

***Flagæ*** are spirits who know the hidden and secret things of men.

***Fel draconis*** [Dragon’s gall] is a name for quicksilver <term>mercury</term> derived from tin.

***Fel vitri*** [Glass gall] is the scum of glass. It is also called the salt of glass or the dregs of glass <note>original: *fæx vitri*; the impurities that float to the top during the glass-making process</note>.

***Fermentum*** [Ferment or Leaven] is a firm matter, much like a "fixed" substance, which prepares matter of its own nature to reach its own stable condition. Before this preparation, the matter does not possess this stability. For example, the leaven of bread is flour that has been firmed or consolidated into a paste; it transforms similar flour—which is loose and disconnected—into a new, fermented dough.

***Flagæ*** are also spirits that appear in a mirror, reflecting from objects.

***Falcanos*** is another name for **Arsenic**, commonly known as ***Auripigmentum*** <note>Orpiment; a golden-yellow mineral used as a pigment and in alchemy</note>.

***Fasdir*** or ***Safdir*** is **Jupiter** <note>In alchemical shorthand, Jupiter represents the metal Tin</note>.

***Faulex*** is **Steel** <note>original: *Chalybs*</note>.

***Fecla*** is the dregs <term>tartar</term> of wine or vinegar.

***Fæces candidæ*** [White dregs] refers to the red and saffron-colored waters <note>In alchemical symbolism, "white" can sometimes refer to a purified state or a specific stage in the process, even if the physical color is different</note>.

***Fedum*** is **Saffron**. ***Felilech*** or ***Faules*** is **Iron**.

***Fæmina*** [The Female] is **Sulphur** <note>In alchemical theory, Sulphur was often assigned the "feminine" or "masculine" role depending on the specific tradition; here it is the active, combustible principle</note>.

***Ferrum Indicum*** [Indian Iron] is the grade of metal that most closely precedes Steel.

***Ferrum*** [Iron] is a very bright metal, slightly reddish, and sharing in an impure whiteness; it is hard. If fixed, earthy sulphur is mixed with fixed, earthy quicksilver <term>mercury</term>, and both are not of a pure whiteness but rather a leaden color—and if the sulphur predominates—then iron is formed. In summary: if the mercury is porous, earthy, and unclean, and the sulphur is unclean, foul-smelling, and earthy, and both are of a fixed substance (likened to a diseased man coupling with a menstruating mother), iron is generated.

<meta>This page continues the alphabetical dictionary under the letter "F," focusing specifically on "Ferrum" (Iron) and the beginning of a detailed entry on "Fermentatio" (Fermentation). It follows previous definitions of chemical and spiritual terms.</meta>

-># CHEMICAL DICTIONARY #<-

**Iron** [original: *Ferrum*] is attributed to **Mars** by the Chymists. Iron is of two kinds: Native <note>natural ore</note> and Cast, or Smelted.

All major metals, when they are cooked <term>smelted</term>, have a residue—so we say.

| | **Residue** [original: *recrementum*] of: |
| :--- | :--- |
| | copper, |
| | silver, |
| | iron, |
| | lead. |

Concerning the species of iron, ferrous metals, and the rust of iron, consult **Dioscorides**, book 5, chapter 26 <note>Pedanius Dioscorides was a 1st-century Greek physician whose "De Materia Medica" was a standard reference for centuries</note>, regarding its powers. Iron rust has an excellent medicinal power for restricting, binding, and drying. It is hot and dry in the second degree. It is recorded that **Achilles** healed **Telephus** with this <note>According to Greek myth, Achilles used the scrapings of rust from his spear to heal a wound he had inflicted on King Telephus</note>. The residue of iron is called **scoria** <term>slag or dross</term> and "dung of iron," as well as the stone **sideritis**, although the magnet which attracts iron is also called by this name. **Ruland** <note>Martin Ruland the Elder, author of a famous alchemical lexicon published in 1612</note> enumerates many kinds of iron from page 207 to 211.

***Ferrugo*** is the slag of iron, or the "excrement" of iron.

***Fermentatio*** [Fermentation] is the exaltation <term>refinement</term> of a thing in its substance through the mixing of a ferment. When its power is distributed through the spirit, it penetrates the entire mass and changes it into its own matter. It was discovered for this purpose: so that from a symbolic, baser efficacy, a most noble substance of fermenting medicine might be made, reaching the peak of what nature allows. This process takes place most especially in metals, whose nature is prone to mutual transformation, and it takes its name from the imitation of a fermented mass <note>referring to how yeast/leaven works in dough</note>. Or, **Fermentation** is the incorporation of a ferment with the material to be fermented. For just as a little leaven can convert a large amount of flour into its own nature and dough, so too does a chemical ferment assimilate the substance to which it is joined. As the ferment is, so shall the fermented result be. The **Philosophers** <note>Alchemists</note> understand "ferment" to be a true body and a true matter which, when joined to its own **Mercury**, [transforms] it into its own nature—

-># CHEMICAL DICTIONARY #<-

...converts. Furthermore, they also desire the Stone itself to be the **ferment** of the perfected body. Since the Stone is so subtle that, when cast upon an imperfect body, it floats on the surface like oil and does not mix with it, there is a need for some body that shares its primary affinity <note>a "sympathetic" material</note> to receive it and introduce it into other bodies. For as soon as that body is influenced and fermented by the Stone, it immediately transforms into a **tincture** <term>a substance capable of transmuting or coloring metals</term>.

**White Ferment** [original: *Fermentum album*] is silver <note>The text specifies "Felda," likely a specific source or alchemical grade of silver</note>. **Ferment** is also the **Elixir**.

**Feru zegi** is the metal tin.

**Dregs** [original: *Fex*]: We understand this to mean the dregs of wine, as **Dioscorides** <note>Ancient Greek physician</note> teaches in book 5, chapter 72. He also adds the dregs of vinegar, and describes how it is burned until dry, like sea-foam <term>halcionium</term>, and in other manners.

**Son of Venus** [original: *Filius Veneris*] is **brass** <term>aurichalcum</term>.

**Filtration** [original: *Filtratio*] is the drawing off of a liquid through a filter. It is a chemical sieve, and can be called a straining or percolation. This is done primarily with liquids, through which the thin, watery parts pass, while the thick and oily parts are held back. The practice is as follows: **Filter paper** [original: *Carta emporetia*] is rolled or folded, or in its place, a shaggy cloth is sewn into the shape of a bag, or applied to a vessel such as a funnel, etc. The liquid poured in is allowed to drip little by little into a receiver; hence this operation is called **distillation through a filter**.

**Firex** is **Oil**.

**Firfir** is a **Red Color**.

**Firmament** [original: *Firmamentum*] is **Azure** <term>lazurium or lapis lazuli</term>.

**Fifarum** is a preparation of **sal ammoniac** <term>ammonium chloride</term>.

**Fistula** is a narrow, long, and calloused cavity or pipe <term>syrinx</term>.

**Fixation** [original: *Fixatio*] is the process of making stable a thing that flees the fire <term>is volatile</term>, so that it no longer—

# CHEMICAL DICTIONARY #

...does not flee, but remains fixed in it through the habituation of fire, whether this is achieved through calcination <term>reducing a substance to powder by heat</term>, or through a slow and long-lasting decoction <term>boiling</term>, or through frequent sublimation <term>turning a solid into vapor and back to solid</term>, or through frequently repeated solution and coagulation, or finally, whether it is perfected through the addition of a "fixed" substance.

->Coagulum.<-
<note>Latin for "curdle" or "rennet," used here to indicate the solidification process.</note>

**Flower** [original: *Flos*] is a lump extracted through sublimation. It must be raised and produced from the center and the lowest parts of the vessel so that it solidifies into a dry form at the top. The "flower" is the spiritual substance of a thing. **Turbith** <term>in alchemy, often a yellow precipitate of mercury</term>, however, is more earthy. Every "flower" is naturally volatile and spiritual, although by the skill of a master it can be fixed and sometimes brought to the nature of Turbith.

**Fluors** [original: *Fluores*] are stones from mines similar to gems, but less hard. Metal-workers call them "fluors" <note>modern fluorite</note> because they melt and flow in the heat of fire just as ice does in the sun.

**Fluors** are almost the opposite of those intermediate or distant metals because they dissolve into spirit and air. If **Theophrastus** <note>Theophrastus Paracelsus, the famous 16th-century Swiss physician and alchemist</note> had known them, he would have called them *fiery* <note>original Greek: ἐκπυρώδης - ekpyrodes</note>, because they are produced in the earth by flowing. **Ruland** <note>Martin Ruland the Elder, author of a standard alchemical lexicon of this era</note> lists various types of fluors from page 217 to page 222.

**Hearth** [original: *Focus*] is the place where lead is purified. In English, it is called a **Smelt-house**.

**Leaves of Datura** <note>referring to the Jimsonweed plant</note> are like golden leaves.

**Fom** is a sound or a voice.

**Fulmination** [original: *Fulminatio*] is a metallic refinement <term>gradatio</term> involving smelting to purity in a cupel <term>cineritium - a shallow vessel made of bone ash</term>; its completion is indicated by a flashing brightness. The process is named such because a flash occurs, similar to the strike of a lightning bolt <note>original: *fulmen*</note>. For...

<meta>This page continues the alphabetical dictionary under the letter "F," concluding a description of the "fulmination" or "lightning" of gold—the moment of its final purification. It then covers terms from "Fumigation" to "Fusion," including a detailed procedure for refining gold using antimony.</meta>

# CHEMICAL DICTIONARY #

...after the foreign substances have been consumed and separated (such as added fluors <term>fluxes used to aid melting</term>, lead, or joined minerals), a certain sulfurous cloud floats across the surface, from which a purple splendor afterwards shines out, which they are accustomed to call "lightning," "thunderbolt," or even "flower"; when this disappears, the remaining refined metal <term>regulus</term> is cooled.

**Fumigation** is a calcination <term>reducing a substance to powder by heat</term> performed by sharp and corrosive smoke. In various ways, the nobler metals are made brittle by the vapor <note>original: *afflatu*</note> of molten lead or mercury, and are afterwards reduced to powder by grinding with salt.

*Furogi* is a rooster <note>often used in alchemical allegory to represent certain stages of heat or specific substances</note>.

*Furnace* is the place in which fire is skillfully and conveniently adapted for treating chemical matter. It is either Open or Covered. For more on furnaces, see *Ruland* <note>Martin Ruland the Elder’s *Lexicon Alchemiae*</note> from pages 226 to 233, and also in *Glauber* <note>Johann Rudolf Glauber, a famous 17th-century chemist</note>.

*Fusion* is the process of melting through heat.

From Iron, copper can be drawn out, and from iron, lead. *Paracelsus, On the Nature of Things, page 460*.

*Fusion* through Antimony, by which we separate true gold not only from imperfect metals but even from silver itself; it is done in this way: I take gold mixed with other metals and mix in three parts of powdered antimony, placing it in a crucible over a stubborn fire in a draft furnace <note>original: *furnum ventosum*</note>. Afterwards, with the help (if desired) of a bellows, I pour the thoroughly melted mass into a heated cone-shaped mold <note>original: *pyramidem*</note> smeared with tallow. When the edges of the mold are struck with a hammer, the refined metal <term>regulus</term> containing the gold will settle at the tip, which I cut off with a hammer. I place the remaining antimony back into the crucible for even a third time and proceed in the aforementioned way, so that all the gold is pushed out of the antimony. Finally, I place each of these metallic buttons in an earthen cupel <note>original: *catillo terreo*</note> near the bellows, and having placed coals over them in the manner of a vault, I kindle the fire and apply the flame until all the antimony

<meta>catchword: eva- (evaporates)</meta>

### 97 ###

...having evaporated, pure and unadulterated gold remains, which immediately solidifies <term>coagulates</term>.

*Fulmen* <note>original: "Lightning"</note>, is purified silver.
*Fimus quinus* <note>original: "Five-fold manure"</note>, is a digestion performed using horse manure, ashes, or water.
*Filum Arsenicale* <note>original: "Arsenical Thread"</note>, is the philosopher's stone.
*Fistula*, is a disease <term>an abnormal tube-like passage in the body</term>.
*Ficus cutis* <note>original: "Skin-fig"</note>, is a wart growing on the skin of horses' legs; also a medicine suited for the womb <term>matrix</term>.
*Fido*, is a name for quicksilver <term>mercury</term>, and sometimes gold.
*Fida*, is silver, and sometimes gold. *Fidda*, is the Moon <term>the alchemical symbol for silver</term>.
*Filius unius diei* <note>original: "Son of one day"</note>, is the philosopher's stone. It is also the egg <note>referring to the "philosophical egg," the sealed glass vessel used in alchemical transformation</note>.
*Filum arsenicale* <note>original: "Arsenical thread"</note>, is sublimated arsenic.
*Flegmon* <note>original: "Phlegmon"</note>, a common abscess.
*Flos cheiri* <note>original: "Flower of Cheiri"</note>, is the essence of gold.
*Flos sectarum croce* <note>original: "Saffron-cut flower"</note>, some maintain these are flowers of saffron because of the addition of the word "saffron" <note>original: *croce*</note>, thinking it was an error made by those who did not accurately read Paracelsus’s handwriting, reading *e* for *ci*. However, I have discovered by rearranging the letters that it is the flower of nutmeg <term>mace</term>, as the word "anthos" <note>Greek: "flower"</note> denotes.
*Fœnix*, or *Phænix*, is the "Son of one day," as mentioned above. Others use it to mean the extract of the celandine flower.
*Fædula*, is a species of mushroom.
*Folia dauræ* <note>original: "Leaves of Gold"</note>, meaning golden leaves.
*Fons Philosophorum* <note>original: "Fountain of the Philosophers"</note>, is a name for the sea-bath <term>bain-marie, or water bath</term>.
*Formæ rerum* <note>original: "Forms of things"</note>. These are called the influences of the heavens, which any lower things receive from the higher ones; the hidden power, force, and virtue of each individual thing.
*Formicæ* <note>original: "Ants"</note>, warty or leek-like <note>original: *porralia*</note> abscesses.
*Fugile*, is gold found in certain regions; also an abscess, called a "parotis" <term>a tumor or swelling of the glands near the ear</term>.

### 98 ###

*Soot of metals* <note>original: "Fuligo metallorum"</note>, is properly called arsenic, but it is also used to refer to mercury. *White smoke* <note>original: "Fumus albus"</note>, is Mercury.

*River in this context* <note>original: "Flumen hoc loco"</note>, refers to the "flowers" of silver refined in a cupel <term>cupellation is a refining process where ores are treated under high heat to separate noble metals like gold and silver from base metals</term> during its purification with lead. From this, "to strike the metals with lightning" <note>original: "fulminare metalla"</note> means the same as to purify them, especially those metals which are called "perfect" <note>referring to gold and silver, which do not oxidize or corrode under heat</note>.

## G.

Gamathei are stones upon which celestial powers and heavenly constellations are imprinted. They are marked or engraved with wondrous characters, images, and figures. Sometimes it happens that these are seen naturally fashioned, having been dug out of mountains or found along the banks of waters.

*Hard ice* <note>original: "Glacies dura"</note>, is Crystal.

*Sword-craft* <note>original: "Gladialis"</note> is the art by which swords and blades are forged according to the course of the stars in the firmament; such blades cannot be resisted even by anvils. For this reason, it is otherwise called *incusma* <note>original: "incusma"; likely derived from "incus," the Latin word for anvil</note>.

*Gelion*, is a word used for a leaf <note>original: "folio"</note>.

*Geomancy* <note>original: "Geomantia"</note>, is a very well-known art and science of the earths. But in this context, it is said to occur when the stars of the earth reveal themselves openly to men, so that they may derive a prophecy or omen from them which then achieves its effect. It is also called the Astronomy of the Earth, which operates in two ways: either through astronomical calculations or through the use of points <note>referring to a divination method involving marking random points in sand or on paper</note>.

*Geluta*, is the carline thistle <note>original: "herba carlina"</note>.

*Tartarean gem* <note>original: "Gemma tartarea"</note>, is a name for a stone or "calculus" <note>a hard sediment or stone-like growth</note> generated from transparent and clear tartar.

*Genuia*, is a plant that degenerates from the transplantation of the parsnip.

*Gibar*, is a metallic medicine. *Gibum* is Cheese.

# CHEMICAL DICTIONARY #
### 99 ###

*Giants* <note>original: "Gigantes"</note>, are men elevated or tall beyond or outside the nature of man, or contrary to the laws of nature.

*Gilla*, or *Grillus*, according to Paracelsus <note>Philippus Aureolus Theophrastus Bombastus von Hohenheim (1493–1541), a Swiss physician and alchemist who pioneered the use of chemicals and minerals in medicine</note>, is vitriol <term>a sulfate mineral</term> that has naturally dissolved into water. Some believe such a liquid is offered by nature itself. There are those who use this name to refer to the water of *sal armoniac* <note>original: "salis armoniaci"; ammonium chloride</note>.

*Glue* <note>original: "Gluten"</note>, for Paracelsus, is *sinonia*—or as some prefer, *finovia* <note>modern term: synovia</note>—a viscous, white, and clear liquid found in all joints like the white of an egg. It is a transparent substance in which, if "tartar" <term>stony deposits or calcification</term> is generated due to the dissolved salts it contains, it sometimes coagulates; from this arises gout <note>original: "podagra"</note>, the place of which he asserts is its true home.

*Tenacity of glue* <note>original: "Glutinis tenacitas"</note>, is a mineral resin, much like the resin of the turpentine tree.

*Gluta*, refers to the strength and effectiveness of sticky pitch.

*Gnomes* <note>original: "Gnomi"</note>, are called little men or rather corporeal spirits living under the earth; otherwise called pygmies, scarcely a cubit <note>an ancient unit of length, roughly the distance from the elbow to the fingertips</note> in height.

*Greek magic* <note>original: "Græca magia"</note>, is a superstitious art invented—or at least cultivated—by the Greeks, dealing in vain visions. By this art, they made things appear which truly were not, and thereby snatched a great reputation for wisdom among the ignorant common folk. In truth, it was nothing other than a transformation of the eyesight through darkening or dulling, taking its starting point from human imaginations. The assent of these people, combined with unfaithful effort, was joined into a likeness by the architect of every lie <note>a reference to the Devil</note>. What does that wicked craftsman not strive for in vain men through this path?

*Gravus*, is a porphyry stone <note>a hard igneous rock with crystals embedded in a fine-grained groundmass</note>, the use of which is like marble in the spagyric art <term>alchemy, specifically the process of separating and recombining the components of substances</term>; it is particularly suitable for the spontaneous dissolution of things.

*Guarini*, are men who live by the influence of the heavens.

# CHEMICAL DICTIONARY #

### 100 ###

*Guma*, is quicksilver, or Mercury <note>original: "argentum vivum, Mercurius"</note>.

*Gumicula*, is valerian <note>original: "phu"; a medicinal herb used since antiquity as a sedative</note>.

*Guta rosacea*, is a redness, especially in the face, similar to the discoloration found at the origin of leprosy <note>original: "tinctura est in origine lepræ"; likely referring to rosacea or similar skin conditions</note>.

*Jet stone* <note>original: "Gagates lapis"</note>, is found in the sea and the Gagas river. It is formed, without doubt, from petroleum—that is, a refined naphtha or a natural liquid bitumen. This is just like the other species of jet, the "Thracian stone," which is made from black naphtha or bitumen when it drips or flows into the sea or into rivers near the sea; that liquid bitumen or naphtha is that same petroleum.

Jet, when heated, and amber <note>original: "succinum"</note>, attract threads and straws through motion <note>referring to static electricity</note>, just as a magnet attracts iron, or the *Sagda* gem <note>a legendary green stone described by Pliny the Elder</note> attracts wood. Both burn when set on fire. But amber does not burn in water, though it may flare up somewhat like oil due to the weakness of the resin from which it is formed. Jet, however, because it is made from bitumen, burns even more fiercely in water, and the waters are ignited by the power of the bitumen, just as they are restrained by oil. Amber (or *electrum*) is not bitumen, but rather the "tears" <term>the sap or resin</term> of poplar or even pine trees.

The stone is called jet <note>original: "gagates"</note> from a place and a river named Gagas in Cilicia <note>a region on the southern coast of modern-day Turkey</note>, near whose mouth or entrance to the sea, by the city of Plagiopolum, this stone is found. As Dioscorides <note>a Greek physician and botanist (c. 40–90 AD) whose work was the primary manual for pharmacology for 1,500 years</note> writes in Book 5, Chapter 92.

*Milk-stone* <note>original: "Galactes lapis"</note> is so called because it exudes a milky juice; it is ash-colored and sweet to the taste, especially in water. Dioscorides says that *Galactites* is of a single color, like milk, and he calls the same stone *Leucogogea* and *synephite*. It is notable for its milky juice and flavor; when tied to the neck, it is said to create an abundance of milk for nursing mothers and to produce saliva in infants. They say that it melts in the mouth and takes away the memory. The Achelous river <note>the largest river in Greece</note> and the Nile provide this stone.

*Gala*, is milk <note>original: "Lac"; from the Greek γαλα</note>. From this comes the "Galaxy" <note>literally "The Milky Way"</note>.

*Gangrene*, is the beginning of the mortification <term>tissue death</term> of an affected part.

### 101 ###

*Gatrinum* is potash <note>original: "Cinis clavellatus"; literally "ash from small clubs," a salt produced by leaching wood ashes in a pot, used in glassmaking and alchemy</note>.

*Gebalum* refers to something that must be restored or repaired. *Coagulare* <note>to coagulate or thicken</note> means the same thing.

*Gecharfam* or *Difdaha* <note>likely derived from the Arabic and Hebrew words for frog</note> is a frog.

*Gemstone from the house spider*, of immense size. 
In the heads of these spiders, it is said that a gemstone is found which is a most effective remedy against all poisons and enchantments. It is no wonder that such a gem possesses such power, since spider webs themselves are of great importance for many various uses.

*Beryl Gemstone. 1. Beryl. 2. Chrysoberyl.* <note>A yellowish-green variety of beryl</note>.
3. *Chrysoprase.* <note>A leek-green variety of chalcedony</note>.

*Opal Gemstone.*

*Thunderstones* <note>original: "Gemmæ Cerauniæ"; literally "lightning stones." Historically, these were often prehistoric stone tools or fossilized belemnites believed to fall from the sky during storms</note>. *1. Long Thunderstone. 2. Round.*

*Iris Gemstone* is a hexagonal, white stone which, when it catches the sun’s rays, casts an arc similar to a heavenly rainbow <note>original: "arcum cœlesti similem"</note> onto a wall. There are also counterfeit Irises.

*Toad-stones* <note>original: "Gemmæ Rubetarum"</note>. The toad <note>original: "Phrynus bufo seu rubeta"</note> is a foul animal living in caves. There is a species of it in France and Spain which is horned, with shining yellow and black spots; under the name *Borax* <note>not to be confused with the modern mineral borax; here it refers to the legendary "toad-stone"</note> they carry gemstones in their heads of the same name. Their color is sometimes a brownish white; sometimes they have a blue "eye" in the middle, if the stone is extracted while the *borax* <term>the toad</term> is still alive or even pulsating. Sometimes it is black with shining black marks, like our local toads. The shape was round and about the size of a medium bean. Sometimes green and even multicolored *Boraces* <term>toad-stones</term> are found. Commonly such stones are called *Cropodinos*. Sometimes the shape of toads with outspread legs appears within these stones. They are worn in rings by princes, for where poisons are present, this stone changes color and emits drops as if sweating. These toad-stones are wonderfully sought after from our toads, so much so that people strive to catch them with a leap. But whether they are born from the brain or from the viscosity of the foam—

### 103 ###

...brings forth from the hidden into the manifest, with the previous appearance unchanged: just as if nature gave white gold, <term>gradation</term> <note>A process of refining or intensifying the color of metals, often to make them appear more "mature" or gold-like</note> makes it red; if it is volatile, it fixes it; if it is impure, it purifies it, etc.

*Garnet* <note>original: "Granatus"</note> is undoubtedly a species of <term>carbuncle</term> <note>A general historical term for red gemstones, including rubies and garnets</note>; it is a red gemstone, translucent like pomegranate blossoms <note>original: "balaustiis"</note>, of a rosy color, though darker than a carbuncle. Another species of it has a color scattered with the violet of a pansy. Its homeland is *Ethiopia* and the *Sands of the Sea*. The Garnet gladdens the heart and drives away sadness. Pliny and Solinus call the female of this stone not *paeaniten*, but *paeantiden*. Paracelsus <note>abbreviated as "Para." in the text</note> testifies that it becomes pregnant and heals those conceiving and those in labor.

*Green grain* <note>original: "Granum viride"</note> is the fruit of the turpentine tree.

*Grain of the Nile* <note>original: "Granum nil"</note> is an Indian seed.

*Granulation* is the breaking down into small grains, which the process of melting serves. It is specifically for metals and is accomplished in various ways. Molten metals are poured into water, by the force of which they break apart into pieces. They ought to be melted in a crucible or an iron ladle. Sometimes wicker brooms made of thin twigs are placed in the water so that they may break apart better. In place of brooms, a board, or a strainer <note>original: "coloratorium"</note> with very narrow holes, or a grate is used.

*Grassa* is Attinkar or Borax.

*Gratiola* <note>Hedge hyssop, known for its medicinal purgative properties</note> is a well-known herb, once brought to us at great price; it was found around Nuremberg and grows abundantly in the territory of Strasbourg <note>original: "Argentoratensi"</note> near the city.

*Grus* [Crane]: A Stork.

*Guaril* is a <term>stellio</term> <note>A spotted lizard; in alchemy, this often refers to a specific mineral or a stage in a reaction</note>.

*Guma, gumi*: Oil of Sulfur. A coagulum <note>A curdled or thickened mass</note>.

*Gum of paradise* <note>original: "Guma paradisi"</note>: <term>Orpiment</term> <note>A yellow arsenic sulfide mineral once used as a pigment and in alchemy</note>.

*Gum of Copper* <note>original: "Guma Cupri"</note>: This is the "drippings of Venus" <note>A liquid byproduct or distillate of copper; Venus is the alchemical symbol for copper</note>.

*Gummi asinar*: This is almond gum.

*Gum of the nut tree* <note>original: "Gummi arboris nucum"</note>: This is from the trembling tree <note>The Aspen</note>.

### 104 ###

**Cedar Resin**, <note>original: "Gummi Cedri"</note> also known as *Cedria*, *Kedria*, *Alkitran* <note>The Arabic term for pitch or tar</note>, or *Xerbin*.

**Gutteo** is a gum that is found near the sea.

**Red Drop** <note>original: "Gutta ruonia" or "Gutta rubea"</note>, or *Butiga*, is an inflammation covering the entire face. <note>This likely refers to a skin condition such as rosacea or severe acne.</note>

**The mortification of gems** <note>In alchemy, "mortification" refers to the process of stripping a substance of its outward form or "life" to prepare it for transformation.</note>, and how it may be done, is found in **Paracelsus**, *Book 5 of On the Nature of Things*, page 428.

**The transmutation of gems** is performed with oil of sulfur, as described in **Book 7** of *On the Nature of Things*, page 462.

**Gypsum** <note>Plaster of Paris or mineral calcium sulfate</note> is perhaps made from the "mirror stone" <note>original: "lapide speculari," referring to translucent minerals like selenite or mica</note> which they call *Spar-glass* <note>original: "Sparodes spergulas"</note> because of its resemblance to glass. **Gypsum** is a relative of **lime** <note>original: "calci"</note>, but it is not as hot. They differ in that lime, when quenched with some liquid, generates heat; however, gypsum does not catch fire or heat up when quenched. Its power is to bind and to block; if swallowed, it causes suffocation. Its use is only external, due to its extreme drying and choking qualities.

**Gypsum** is of two types:

1. **Natural or Mined**, which is dug out of the earth, though usually from the upper layers. It is a substance almost similar to <term>nitre</term> <note>saltpeter or native soda</note>, sometimes found in wide veins. It was known to **Theophrastus** and **Pliny** and is found near the Elbe river.

2. The other **Gypsum** is **Man-made**. It is created or "cooked" (to use Pliny’s word) from a white stone similar to alabaster. Veins of this stone are found in the rocks of **Jena**, and at the **Harz Mountains** <note>original: "sylvam Hercynam"</note>, and at the mines of **Saalfeld**. **Ruland** <note>Martin Ruland the Elder, a famous physician and alchemist</note> thinks this stone is like the hardened marrow of the rock; or it seems to be a species of <term>aphonitrum</term> <note>a salt-like mineral efflorescence</note>, or true gypsum, or <term>calamine</term> <note>zinc ore</note>.

Secondly, man-made **Gypsum** is made from that layered mirror-stone which is called by the Germans *Sperglass* or *Mary’s Glass* <note>original: "glacies Mariae," referring to selenite, which was used to cover icons of the Virgin Mary</note>, because it looks like ice. This is the best Gypsum, as **Pliny** testifies in *Book 36, Chapter 24*. **Ruland**, on page 249, lists sixteen different species of gypsums.

## H.

**Hal** is a name for salt. <note>original: "sal"</note> **Hal**, or sometimes **Hol**, also refers to Vinegar. <note>original: "Acetum"</note>

**Halcyon**, or *alcion*, is the "ice-bird" or kingfisher. <note>original: "glacialis avicula"</note>

**Harmel** is the seed of the wild rue.

**Haro** is a species of fern, similar to Polypody.

**Hadid** is iron. <note>This is the Latinized form of the Arabic word for iron, *ḥadīd*.</note>

**Hematite** <note>original: "Hæmatites"</note> is called **Bloodstone** <note>original: "Blutstein"</note> by the Germans because of its blood-red color, which it can impart as a dye. It is primarily observed as a mineral found in mines; it is also valued for its medicinal effect in stopping blood in all types of hemorrhages. **Ruland**, drawing from **Pliny** and **Dioscorides**, lists six species of this stone on pages 250 and 251.

**Hager** is a stone.

**Halimar** is Copper.

**Halcyonium** is nothing other than congealed sea-foam. It is a fissile material on the inside that causes a stinging sensation in the throat and eyes; for this reason, it is known in pharmacies today as "sea-foam" <note>original: "spuma maris"</note>. However, it is not the same as the "foam of salt" or common sea-foam, though they are related. The "foam of salt" is a downy or shaved-off portion of the foamy sea that has coagulated on rocks. **Halcyonium**, by contrast, is a congealed, fissile, and spongy sea-foam. **Ruland**, following **Dioscorides**, lists five types of halcyonium on page 253.

**Hel** is honey.

**Red Henry** <note>original: "Henricus rubeus"</note> is the <term>colcothar</term> <note>The red iron oxide remains left after the distillation of sulfuric acid from vitriol.</note> of vitriol; that is, vitriol that has been calcined (burnt) until it turns red.

**Helebria** is a species of black hellebore <note>original: "veratri nigri"</note> that produces red flowers.

**Heliotropium** is the "Melissa of Paracelsus." <note>Likely referring to a specific alchemical preparation of lemon balm (Melissa officinalis) or a plant Paracelsus associated with the sun.</note>

**Hemorrhoids** is a flow of blood from the anus.

**Hydromancy** <note>original: "Hydromantia"</note> is the art derived from the "stars of the water," <note>This refers to the alchemical or astrological belief that terrestrial elements like water are influenced by specific celestial bodies.</note> when...

**Hinnicula**, also known as *genicula* <note>little knee</note> or, as mentioned above, *gumicula* <note>little bud</note>, is Valerian. Truly, I believe this variation in name arises from a scribal error.

**Homunculi** [Little Men] are superstitious little images fashioned in the place or name of someone, intended to contain the invisible astral man; they were created for this purpose for superstitious use. <note>In alchemy, this term often refers to the creation of artificial life, but here it describes talismans or effigies used in folk magic.</note>

**Horizon** is the mercury of gold.

**Humor of life** <note>original: "Humor vitae"</note> is the nourishment and food of natural heat, otherwise known as the <term>radical moisture</term> <note>The "humor radicalis" was believed to be the essential fluid within all living things that maintained life and prevented aging or "withering."</note>, which sustains all living things so they do not wither away.

**Hyarith** is the Moon. <note>An alchemical code name for Silver.</note>

**Hyacinth** <note>original: "Hyacinthus"</note> is a gemstone differing from Amethyst in that the violet flash shining in amethyst is diluted in hyacinth; it is pleasing at first sight, but it fades before it can satisfy the eye, withering faster than the flower of the same name. **Pliny**, Book 37, Chapter 9. Ethiopia, Arabia, and India provide **Hyacinths** and **Chrysoliths**. The Arabs distinguish three species: those of a red color, a lemon-yellow <note>original: "Citrini"</note> color, and an antimony color.

**Hyacinth** induces sleep, is effective against poisons <note>original: "toxica"</note>, cheers the person, and strengthens the heart. The ancient Astrologers dedicated the **Hyacinth** to Jupiter. **Ruland** lists six species on page 256.

# I.

**Jasper** <note>original: "Iaspis"</note> is the bloodstone <note>original: "lapis hæmatites." In early mineralogy, these stones were often grouped by their supposed stanching properties.</note>.
**Iassa** is the pansy <note>original: "herba trinitatis," literally "herb of the trinity"</note> and smartweed <note>original: "persicaria"</note>. See page 457 of the *Great Surgery* <note>original: "Mag. Chyrurg."; this refers to the *Great Surgery* of Paracelsus.</note>.
**Red jaundice** <note>original: "Icteritia rubea"</note> is **St. Anthony’s Fire** <note>original: "Erysipelas," a skin infection characterized by a red, painful rash.</note>.
**Idiots** are those who despise the true arts, even if they are highly skilled professors of false ones.
**Lion’s fire** <note>original: "Ignis leonis"</note> is the element of fire itself.
**Persian fire** <note>original: "Ignis persicus"</note> is a burning ulcer, twisting with heat.
**Attained frost-fire** <note>original: "Ignis pruinus adeptus"</note> is the <term>quintessence</term> <note>The "fifth essence," or the most pure and concentrated extract of a substance.</note> of vitriol (or wine) rectified with tartar.
**Primary Ilech** <note>original: "Ilech primum"</note> is the first principle. See also *Ileias* and *Ileadus*. <note>In the philosophy of Paracelsus, "Ilech" refers to the vital force or the primordial beginning of things.</note>
**Supernatural Ilech** is the conjunction of the higher and firmamental stars with the stars of earthly things and wine.
**Great Ilech** is the ascendant or the "star" of a medicine, which is taken along with the medicine in which it is hidden—no differently than the higher stars are in the firmament or the lower stars are in man.
**Raw Ilech** is a composition from the <term>prime matter</term> <note>The foundational substance from which all physical things are formed.</note> of the three first principles: Mercury, Salt, and Sulphur, from which all things are composed.
**Ileidus** is elemental air; in humans, however, it is the spirit that passes through all the limbs.
**Iliaster** (or *Iliastes* or *Iliadum*) is the prime matter of all things, consisting of sulphur, mercury, and salt; it is fourfold according to the four elements.
**The first** is the chaos of the earth.
**The second** is the chaos of the water.
**The third** is the chaos of the air.

### 108 ###

The fourth, indeed, is the chaos of fire.

Likewise, there are four *Iliastri* <note>In Paracelsian philosophy, the *Iliaster* is the primeval matter or the vital force that governs the lifespan and growth of things.</note> of men concerning a long life.

**Iacens** [Lying down] is the foundation of a mountain.

Ruland <note>Martin Ruland the Elder (1533–1602), a physician and author of a famous alchemical dictionary.</note> enumerates 22 species of **Jasper** (from page 257 to 259).

**Idum** is a pottage <note>A thick soup or porridge.</note>.

**Idroagira** is alkali water.

**Iffides** is ceruse <note>White lead, historically used as a pigment and in medicinal salves.</note> or the ash of lead.

**Fire**: Fire used as a name for the **philosopher’s stone**. Ruland, page 9.
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;He enumerates the species of **fire** on page 260.
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;The **heat of fire** is either natural or artificial.
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;**Algir fire** is the strongest fire.
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;**Elementary fire** is Sulfur, but not that of the common sort <note>Alchemists distinguished "philosophical sulfur" (a pure principle of matter) from "vulgar" or common sulfur.</note>.
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;**Fire of the wise** is warm horse manure <note>original: "fimus equinus." Manure was used in laboratories to provide a gentle, steady, and long-lasting heat for the "digestion" of chemical mixtures.</note>.
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;**Brightly burning fire** is sulfur.
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;**Extinguished fire** is extinguished sulfur.

The element of **Venus's fire** is the oil from the sulfur of Copper <note>In alchemy, Venus is the planetary symbol for copper.</note>. Van Helmont <note>Jan Baptist van Helmont (1580–1644), a chemist who helped bridge the gap between alchemy and modern chemistry.</note>.

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;**Fire of Gehenna** [Hellfire] is a specific corrosive. Described by Paracelsus in book 7 of the *Archidoxes*, page 227.

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;There are four degrees of **fire** to be observed by craftsmen, just as the ancient philosophers observed them. They are judged not only by the senses, but also by their effect on the things themselves, and by judgment joined with the senses—most especially by sight and touch.

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;The first degree is the slowest, like a very slight or sluggish warmth; it is called the heat of a mild bath <note>original: "balnei mitis"</note>, or of manure, or of circulatory digestion, etc. It is described by touch such that a person's finger can always bear to be held in it; and although it has its intensifications and relaxations, it ought never to produce an evident or sharp sensation. Of this kind is the **vaporous fire** of the philosophers, which is described as the brooding of a hen...

### 109 ###

...of a hen hatching chicks <note>The text likely completes the word "gallina" from the previous page.</note>, or the nature of a person of healthy constitution. This heat is generally such as is provided by the furnace of a **vapor bath** <note>original: "vaporarium"; a vessel for heating substances with steam.</note> moderately heated, or like that of a **sand bowl** <note>original: "catinus arenarius"; a sand bath used to distribute heat evenly.</note> sitting on a furnace, or a modest flame beneath such a bowl.

The **second degree** is more intense, so that it clearly strikes the sense of touch, yet without causing injury to the body <note>Literally "to the organ" of touch.</note>. They call this the **heat of ashes**, such as is provided by a moderate fire lit beneath a bowl of ashes. For ashes, due to their porous nature, do not hold the air as much.

The **third degree** causes injury to the one touching it, and is compared to boiling sand or iron filings; thus it is called the **fire of sand**, or of iron filings, etc.

The **fourth** is the **highest degree**, and is usually destructive. They call it the **reverberatory fire** <note>original: "ignem reverberii"; a furnace where the flame is "reverberated" or reflected down onto the material to achieve extreme temperatures.</note>, and it is produced by the living flame of wood, or by heaps of glowing coals vigorously fanned by bellows. These degrees, when applied to certain things, change significantly. Thus it happens that what would be the highest degree for a plant might be the first for **antimony** <note>original: "stibium"</note> or another solid substance.

But although the heat of a **bath** <note>original: "balneum"</note> is usually understood as the first degree, nevertheless in the operation of a bath all four degrees can take place:
1. The first, when the water grows moderately warm.
2. The second, when it is already sharply felt.
3. The third, when it burns.
4. The fourth, when with maximum heat it even destroys; all of which are more clearly seen in oil.

<margin>Degrees of alchemical fire. Paracelsus, *On the Nature of Things*, book 7, page 457.</margin>

**Ignition** <note>original: "Ignitio"</note> is **Calcination**, reducing bodies to lime by the violence of fire. It consists of combustion and reverberation.

**To Imbibe** <note>original: "Imbibere"</note> is to thicken by grinding <note>original: "terendo inspissare"; a process of mixing a liquid into a solid by grinding them together with a mortar and pestle.</note>.

**Imbibition** <note>original: "Imbibitio"</note> is a washing, when a liquid is added to a body...

### 110 ###

...joined together, it rises, and finding no exit, it falls back into the body, washing it with frequent moistenings until, purely coagulated with the body, it can no longer ascend but remains entirely fixed. This is a purely philosophical operation <note>In alchemy, "philosophical" refers to the refined methods of the "Sons of Art" rather than common chemical procedures.</note> and does not descend to the level of common workers.

**Immersive corrosion** is when bodies, submerged in other substances, are reduced to a powder <term>calx: the crumbly residue left after a metal or mineral has been burnt or corroded</term>. It is categorized as either "moist" or "dry."

**Inceration** <note>original: "Inceratio"; the process of bringing a substance to a waxy state</note> is the mixing of a liquid with a dry substance through slow, repeated soaking until it reaches the consistency of softened wax. For this reason, it is called **waxing** <note>original Greek: "εγκήρωσις" (enkērōsis)</note>; otherwise it is also called **imbibition**, which occurs through "irrigation," where the dry substance "drinks up" the moisture. This is no different from the "nourishment" used by physicians, where substances like **flesh-glue** <term>sarcocolla: a tree resin used since antiquity to heal wounds and "glue" flesh together</term>, **litharge** <term>lead oxide</term>, and others are gradually soaked in a specific juice until they become fit for use.

**Incineration** is the ignition of a body with a more intense fire, converting it into ashes.

Vegetable and animal matters are **incinerated**; however, minerals (speaking in the chemical fashion) are properly said to be reduced to **lime**, not to ashes.

**Incorporation** is a thorough mixing by which moist and dry things are immediately blended into a single body in the form of a mass. Unlike the slow process of "nourishment," the full amount of liquid required for the consistency of the mixture is added all at once, so that it becomes like a dough; thus it can also be called **impastation** <note>making into a paste</note>, or in some cases **kneading**. These incorporated substances are then left in "digestive" heat <note>a gentle, steady warmth like that of a stomach or a nesting bird</note> so that, through their mutual interaction, they may achieve a common temperament <note>original: "crasis"; a medical and alchemical term for a balanced mixture of qualities</note>.

**Incuba** is the "bride of the sun" <note>In alchemical allegory, the "Sun" is Gold or the active masculine principle; his bride is usually the receptive feminine principle, such as Silver or Mercury.</note>.

**Indian Salt** is **rock salt** <note>original: "Sal gemmae"; a transparent, high-purity crystalline salt.</note>.

**Indian foam** <note>Likely referring to Indigo pigment</note> is of two kinds. Specifically, there is a "native" Indian foam, which grows spontaneously on certain reeds in India. Painters call it **la-**

<meta>catchword: pis (likely "lapis," meaning stone, referring to lapis indigo)</meta>

### 111 ###

...**Indian stone** <note>original: "Lapis Indicus"; referring here to Indigo dye, which was often mistaken for a mineral or stone in early accounts</note>, though improperly named, for it is not actually a stone; it is very similar to **adarces** <note>a salt-like crust or efflorescence found on reeds in marshy areas</note>, which likewise adheres to reeds. However, while *adarces* is formed by accretion, Indigo grows naturally upon reeds; it is also partly an artificial product made in dyers' workshops. It is a purple foam that forms on the dyeing vats. The best artificial version is that which appears blue; it possesses a blue color within a black base.

It is mildly astringent, cleanses, and suppresses ulcers. Others say it is simply the juice of a plant with which cloth is now dyed, which they call **Indigo**—that is, **woad** <term>glastum: the plant *Isatis tinctoria*, used since ancient times to produce blue dye</term>. One variety is the domestic type known to the people of Thuringia <note>a region in central Germany famous for its woad production</note>, which is used extensively by dyers. This is the source from which artificial Indigo is properly made; the other variety is wild woad. On these, see *Dioscorides*, Book 2, in the chapter on woad.

**Infundibulum** <note>literally "a funnel," but described here as an ingot mold</note> is an oblong metal vessel with a handle, hollowed out on the inside, which receives molten metals and shapes them into rods or bars.

**Inhumation** and **To Inhume** <note>the process of "burying" a vessel in earth, sand, or warm dung to maintain a gentle, steady heat</note>.
In fire, the seven Planets.
In water, the seven metals.
In earth, the seven herbs.
In air, the seven **mannas** <term>tereniabin: a Persian-derived term for "honey-dew" or manna that falls from the sky onto plants</term>.
In animals, there are seven principal organs.

<margin>Paracelsus, volume [missing], page 872.</margin>
For there is nothing in the whole nature of things that does not consist of these three: **Mercury, Salt, and Sulfur**. These are the three principles of **Theophrastus** <note>Theophrastus von Hohenheim, the birth name of the famous alchemist Paracelsus</note>, which are uncovered through **spagyric analysis** <term>spagyric: the alchemical process of separating a substance into its components and then purifying and recombining them</term>. We shall find nothing besides these three, each of which exists within every element. **Jean Fernel** <note>a highly influential 16th-century French physician</note> also observed these three in his medical work regarding "innate moisture." The ancients also passed these down long ago, so that *Theophrastus* was not the first to discover them, but rather he brought them back into the light after they had been buried in shadows.

A metal instrument indicating the parts of the world: a **Compass**.
