# CHEMICAL DICTIONARY
<page-num>138</page-num>

<header>CHEMICAL DICTIONARY</header>

In every respect, there are four species of this:
- **Inceration** <term>Inceratio: the process of softening a dry substance by adding wax or oils until it reaches a wax-like consistency</term>,
- And **incorporation**,
- **Colliquation** <term>Colliquatio: the melting or fusing together of different solid substances</term>,
- And **Mingling** <note>original: "Confusio"</note>.

*Mixture*, made of equal parts copper and silver.

*Molybdochalcum* <note>original: "Molibotum calchum"</note>, is leaded copper or brass.

*Molybdides*, is a lead-stone, or something pertaining to lead.

*Murmentum*, is the mountain dormouse <note>likely referring to a marmot, known in Latin as "glis montanus"</note>.

*Hydraulics* <note>original: "Mechanopeotica"</note>, is the invention of the water-art, such as fountains driven into the air, syringes, the lifting of water through pipes and valves, bellows, and similar devices.

*Meditation*, is said to occur whenever an internal dialogue is held with someone else who is not seen, such as with God by invoking Him, or with oneself, or with one’s own good angel.

*Melaones, or Meloe*, are flying beetles of a shining golden color which, when crushed, give off a sweet odor; they are found in meadows, most especially in the month of May <note>likely the Oil Beetle or similar blister beetles used in historical medicine</note>.

*Meliboeum*, is copper. Also written as *Melibocum*.

*Melissa*, is understood here not as lemon balm <note>original: "apiastrum"</note>, but as Manna drawn from the more noble herbs by means of a **magistery** <term>magisterio: a master-process or expert chemical preparation</term>.

*Melusines, and "better royal daughters"* <note>original: "Melusinæ, & melioræ filiæ regiæ"</note>, who, having once fallen into despair because of their sins, were snatched away by Satan and transformed into specters, evil spirits, horrible **lemures** <note>restless ghosts of the dead</note>, and into immense monsters. They are thought to live without a rational soul, existing only in a physical "phantom body" <note>original: "corpore phantastico"</note>, and are nourished by the elements. It is believed they will pass away along with the elements on the final Day of Judgment, unless they happen to contract a marriage with a human man; in that case, they are finally able to die a natural death as he does, and can live a natural life by virtue of this union. It is believed that many specters of this same status and kind are found in deserts, forests, ruins, monuments, empty castles, and on the remote shores of the sea. They are commonly called "cursed huma-"

<meta>catchword: huma- (homines / humans)</meta>

# CHEMICAL DICTIONARY
<page-num>135</page-num>

<header>CHEMICAL DICTIONARY</header>

...men, but by their proper name they are called specters; they are also the "saints" of the devils, with whom evil demons <note>original: "cacodæmones"</note> dwell and perform their illusions and wonders.

*Philosophical Month* <note>original: "Mensis philosophicus"</note>, is the time required for digestion <term>Digestion: the process of exposing a substance to gentle, continuous heat, similar to the incubation of an egg</term>, namely forty days.

*Mercury coagulated by nature*, is solid metal. Indeed, it is coagulated by art, either with metals or minerals, or by itself without them, or through other things existing outside of the mineral nature. See above in the second treatise concerning its coagulations.

*Crystalline Mercury*, is that which, through frequently repeated exaltations <term>Exaltation: a chemical process that raises a substance to a higher level of purity or "power"</term>, has been brought to the transparency of crystal.

*Coralline Mercury*, is that which is brought to a coral-like redness through oil of eggs and other waters.

*Raw Mercury*, is that which has not yet been separated from its matrix or the ore in which it was generated.

*Loose Mercury* <note>original: "Mercurius laxus"</note>, is mineral turbith <note>A yellow basic sulfate of mercury, often used as a purgative in early medicine</note>.

*Precipitated Mercury of metals*, is mercury drawn from metals and precipitated; it is called "mercury of metals," or otherwise "corporeal mercury." It is the passive female seed.

*Mercury of minerals*, is the oiliness or unctuousness drawn from ores of gold and silver.

*Regenerated Mercury*, is the first essence <term>Primum ens: the "first being" or fundamental source of a substance's power</term> of mercury.

*Savage Mercury* <note>original: "Mercurialis sæva"</note>, is the water of alum in which mercury is generated. It is also a purgative salt of a golden color found in salt caverns, very well known to miners; they use this orally for pneumonia.

*Running Metal*, is quicksilver <note>original: "mercurius vivus"</note>.

*Microcosm*, is the small and middle world, situated naturally between the firmamental and the elementary [worlds], because it participates in both: whatever is contained in those really and actually is likewise held in man spiritually and potentially. Thus it happens that there is one and the same...

Everywhere there are four species of this:
- Inceration <term>Inceration: an alchemical process of adding liquid to a substance until it reaches the consistency of soft wax</term>,
- And incorporation,
- Colliquation <term>Colliquation: the melting together or liquefying of different substances</term>,
- And Confusion <note>In this context, the thorough mingling or blending of different elements</note>.

*Mixture* <note>original: "Mistura"</note>, made of equal parts copper <note>original: "ære"</note> and silver.
*Lead-copper* <note>original: "Molibotum calchum"</note>, is copper of lead.
*Molipdides*, is a lead stone, or something made of lead.
*Murmentum*, is a mountain dormouse.
*Mechanopeotica*, is the invention of the water-works art, such as fountains driven into the air, syringes, the raising of waters through pipes and valves, bellows, and similar things.
*Meditation*, is what it is called whenever an internal conversation is held with someone else who is not seen—such as with God by invoking Him, or with oneself, or with one’s own good angel.
*Melaones, or Meloes*, are flying beetles, shining with a golden color, which give off a sweet odor when crushed; they are found in meadows especially in the month of May <note>likely referring to oil beetles or Spanish flies, which were used in early medicine</note>.
*Melibæum*, is copper. *Melibocum*.
*Melissa*, used here not for lemon balm <note>original: "apiastro"</note>, but for manna extracted from the more noble herbs through mastery <term>Magistery: a high-level alchemical preparation where the "sulfur" or essence of a plant is extracted and purified</term>.
*Melusines, and Meliorae*, were former royal daughters, once desperate because of their sins, who were snatched away by Satan and transformed into specters, evil spirits, horrible ghosts <note>original: "lemures"</note>, and into immense monsters. They are thought to live without a rational soul, existing only in a physical body of fantasy, nourished by the elements; they are destined to pass away along with the elements on the final day of judgment, unless by some chance they contract a marriage with a human man. Only then, like the man himself, can they die a natural death, as they are able to live naturally by virtue of this union. It is believed that many more specters of the same state and kind are found in deserts, forests, ruins, tombs, empty citadels, and on the farthest shores of the sea. They are commonly called the "cursed ones."

...from these, a true philosophical anatomy can and ought to be made—not the dead anatomy of a corpse, but one that is living, essential, and efficacious.

# CHEMICAL DICTIONARY
<page-num>141</page-num>

<header>CHEMICAL DICTIONARY</header>

...from coagulated moisture <note>original: "humore coagulato"</note>, such as *chalcitis*, *misy*, and *sori* <note>original: "chalcitis, misy & sori"—these are historical terms for various metallic sulfates or vitriols</note>, which are related to the genus of *chalcitis* as if produced from a single root. *Misy* is of a thinner consistency, while *sori* is thicker, but today *misy* is not recognized.

Every cause of *Diseases* <note>original: "Morborum"</note> must be investigated from its primary head and its own origin, so that an analogical cure may be applied to them. This must be maintained for all classes of diseases or "Entities" <term>Entia: a Paracelsian term referring to the fundamental spheres of influence that cause disease</term>, as *Theophrastus* <note>Theophrastus von Hohenheim, better known as Paracelsus</note> preferred. He established five main types of these: the Natural Entity (*Ens Natura*), the Astral Entity (*Ens Astrale*), the Entity of Poison (*Ens Veneni*), the Magical Entity (*Ens Magicum*), and the Divine Entity (*Ens Divinum*). Each of these Entities requires its own specific treatment. This is especially true of sidereal or astral diseases, for which no medicines are useful except those derived from a "Firmamental" Cure <note>referring to medicine that utilizes the power of the heavens or stars</note>, through the intervening cooperation of celestial influence. These must be paired only with those things that have an analogy balanced between the Lesser World <note>the Microcosm, or the human body</note> and the Greater World <note>the Macrocosm, or the universe</note>, within which lies the true force of the Firmament—what they call "Magnetic" force and "Astronomical impression"—which is suited for restraining and subduing astral diseases.

The ancestral seeds and roots of *Diseases*, and the way they are transplanted and derived from the workshop or "Economy" of the Microcosm <note>the human body</note> into the family of the Microcosm and other living creatures through the benefit of the Magnetic Mummy <term>Mumia: a vital spiritual essence or "balsam" believed to reside in the body and capable of being used for healing or sympathetic magic</term> of the Microcosm, are recounted in the book titled *Biolychmium* <note>The "Lamp of Life," a work published in 1611</note> by *Ernestus Burgravius*.

There is a certain occult operation from the magnet, which they call "Antipathic" <note>acting through natural aversion or opposing forces</note>, which binds an adversary as if by some hidden fascination, so that the person from whom this power emanates becomes a source of dread to any enemy in an invisible manner. These powers are indeed observed in nature.

There exists in a certain occult property of Nature—though not without the influence of God—a virtue such as that found in Octavian <note>the birth name of the Emperor Augustus</note>, even before he was Augustus, who was superior in every playful contest entered into with Mark Antony. *Burgravius.*

# CHEMICAL DICTIONARY
<page-num>142</page-num>

<header>CHEMICAL DICTIONARY</header>

***Molochites*** <note>original: "Molochites"—modern malachite</note> is a gemstone that is not translucent, and is a denser green than an emerald; it takes its name from the color of the mallow plant <note>original: "malvæ"</note>. It is uniquely powerful against all dangers to infants; Arabia sends it forth.

***Molybdena*** <note>original: "Molybdæna"—in this context, it refers to various lead-based minerals like litharge or galena rather than the modern element molybdenum</note>. 1. Either metallic lead-ore <note>original: "plumbago metallica"</note>. 2. Produced from lead and copper. 3. From a mixture of lead and silver. 4. From a mixture of lead and gold. 5. Prepared from lead plates, etc.

***Morochthus*** is a stone. It is born in Egypt and is soft, so that lines may be drawn with it; it is extremely white, easily melting, and useful for whitening clothes. <note>This likely refers to a type of white clay or fibrous gypsum known as "French chalk."</note>

***Mortaliorum*** is a mold for forming ash cups <term>cupels: small, porous bowls used by alchemists and assayers to separate precious metals from base metals through heating</term>, made in the manner of a wide circle or a pierced basin, usually made of brass <note>original: "orichalco"</note>. This ***Mortariolum*** <note>small mortar or mold</note> is accompanied by a *pila* (commonly called a "monk"), which is a pestle with a rounded protrusion from a wide central base, by whose strike the hollow of the cupel is formed. But to form larger cups—that is, those capable of holding a greater abundance of material—we use an iron circle and produce the hollow with a suitable pestle.

***Every metal*** is coagulated Mercury. <note>Paracelsus, page 883.</note>

According to the order of the ***Matrices*** <term>Matrices: in Paracelsian thought, these are the elemental "wombs"—Earth, Air, Water, and Fire—from which all things are generated</term>, there are four degrees of things:
1. Those that come from the earth are of the first degree.
2. Those from the air are of the second degree.
3. Those from the water are of the third degree.
4. Those from the fire are of the fourth degree. Furthermore, the specific virtue by which one thing overcomes another within each separate degree is a matter of "points" <note>specific intensities</note>, not of the degrees themselves.

***Lemon Balm*** <note>original: "Melissa"</note> is the best herb for the heart of all those produced by the earth. <note>Paracelsus</note>

***Mos*** is musk <note>original: "mycra"—likely a corruption of the Greek 'myron' for ointment or 'muscus' for musk</note>.

***Brine*** <note>original: "Muria"</note> has the effect of salt and seawater. It is of two kinds.

# CHEMICAL DICTIONARY 141

<header>CHEMICAL DICTIONARY. 141</header>

1. **Native [brine]**, from which our common salt is produced or boiled, as is done at Halle in Saxony and other places.
2. **Artificial [brine]**, as when we mix a little salt into "celestial water" <note>original: "coelesti aquę"—usually refers to rainwater in alchemical texts</note>. From this, we call it a "light salt" when the salt added to it dissolves, and conversely, a "hard salt" when the added salt does not dissolve; this is also a passing way to test whether a salt is of good quality. From this come certain varieties like **garum**, that liquid made from fish and meat steeped in salt, which is called *Pechel* <note>original: "Pechel"—likely related to the German "Pökel" or "Pickle," meaning brine</note>. Another variety is **Oxalme**, which is an acidic brine made from vinegar and seawater.

***Muria*** <note>original: "Muria"—brine</note>, a foul-smelling solvent <term>menstruum: a solvent used to extract essence from a solid</term>, or nitric acid <note>original: "aqua fortis"</note>.
***Muscula***, a squirrel; or a "curved squirrel" <note>original: "campsciurus"</note>.
***Mustum***, something unripe <note>original: "immaturum"</note>.
***Mydisis***, the corruption or decay of bodily parts.
***Mydriasis***, a disease of the eyes caused by an excess of fluid <note>modern medicine uses this term for pupil dilation</note>.
***Mylphæ***, the falling out of the eyelashes, or dry medications used for discharges from the eyes.
***Myopiasis***, a disease of the eyes where we can see nothing from a distance, but only when it is close <note>nearsightedness</note>.
***Myricetum***, a field or place where broom <note>original: "genista"</note> grows.
***Myrmecia***, a certain growth or condition of the skin. **Myrmecia** are also warts on the private parts.
***Myron***, an ointment. **Myrocopus**, one who makes or sells ointments.
***Mysis***, a blockage of the pores.
***Mysterium*** <note>The "Mystery"</note>, is the essence of the inner nature, carrying the whole of a substance within a subtle and hidden part of the matter. Just as the juice differs from the body of a fruit, so does this essence differ from the juice; and because it lies hidden in the innermost recesses of bodily matter and possesses an extremely subtle nature, it is usually called a "mystery" or an **arcanum** <term>arcanum: a secret or "shut away" healing power or essence</term>. It is extracted either immediately from things themselves (after proper preparation) or sometimes from a "magistery" <term>magistery: a refined medicinal preparation where the whole substance is transformed rather than just the essence extracted</term> or juice. It is of two types: the **Quintessence** <note>the "fifth element," or the most purified essence of a thing</note>, and the **Specific Arcanum**.

<meta>catchword: Magnum (Great)</meta>

<summary>This page concludes a discussion on salt solutions and brines before defining a series of medical eye conditions, skin ailments, and the Paracelsian concept of the "Mysterium"—the hidden, subtle essence within physical matter.</summary>

<keywords>brine, salt production, ophthalmology, nearsightedness, ointments, Mysterium, Arcanum, Quintessence, Paracelsian chemistry</keywords>

# CHEMICAL DICTIONARY 144

<header>CHEMICAL DICTIONARY. 144</header>

**The Great Necessity** <note>original: "Magnum Oportet," a concept referring to the unavoidable toils and biological requirements of human life</note> is a thistle and a thorn of the earth in the middle of human life; about which *Van Helmont* <note>Jan Baptist van Helmont (1580–1644), a pioneering Flemish chemist and physician who bridged the gap between alchemy and early chemistry</note> wrote a specific treatise.

***The Mystery of Black Hellebore***, refers specifically to the leaves of black hellebore <note>a plant used in ancient and early modern medicine, often as a purgative for "melancholy"</note>. *Paracelsus* <note>indicated by the abbreviation "Para."</note> teaches that a wound ought to be cured through the principal organs: for instance, ailments of the head through remedies for the brain, liver ailments through hepatic remedies, and so on for others.

**Monsters** <note>original: "Monstra"</note> are certain living creatures born outside the course of nature, which heaven excludes from the egg of their illicit putrefaction and shameful mixing. They generate nothing thereafter because they are born from the joining of two contrary seeds of different species, such as from a human and a beast; sometimes even from sorcerous parents. From this, giants once took their origin, as did other monstrous men, horrific to behold and cursed, contrary to the laws of nature. Nor is it done with any greater honor by the malice of men, who by force compel beasts of different species to mate—such as a horse and a female donkey—to generate those who carry the burdens of the "wisdom of this world" <note>likely referring to mules; here used as a metaphor for the prideful carrying of worldly knowledge</note>. These too are monsters, unfit and sterile for reproduction, as nature shrinks away from them just as it does from others. 

A story is told which happened not so long ago in Salzburg, concerning a monster found by hunters in the forest in the neighboring regions of Carinthia. When they had captured it—though the dogs were unwilling to bite it—they brought it, surrounded on all sides, to their Bishop. It was remarkable for its clearly human face, with similar hands, feet, and fingers, but all its other limbs were hairy like a beast. It walked on four legs, held quite low like a pig. When food and drink were set before it, it never wished to taste anything; it was tested with beatings, piercings, burns, and various other torments to see if any articulate or even animal-like voice could be wrung from it, but in vain. Fearful—

# CHEMICAL DICTIONARY 145

<header>CHEMICAL DICTIONARY. 145</header>

...very dangerous; fleeing the light and the presence of men, it strove to hide itself in any place. At last, on the third day—which those captured rarely survive—it died. <note>This continues the description of a "monster" or hybrid creature from the previous page.</note>

***Morbilli*** <note>original: "Morbilli," literally "little diseases"</note> are venereal pustules; sometimes the term is even used for the venereal disease itself. <note>While modern "morbilli" refers to measles, in this period, it was often used for various eruptive skin conditions, including those associated with syphilis.</note>

***Morellum*** is nightshade. <note>original: "solatrum," likely Solanum nigrum or Black Nightshade.</note>

***Morphea*** <note>original: "Morphea"</note> is a species of leprosy, not unlike *albora* <note>a skin disease characterized by white patches, similar to vitiligo or localized leprosy</note>.

***Morus*** <note>original: "Morus," meaning mulberry</note> is an abscess that resembles a mulberry in its appearance.

***Mulæ*** <note>original: "Mulae," literally "mules"</note> are pustules that arise from either cold or heat. <note>Commonly referring to chilblains or heat blisters.</note>

***Mummy*** <note>original: "Mumia"</note> refers not only to human flesh preserved with balsam, but also to any other flesh that did not die of natural causes, but was killed and then treated with the medicinal power of the curative faculty. <note>In alchemical medicine, "Mumia" was the "vital balsam" or life force extracted from remains, believed to have profound healing properties.</note>

***Mummy of Marrow*** <note>original: "Mumia medullæ"</note> refers to the actual marrow of the bones.

***Mummy of the Elements*** <note>original: "Mumia elementorum"</note> is the balsam of the external elements.

***Overseas Mummy*** <note>original: "Mumia transmarina"</note> is, according to Paracelsus, *manna* <note>a sweet, medicinal substance gathered from plants, believed by Paracelsians to have celestial origins</note>.

***Turned Mummy*** <note>original: "Mumia versa," meaning converted</note> is the liquid form of mummy.

***Mucilage*** <note>original: "Muscilago"</note> is a viscous and sticky liquid.

***Cleansing Medicines*** <note>original: "Mundificativa"</note> are the remedies of surgeons which separate and purge weak and putrid flesh from that which is healthy and living.

***Mustus*** <note>original: "Mustus"</note> is a white sediment in the urine, appearing like fat.

***Missadar*** <note>original: "Missadar"</note> is Mercury. <note>Often used in alchemical texts to refer to Sal Ammoniac or a specific mercurial salt.</note>

***The Great Mystery*** <note>original: "Misterium magnum"</note> is the "prime matter" <term>materia prima|the fundamental, unformed substance of the universe</term> of all things; the beginning and the mother of all the combined, corruptible creatures of God. See the book *To the Athenians*. <note>Referring to Paracelsus's "Philosophia ad Athenienses," which outlines his cosmology.</note>

# CHEMICAL DICTIONARY 146

<header>CHEMICAL DICTIONARY.</header>

***Narcotics*** <note>original: "Narcotica"</note> are sleep-inducing medicines that take away pains or act to make them less agonizing.

***Nebulgea*** is a salt formed from the moisture of mists <note>original: "nebularum," referring to clouds or fog</note> that falls upon stones and is hardened by the heat of the sun.

***Necrocomica*** are wondrous omens that announce future events through signs falling from the air onto the earth, such as caterpillars in former times and many other things. <note>In the early modern period, "rains" of insects or unusual substances were often interpreted as divine or natural warnings.</note>

***Necrolium*** is that which is able to repel death and preserve life.

***Necrolics*** <note>original: "Necrolica"</note> are medicines of a similar nature.

***Necromancy*** <note>original: "Necromantica"</note> is an illicit art that worked with the dead in ancient times, as when the stars were manifested among the departed. He is truly called a necromancer who causes the dead to appear and be present, who can extort voices and answers from them, and who compels them to wander about and perform marvels. Whatever is seized or fulfilled by means of the heads, bones, needles, linens, quivers, or tombs of the dead falls under this nefarious art; it is superstitious and to be detested by any Christian.

***Nasda Sinapta*** is petroleum or asphalt.

***Napta*** is a gum. ***White Napta*** is white petroleum.

***Naphtha*** is nothing other than petroleum, that liquid oil which charlatans <note>original: "Agyrtæ," referring to wandering quacks or street performers who sold questionable medicines</note> bring to us, though it be corrupted. ***Bright Naphtha*** <note>original: "Naphtha candidum"</note> is our petroleum. ***Black Naphtha*** is perhaps that pitch-like and sticky substance of *Amiavus*, very similar to bitumen, which when burning can be extinguished by no other means than by throwing dust on it. Some of the ancients claimed this black naphtha was the "dung of demons" <note>original: "stercus dæmonum"</note>; we now recognize it as *laserpitium* <note>a plant resin prized in antiquity</note> or *asafoetida* <note>a pungent, sulfurous resin used in medicine and cooking</note>. Naphtha has a rapacious power of fire. There are two kinds of Naphtha:

1. Natural, which is properly petroleum.
2. Artificial, which is a strained bitumen.

In this way, those crusty coals or stones, which among the Ger-

# CHEMICAL DICTIONARY 147

<header>CHEMICAL DICTIONARY.</header>

...extracted from the earth <note>original: "manos," likely a typo for "fodinis" (mines) or "montibus" (mountains)</note> are a species of jet <note>original: "gagatis," a precursor to coal</note>, which are bitumen hardened under the earth or cooked by heat. These coals made of bitumen are the reason why mountains, such as Etna and Vesuvius, burn; they burn more by night than by day because during the day the heat of the sun suppresses or obscures that fire, whereas at night, which is humid and cold, the matter is again stirred up by the moisture.

***Nar*** is fire <note>original: "pyr," from the Greek word for fire</note>.

***Narbasathar*** is the breath <note>original: "flatus"</note> of the mine, or mountain brass <note>original: "aurichalcum," a golden-colored alloy or ore</note>.

***Nataron*** or ***natron*** is nitre <note>saltpeter or native soda</note>.

***Nectar*** is made from corrected and clear wine. Corrected red wine is "coagulated," while clear wine is white wine.

***Nitrum*** <note>In historical alchemy, "Nitrum" usually refers to saltpeter (potassium nitrate), though it sometimes refers to soda (sodium carbonate).</note>, also known as Baurach or Stone-salt <note>original: "Sal petorsum," the root of the word "saltpeter"</note>, is nitre, which is as if to say "salt of the stone," hot and dry in the second degree. Nitre is a substance related to salt and is a species of salt. Thus, "luminous salt," "Alexandrian salt," "salt of Anderena," and "salt nitre" are one and the same. Nitre is formed from coagulated moisture. Our medicinal preparations of it are quite excellent because they open the passages of the body much more and draw out the humors; however, they must be used with judgment. Among us, nitre is formed in various ways: in stables, in sheepfolds, in ancient monasteries, in areas trodden down many centuries ago, in rocks, cellars, and walls <note>This refers to the white, salty crust (efflorescence) that grows on damp walls in nitrogen-rich environments.</note>.

***Martin Ruland*** <note>Martin Ruland the Elder (1532–1602) was a famous physician and alchemist; his dictionary was a standard reference for these terms.</note> ***on page 346 enumerates six kinds of nitre:***
1. Armenian.
2. Arabath.
3. African.
4. Roman.
5. Egyptian.
6. Babylonian. The Arabs alone celebrated the first and sixth types.

There is also an artificial or man-made nitre, such as the "flower of the wall" <note>original: "flos parietis"</note>, or the flower of the stone, or the flower of salt. This is cooked from salt or salt-water.

***Nitrum*** is also defined as a salty stone; that is, white sulfur.

***Alexandrian Nitre*** **is** Potash. <note>original: "Cinis clavellatus," literally "clubbed ashes" or pearl ash, an alkaline salt used in making glass and soap.</note>

# CHEMICAL DICTIONARY 149

<header>CHEMICAL DICTIONARY. 149</header>

<sig>L</sig>

[...] who bring [it] to someone, or carry it to the most remote places: it is intended most especially for the night, because malignant demons flee the light and are ministers of shadows and darkness. <note>This fragment concludes an entry from the previous page, likely regarding 'Noctambuli' or night-walking spirits.</note>

***Nitre*** is a certain saltiness <note>original: "falsugo"</note> prepared into salt from collections of urine.

***Nitriales*** are all things that are caustic <note>original: "adurentes," meaning burning or stinging</note> and which reduce substances to a calx, such as saltpeter or nitre, Sulfur, etc. <note>In alchemy, "calx" refers to the powdery oxide or ash left after a metal or mineral has been burned.</note>

***Nobock*** is an instrument used in necromancy.

***Nodes*** <note>original: "Nodi"</note> are hardened swellings of the joints.

***Nostoch*** is the discharge of some star, or rather something cast down to the earth from its purging. It is found most especially in June, July, and August upon wide fields or in meadows, like a large fungus or sponge; it is a porous and light thing, tending toward a tawny or grayish-yellow color, and like a coagulated broth, it trembles when touched. There are those who understand *nostoch* metaphorically as wax. <note>Known today as *Nostoc*, a type of cyanobacteria that swells into a jelly-like mass after rain; in folklore, it was often called "star-jelly," believed to have fallen from the sky.</note>

***Nuba*** is the second species of *tereniabin* or manna, of a rosy color, such as is said to fall in Ireland. <note>"Tereniabin" is a Persian-derived term for a type of sugary manna found on plants.</note>

***Nymphs*** are spiritual men and women, or bodily spirits inhabiting the waters, such as was Melusina. <note>Melusina is a figure of European folklore, a feminine spirit of fresh water in sacred springs and rivers, often depicted as a woman with a fish or serpent tail.</note>

***Nymphidica*** is a term that can be used metaphorically in the Art for the spirits of dissolving waters.

# O.

***Obrizum*** is pure gold that has been chemically calcined or powdered into a reddish-brown color.

***Odorifera*** are medicines that expel diseases through their scent, whether pleasant or foul.

***Burning Oil*** <note>original: "Oleum ardens"</note> is oil of tartar <note>A concentrated solution of potassium carbonate.</note> purified to the highest degree.

***The Oiliness of things*** <note>original: "Oleitas rerum"</note>: in all things, their sulfur is their oiliness. <note>According to the Paracelsian theory of the "Three Primes," Sulfur represented the oily, flammable, and soul-like principle of a substance.</note>

# CHEMICAL DICTIONARY

<header>CHEMICAL DICTIONARY</header>

<page-num>150</page-num>

***Gold-enriched oil of vitriol*** <note>original: "Oleum vitrioli aurificatum"</note> is that which has been skillfully sweetened with gold. <note>Oil of vitriol is the archaic name for concentrated sulfuric acid.</note>

***Oil of colcothar*** <note>original: "Oleum colcotharinum"</note> is the red oil derived from vitriol. <note>Colcothar is the reddish-brown iron oxide residue remaining after the distillation of vitriol.</note>

***Olympic spirit*** <note>original: "Olympicvs spiritus"</note> is the "star" within a human being, which causes one to cast a shadow of oneself. <note>In Paracelsian philosophy, the "spiritus olympicus" relates to the sidereal or astral body that exists alongside the physical body.</note>

***Opermethiolim*** is a mineralized spirit.

***Helpful remedies*** <note>original: "Opitulativa"</note> are medicines that bind and block discharges <note>original: "fluxus"</note>.

***Opodeldoc*** <note>original: "Oppodeltoch"</note> is the primary ointment of Paracelsus. A plaster for stings or wounds. <note>original: "Stichpflaster"</note>

***Hordeolum*** <note>original: "Ordoleum"</note> is a small abscess resembling a grain of barley. <note>Commonly known today as a stye on the eyelid.</note>

***Orexis*** is a burning heat excited by tartar. <note>In alchemical medicine, "tartar" refers to the stony or calcified waste products that accumulate in the body’s "laboratories" (organs).</note>

***Organopeotica*** is the art of inventing military instruments.

***Orizeum*** is gold.

***Leaf gold*** <note>original: "Orizeum foliatum"</note> is gold beaten with a hammer into the thinnest sheets.

***Precipitated gold*** <note>original: "Orizeum præcipitatum"</note> is gold reduced to its "saffron" <note>original: "crocum"</note> with the help of the "saffron of mercury." <note>"Crocus" in alchemy refers to a fine powder, usually an oxide, named for its yellow or reddish-orange color.</note>

***Orobo*** is metallic glass.

***Ossa paralleli*** is a universal medicine for gout. <note>original: "podagra"</note>

***Obelchera*** or ***obelkara*** is a Cucurbit. <note>A gourd-shaped glass vessel used as the lower part of a still.</note>

***Ocob***, ***ocab***, or ***ocop*** is Sal Ammoniac. <note>Ammonium chloride, a salt used to clean metals and in various chemical reactions.</note>

***Ochre*** <note>original: "Ochra"</note>: Theophrastus <note>An ancient Greek successor to Aristotle known for his work on stones and plants.</note> simply describes ochre as a yellow earth, and lists various types based on their location, saying: The best pigments are born in silver and gold mines, namely *caeruleum* <note>blue</note>, which is properly a slime. The best of these is called Attic; the next is marble-like; the third is "pressed Cyricum." If we consider it closely, we have four species of Ochre.

1. Stony ochre from mines, and on the banks and shores of rivers.

2. It is a powder and dry earth clinging to stones, like *caeruleum* <note>blue pigment</note> and Chrysocolla <note>a green copper-based mineral often called "gold-glue"</note>.

<meta>catchword: 3. Est (It is)</meta>

3. It is a simple dry earth found on riverbanks.
4. It is either muddy and sticky or marble-like from mines. It has medicinal power: to bind, to erode, and to dissipate. Furthermore, from burnt <term>ochre</term> <note>original: "ochra"</note> comes <term>ruddle</term> <note>original: "rubrica fabrilis"; a red earth used by carpenters for marking.</note>. The artificial kind is made from lead. Painters use <term>soda</term> <note>original: "Nitrum"; in this period, "nitrum" often referred to natron or soda rather than saltpeter.</note> for this purpose more than the native variety.

***The West*** <note>original: "Occidens"</note> is vinegar.

***The Western Star*** <note>original: "Occidens stella"</note> is sal ammoniac.

***The West*** <note>original: "Occidens"</note> is Mercury, Saturn, the <term>Hermaphrodite</term> <note>A symbolic name for the union of sulfur and mercury.</note>, living water, viscous water, etc.

***Dissolved oils*** <note>original: "Olea soluta"</note> are those which have separated through the <term>liquefaction</term> <note>original: "deliquium"; the process of a substance absorbing atmospheric moisture until it dissolves.</note> of subtle oily parts. They are made in the same way as the <term>magisteries</term> <note>original: "magisteria"; a "mastery" is a preparation where a substance is purified or concentrated without losing its essential nature.</note> of liquids, except that in a mastery the entire substance is dissolved into any liquid, whereas here only the internal essential parts are turned into a fatty juice, leaving behind the coarse parts. It is necessary for the preparation to proceed so that they can flow down and yield their oiliness of themselves; hence they are generally prepared in the manner of masteries—such as by calcination, maceration, boiling, etc.—or even from the three juices, extracts, and the like, except that an extract should be natural and of such thinness that it flows easily without prolonged effort.

***Oil of grain*** <note>original: "Oleum frumenti"</note> is prepared in two ways.

***Palestinian oil*** <note>original: "Oleum Palæstinum"</note> is Vinegar.

***Fixed oil*** <note>original: "Oleum fixum"</note> is that which is reduced to an oily consistency from dissolved and purely filtered substances, primarily by the drawing off of a <unclear>sieve</unclear> <note>original: "cribrum"; this may be an OCR error for a term related to the distillation or separation of alcohol.</note> of spirit of wine. For we call "fixing" here the production of an oily and stable substance from a dilute and thin consistency by drawing off or digesting the wateriness. Therefore, it is necessary for the substance to be dissolved in a liquid so that, once the coarse parts are separated by filtration, the thin parts are deposited into a fluid, from which they are brought back through such fixing. The common practice is for the substance to be dissolved in some liquid, employing macerations or boilings.

...either by <term>liquefaction</term> <note>original: "deliquium"; the process of a substance absorbing moisture from the air until it dissolves.</note> alone, or with other substances, etc. If the solution contains any dregs, let it be strained sufficiently. Afterward, <term>spirit of wine</term> <note>High-proof alcohol used as a solvent.</note> is poured on and they are digested together; the spirit is then drawn off again, and this process is repeated until the oily consistency is pleasing to the operator.

***Distilled oil*** <note>original: "Oleum distillatum"</note> is that which is extracted through distillation. This is done either by <term>ascent</term> <note>Vapors rising into a still-head.</note> or <term>descent</term> <note>Vapors or liquids driven downward by heat applied from above.</note>, whether the oil is innate to the substance or has been soaked into it. Everything that is soaked in is drawn out by distillation alone, but innate oil can also be separated by other methods.

***Distilled oil by descent*** <note>original: "Oleum stillatitium decensorium"</note> is that which is produced by distillation through a downward path.

***Oil extracted and boiled out through decoction*** <note>original: "Oleum per elixationem"</note> is that which is extracted by cooking. This is done either by boiling in a liquid or by the application of a dry flame.

***Oil from roasting*** <note>original: "Oleum ex assatione"</note> is made by a dry flame when substances, by themselves and without any <term>solvent</term> <note>original: "menstruo"</note>, are exposed to fiery heat and ignite the oil; this is then collected by any available means.

It is performed in two ways:
- By Roasting
- By Combustion

***Onyx*** is a gemstone representing the whiteness of a fingernail <note>The name "Onyx" comes from the Greek word for fingernail or claw.</note>. The Indian variety is sometimes fiery, black, or horn-colored, surrounded by white veins; sometimes these appear like eyes or slanted veins. They say that the **Onyx** is formed and petrifies from the "tears" <note>Sap or resin.</note> of a tree. When burned by fire, it gives off an odor and appears in various impressions and forms.

**Albertus** <note>Likely referring to the medieval scholar Albertus Magnus, who wrote extensively on minerals.</note> **identifies these species of Onyx:**
- Black
- Reddish
- White

When all of these are rubbed, they dissolve into a substance that resembles a human fingernail. **Onyx** excites sadness and fears: it stirs up melancholy, provokes quarrels and turbulent dreams, etc. **Rulandus** <note>Martin Ruland the Elder, author of a famous 1612 alchemical lexicon.</note> on page 354 lists eight types of **Onyx**.

***Opals*** find their true motherland in India. It is a precious gemstone in which the fire of a small <term>carbuncle</term> <note>A red gemstone, usually a garnet.</note> appears more delicate, and—

## P.

**Pandalitium**, *paneritium*, or *passa* <note>original: "Pandalitium, paneritium, vel passa"</note>, is an abscess <note>An "apostema" or gathering of pus.</note> at the tips of the fingers.
***Palantina*** and ***perfina*** were **Queens**, such as ***Melusina*** and ***Melora*** <note>These names refer to legendary or elemental female spirits often associated with folklore and alchemy; Melusina is a famous freshwater spirit of European legends.</note>.
***Pannus*** <note>literally "cloth"</note> is a birthmark or blemish present from birth.
**Part with part** <note>original: "pars cum parte"</note> is a mass of gold and silver cemented and refined in equal portions.
***Parthenion*** is the more noble chamomile, or **Roman chamomile**, which is also called *pyrola sylvana* <note>wild wintergreen</note>.
***Parædri*** are evil **Genii** <note>Attendant spirits or demons believed to assist a person, here specified as malevolent.</note>.
**Loaves** <note>original: "panes"</note> of copper that are crumbling or decaying.
**Loaves** smelted from **pyrite** and **Cadmia** <note>A term used for various zinc-rich ores or furnace residues.</note>.
**Silver bread** <note>original: "panis Argenteus"</note>.
There are three types of copper **loaves**.
**Loaves** made from mined copper.
The **father** is **Sulphur**.
**The Father before the son** <note>original: "Pater ante filium"</note>, that is, the killing of the father. It is the herb ***Satyrion*** <note>A name for various orchids, historically associated with aphrodisiac properties and "killing" the parent bulb as the new one grows.</note>.
**Father and mother of the king**. See more in **Ruland** <note>Martin Ruland the Elder (1532–1602), author of a famous alchemical lexicon.</note>, page 359.
***Patias*** is a scale <note>Likely referring to metal scales or dross.</note>.
***Pedagra*** <note>A play on "podagra" or gout</note> is **Tartar** <note>In Paracelsian medicine, "Tartar" refers to stony deposits in the body thought to cause disease.</note>.
***Pegernus***, meaning **stranger** or **traveler**, is **Mercury**.
The **pelican** <note>original: "pelecanus"</note> is a **circulating vessel** <note>A type of digestive flask used for continuous distillation.</note>, named after the shape of the **Pelican** pecking its own breast to feed its young with its blood. It has a wide belly that gradually narrows into a thin neck, which then curves back around to re-insert its mouth into the belly. This vessel has a channel at the bottom through which liquid is poured; once the liquid is inside, it is closed with the **Hermetic seal** <note>An airtight seal made by melting the glass neck of the vessel.</note> and moved to the heat.
***Pelicide*** or ***pelude*** is boiled honey.
**Penetration** <note>original: "penetratio"</note> is submersion.

**Phthisis** <note>A term for consumption or tuberculosis.</note> or **wasting** <note>original: "tabes"</note> is otherwise almost the same as **Atrophy**: a cessation of nourishment, arising either from fear or from excessive greed.
**Phthisis** is the consumption of the body’s natural humors resulting from an ulcer of the lung.

**Phthisis** differs from **Hectic fever** by:
{ Subject,
{ Genus <note>Type or category.</note>.
{ Accident <note>Specific symptoms or occurrences.</note>.

1. In **Subject**: **Hectic fever** is a disease of the Heart; **Phthisis** is of the Lung.
2. In **Genus**: **Hectic** is a Fever; **Phthisis** is not.
3. In **Accident**: **Hectic** is a general disease with respect to the Heart; **Phthisis** is a specific disease of the Lung.

***Pilos*** is clay or moist earth.
**A pestle** <note>original: "Pilum"</note>, or the head of a pestle, with which loaves of copper are broken.
***Pissare*** means to strain or filter. ***Pistare*** means to pound or crush.
**Liquid pitch** is turpentine. ***Pixari*** is pitch.
**Lead** is assigned to **Saturn** by the Chemists and is called by his name. It is a metallic body, leaden-colored, earthy, and heavy, possessing a small amount of whiteness; it contains much earthy substance and is turned into **tin** through washing. By this, it is evident that tin is more "perfect" than lead. Lead has more fixed sulfur in its composition than **Jupiter** (that is, tin). In short, mercury <note>original: "argentum vivum," literally "living silver."</note>, a thick, bad...

...of a bad taste, foul-smelling, of weak power, like a menstruous mother, it is entered by a sulfur husband, leprous or leaden-colored: and that cold Saturnine son is born, whose name is lead. It lacks purity, stability <note>original: "fixatio"</note>, color, and the ability to be ignited. Note that pure lead of any color is not found in mines; and lead that is smelted is of three kinds:

-> Namely: <-
{ 1. Black, which is true lead:
{ 2. then Ash-colored,
{ 3. and White <note>likely referring to tin, which was often called "white lead" (plumbum album) in antiquity.</note>.

Black lead is now discussed, called *molybdon* <note>original: "μόλυβδον"</note> by the Greeks. It is produced and smelted from galena <note>Lead sulfide, the primary ore of lead.</note>, or it is made from pyrite. Washed lead is kept in certain workshops <note>Apothecaries or chemical laboratories.</note> for use against inflamed ulcers and cancer; but calcined <note>original: "crematum," meaning burned or reduced to ash through heat.</note> lead has more effective powers than washed lead, and sugar of lead <note>original: "saccharum Saturni," lead acetate, named for its sweet taste despite being highly toxic.</note> is more effective than both. For potters use calcined lead most frequently for glazing <note>original: "pingenda," literally "painting."</note> vessels. You may see more, if you please, concerning the types of lead in Ruland’s work, from page 364 to 370 <note>Refers to Martin Ruland the Elder's "Lexicon Alchemiae" (1612), a standard reference for alchemical terms.</note>.

***Rich lead*** <note>original: "Plumbum, dives." Lead containing a high proportion of silver.</note>.
***Impoverished lead*** <note>original: "Plumbum, depauperatum." Lead from which the silver has been extracted.</note>.

**Plumbago**, **Galena molybdena**, or **lead-stone**—the source from which lead is made—are one and the same. It is rightly called lead-stone, lead-veins, lead-earth, and the matter from which lead is made. It has the same medicinal powers as washed lead and lead dross <note>original: "recremento," the waste or slag from the smelting process.</note>, and it is washed in the same way; it differs from the *Molybdena* of Dioscorides <note>A 1st-century Greek physician and author of the pharmacological treatise "De Materia Medica."</note> only in color, not in substance. For the color in plumbago can be easily changed by various vapors stirred up from the bowels of the earth. If you would make a distinction: call the lead-stone "molybdena" when it contains only lead; on the contrary, when it contains lead and silver, call it "galena."

It is twofold, { Native, & / Artificial.

Concerning these and their various species, you may see more in Ruland, from page 372 to page 376 <note>Refers to Martin Ruland the Elder's *Lexicon Alchemiae* (1612), a standard reference for alchemical and metallurgical terms.</note>.

**Pompholix** <note>A white zinc oxide or metallic "flower" that sublimates during smelting.</note>: **Dioscorides** <note>A famous Greek physician and botanist (c. 40–90 AD).</note> joins together *pompholix*, *spodion*, and *antispodon*.

1. **Pompholix** is a metallic ash that is formed at the top, or on the walls of furnaces, or in the small rooms where furnaces are, once the embers are extinguished. The ash differs according to the variety of the metals and the place where it is produced. For that which is ash-colored and made from pyrite <note>Iron disulfide, often called "fool's gold," though here used as a source of other minerals.</note> is more effective than that made from lead-stone. Because of this, the best of all is from Meissen <note>original: "Misnensium"; Meissen was a major mining and metallurgical center in Saxony.</note>, containing within it silver, lead, and copper. If the *pompholix* is on the sides and the top of the chambers, the part which hangs from the ceiling is *pompholix*, and it differs from *spodion* in its whiteness and lightness. On the contrary, the part that is darker and heavier, settling in the lower places, is *spodion*. *Pompholix* differs from *spodion* in appearance only, not in kind. The Arabs distinguish two types of **Tutty** <note>original: "Tutia"; an impure form of zinc oxide used since antiquity in eye salves.</note>.

1. **Native**, found in mines: white, green, and lemon-yellow, found on the shores of the Indian Sea.
2. **Artificial**, produced in furnaces, which is discussed here.

The Arabs have two types of **Spodion**.
1. From the root of burnt overseas cane <note>likely referring to *Tabasheer*, a silica deposit found in the joints of bamboo, used in medieval medicine.</note>.
2. That which is made from true burnt ivory. There is also *spodos* <note>Greek for "ash."</note> burnt from the bones of elephants, dogs, and other animals until it reaches a state of lightness, whereas it truly ought to be made from burnt ivory.

<margin>*Dioscorides*</margin>
**Antispodium** <note>A substitute or "counter-ash" used when true Spodium was unavailable.</note> is made from small styptic pebbles <note>original: "cauticulis stipticis"; "styptic" refers to substances that shrink tissues or stop bleeding.</note>. According to—

**Spodion** can be made from lead in the same way that it is made from Cyprian copper. In Meissen, it is similar to lead in the workshops where lead is smelted <note>original: "coquitur," literally "cooked" or boiled.</note> most frequently.

1. **Pompholix** is the soot that is collected in the chambers where metal is smelted.

2. **Pompholix** <note>A white metallic ash or oxide, usually zinc oxide, collected during smelting.</note>, collected from silver.
3. **Yellow [Pompholix]** <note>original: "Lutea"</note>, which sticks to the walls when silver is separated from lead.
4. **Pompholix**, from Mansfeld copper <note>Mansfeld, in modern Germany, was one of the most famous copper-mining regions in Europe.</note>.
5. That which is collected when silver is separated from copper.
6. That which is collected in the chimneys where white lead <note>Likely referring to tin (*plumbum candidum*).</note> is smelted.
7. Most pure **white** [Pompholix].
8. Formed into crusts from roasted "stone loaves" <note>Slag cakes or "mattes" produced during the smelting process.</note> when copper is smelted.
9. **White Pompholix**, which the workshops call "white nothing" <note>original: "nihilum album"; so named because the light, airy flakes of zinc oxide seemed to have no weight.</note>.

*Pompholix*, *Tutty* <note>original: "Tutiae"; an impure zinc oxide often used in medicinal eye salves.</note>, and *spodios* <note>original: "spodius"; a heavier, darker metallic residue.</note> are one and the same thing.

*Poponac* <note>likely Opopanax.</note> is a gum.

*Porfitigon* is iron scales <note>The flakes that fly off iron when it is hammered while hot.</note>.

*Prasis*, or *Prasimum green*, is the "flower of copper" <note>original: "flos æris"; likely verdigris or a green copper carbonate.</note>.

**Precipitation** <note>original: "Præcipitatio"</note>: when bodies are corroded by "corrosive waters" <note>Mineral acids like nitric or hydrochloric acid.</note>, dissolved in that water, and then—either by the removal of the corrosive water or by some other procedure <term>Encherema: a chemical operation or manual manipulation</term>—they are "struck back" into a certain powder <note>original: "calcem" (lime/calx); early chemists used this term for any fine powder produced by heat or acid.</note>. Thus, silver dissolved in *aqua fortis* <note>Nitric acid.</note> is precipitated by throwing in copper, common salt, or sal ammoniac. Thus gold dissolved in *aqua regia* <note>A mixture of nitric and hydrochloric acids capable of dissolving gold.</note> is "struck back" by applying a little quicksilver <note>Mercury.</note>; if a little sulfur leaf is mixed into this, and it is placed in a closed vessel over a fire so that the quicksilver and sulfur can evaporate and depart, a most subtle powder will remain. A similar judgment should be held concerning other substances. But the more thoroughly bodies are dissolved, the more finely and subtly they are calcined <note>Reduced to powder.</note>.

*Presmucum* is white lead <note>original: "Cerusa"; basic lead carbonate.</note>.

**Chemical Principles.**
**Principles** are those things from which a matter in any kind of mixture is made; because they are present within, and [already possess] a certain [limit] for themselves...

It begins to define the "Principles of Chemistry," the fundamental components of all mixed bodies.</summary>

<page-num>159</page-num>

[...] have an underlying nature in which they thrive, and from which they can be drawn out. These principles are different from those of **Aristotle** <note>Aristotle's four elements were Earth, Air, Fire, and Water; the author is distinguishing them from the three alchemical principles.</note>, and they are not understood as the primary origins of all things. Instead, they are the primary principles within any specific category, although they are analogous to the Aristotelian elements. Furthermore, the powers of these principles are founded both in their elemental parts and in the celestial. Thus, in one way they approach the nature of the elements, and in another, they approach the nature of essences <note>original: "essentias"; the pure, spiritual core of a substance.</note>.

The three principles are:
*   **Mercury**,
*   **Salt**,
*   **Sulfur**.

### *The primary constitution.*

**Projection** <note>original: "Projectio"</note> is the exaltation and transformation of a substance by means of a "medicine" <note>The Philosopher's Stone or a transmuting powder.</note> thrown upon the matter to be changed, resulting in a sudden entry. It is similar to fermentation because it changes the internal substance of the matter; however, it differs because it is not achieved through slow "digestion" <note>A process of gentle, prolonged heating.</note>, where the mixed materials are altered gradually to reach a balanced temperament <note>original: "crasin".</note>. Instead, it occurs through a violent penetration, transfiguring the matter almost at the moment of entry. This medicine is also called a **tincture** rather than a ferment.

**Drawing forth** <note>original: "Prolectatio"; a method of extraction or attraction.</note> is an extraction performed by the thinning out of subtle parts, so that once they are made rare <note>Less dense or more spiritual.</note> by the inclination of their own nature, they are carried away from the coarser parts in a different direction and settle there. Therefore, the substances from which we draw something forth are called "thinners" or "rarefiers." Before the extracted part settles, it is supplied with a fiery, airy, or watery form, so that through the mediation of these elements, the drawing forth occurs, and it separates from the fixed earthy parts by the power of its own lightness or flow.

**Sand** <note>original: "Psamos"; from the Greek *psammos*.</note> is arena.
**White lead** <note>original: "Psincus" or "Psinkus"; also known as ceruse.</note> is Cerusa.
**Cold** <note>original: "Psyathos"; likely from the Greek *psychos*.</note> is chill.
**Pumice stone** <note>original: "Pumex"</note>, called **Samechi** or **Senech** in Arabic, or the **stone of Vulcan** <note>Vulcan was the Roman god of fire and smithing; pumice is a volcanic rock.</note>.

There are two species of it:
*   **Black**,
*   **&**
*   **White.**

<meta>catchword: Black-</meta>

<summary>This page defines the "Tria Prima" (Mercury, Salt, Sulfur) as the fundamental chemical principles and explains the sudden transformative process of "Projection" compared to slower "Drawing forth" or extraction methods. It also provides a glossary of mineral and physical terms like sand, white lead, and pumice.</summary>

<keywords>Tria Prima, Mercury, Salt, Sulfur, Projection, Extraction, Tincture, White Lead, Pumice, Stone of Vulcan</keywords>

--- Page 214 ---
<meta>This page continues from the previous entries in the chemical dictionary. Although the previous page ended with an entry starting with 'D', this page appears to be continuing an entry for "Pumex" (Pumice) under the letter 'P', before moving on to "Purifier" and "Putrefaction."</meta>

# CHEMICAL DICTIONARY

<page-num>160</page-num>

Black <note>referring to the black variety of pumice stone</note> is also called *tufa* <note>original: "tophus"; a porous rock often formed from volcanic ash</note>, and this is most excellent in Melos and the Aeolian Islands <note>islands near Sicily known for volcanic activity</note>, being very suitable for smoothing bodies.

The white variety is light, spongy, and easily split; it is not sandy, it is easily ground down, and it is bright white. It is called pumice stone—called a "stone" because of its hardness, but not because of its density; for in its porosity it is not like a stone, and it clearly deviates from the nature of a stone because it floats on water. Pumice is found where **Alum** <term>Alumen; a versatile mineral salt used in dyeing and medicine</term> and **Sulfur** are born. It is believed to be earth that has been entirely burnt up on islands, which is then carried by the waters as far as the city of Barce in Libya. Thus it is called the "stone of Barce."

**The Purifier of Silver.** <note>original: "Purgator, Argenti"; likely referring to a refining agent or process for silver.</note>

*Putrefaction* <term>Putrefactio; in alchemy, this is the "Nigredo" or blackening stage where matter is broken down to be reborn</term> is the breakdown of a compound through natural decay in a warm, moist environment. For it is necessary for the moisture to overcome the confining dryness through the action of external heat. When this happens, the innate heat, along with its substantial moisture, is separated from the combined parts and preserves its own homogeneity, standing apart. Therefore, if the substance to be putrefied abounds in moisture, it is left crushed within that very moisture and placed for "digestion" in warm dung or a warm bath—that is, with moist heat applied from the outside. If, however, the substance itself has little or no moisture of its own, it is ground up and sprinkled with a proportional amount of moisture, and then placed in its vessel to putrefy.

*Putrefaction* is a digestion that dissolves the substance of a thing by retaining vapors and adding external heat, in order to generate a more excellent thing. It is characteristic of putrefaction to consume the old nature of things and introduce a new one, and sometimes to produce the fruit of a different generation. Even corrosive spirits <note>Strong acids or reactive volatile liquids</note> become sweet and mild through it; all colors are changed into others; likewise, the pure is separated from the impure, with the impure sinking to the bottom. This occurs with the material placed in a vessel, the vessel placed in dung <note>A common alchemical heat source, as fermenting manure provides a steady, gentle warmth</note>, and the heat of the dung maintained for a fixed time. However, it is better to place the vessel in a vapor bath <note>original: "balneum roris"; literally "bath of dew," referring to a gentle steam bath</note>.

<meta>catchword: Putre-</meta>

<summary>This page defines the varieties and origins of pumice stone before providing a detailed explanation of putrefaction as a transformative alchemical process. It emphasizes putrefaction as a method of breaking down old forms to generate more refined substances using controlled, moist heat.</summary>

<keywords>pumice, tufa, Barce, silver purification, putrefaction, digestion, dung bath, vapor bath, chemical transformation</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the alphabetical entries of the chemical dictionary, specifically focusing on terms beginning with the letter 'P'. The previous page concluded with a definition of putrefaction as a moist process; this page begins by defining a "dry" version of that same process.</meta>

# CHEMICAL DICTIONARY

<page-num>161</page-num>

**Another kind of Putrefaction** <term>Putrefactio alia</term> is a state that hovers between corrosive calcination <note>the process of reducing a substance to powder through heat or chemicals</note> and standard putrefaction. It is known as "dry and philosophical putrefaction." It receives various names, not without reason, such as sublimation, the separation of elements, cooking, or solution. This process takes place in the "dry water of the philosophers," or the sharpest vinegar <note>likely referring to a powerful solvent or acid</note>; it is nothing other than the refinement of **Gold and Silver** <note>original: "Solis & Lunae"; the Sun and Moon are the standard alchemical symbols for gold and silver</note>.

**Penates** are said to be spirits of the element of fire and of the heavens. They are called "household spirits," appearing as if they were born alongside us <note>original: "penes nos nati"; a folk etymology for the Roman household gods</note>.

**Pentacles** <term>Pentacula</term> are signs, seals, or sketches occasionally entwined with marvelous and unknown letters and characters. When hung around the neck, they are believed to preserve the wearer from evil spirits and from any kind of spell or fascination.

**Perdettae** are small, yellow turnips.

**Perdonium** is a wine made from herbs.

**Period** <term>Periodus</term> is a cell <note>original: "celia"</note> or the "muscle of life"—a pulse running throughout the entire body.

**Peucedanum** is wild angelica <note>also known as hog's fennel</note>.

**Pilizenii** are the hairs that grow around the tails of hares.

**Pilosella** is mouse-ear, a hairy herb.

**Pinguedinosa** is a term for any kind of fatty or oily substance.

**Philosophers' Lead** <term>Plumbum Philosophorum</term> is a substance extracted from antimony <note>a brittle, silvery-white metalloid used in alchemical alloys</note>.

**Potables** <term>Potabilia</term> are things that can be swallowed by mouth.

**Gout** <term>Podagra</term> is the "tartar of finonia" <note>a Paracelsian term for the fluid or 'glue' in the joints</note> or of white glue.

**Phantasms** <term>Phantasmata</term> are forest spirits that are not diabolical in nature. They dwell in deserts and along the sea shores. They most frequently speak with imaginative people and occasionally cause terror. They derive their origin from the imaginations that living people have concerning or in connection with the dead.

Regarding these, read more extensively in **Paracelsus** <note>Theophrastus von Hohenheim, a Swiss physician and alchemist</note> in the first exposition he makes on the *Gospel of Saint Matthew, Chapter 14*.

**Phionitides** is the name given to natural enemies who mutually pursue one another unto death, such as the stork and frogs, the cat and mice, or the spider and toads.

<meta>catchword: Phoenix,</meta>

->**LEXICON CHYMICUM**<-

**Phoenix** <note>original: "Phænix"</note>: Said to be the quintessence of fire, and also that most celebrated **Stone of the Philosophers**.

**Physiognomy** <note>original: "physiognomia"</note>: The art by which a person’s character and nature are understood from the face down to the chest, so that a judgment of each individual's intellect and disposition may be made from their appearance.

**Pulpezia**, or **pulpelxia**: Stupor, being thunderstruck, apoplexy, or "sideration" <note>a sudden paralysis or stroke once believed to be caused by the malevolent influence of the stars or planets</note>.

**Presage** <note>original: "Præsagium"</note>: A notable sign preceding the history of a future event, from which prophecy is sometimes derived.

**Preservatives** <note>original: "Præservativa"</note>: Medicines that defend life from its enemies and from decay.

**Press** <note>original: "Pressorium"</note>: A squeezer or wine-press; an instrument by which crushed substances <note>original: "species"</note> are squeezed out.

**Primales**: Deceptive bodies placed before our eyes by the stars, about which Paracelsus <note>a famous Swiss physician and alchemist</note> discusses more extensively in the same section on the Gospel of Matthew; they are elsewhere called *Prumalia*.

**Pruinum**: The first form of tartar <note>the crystalline crust that forms in wine casks, used in alchemical preparations</note>.

**Pruina**, or rather **Pruna**: "Persian fire" <note>an old medical term for skin conditions such as shingles or anthrax ulcers; the author is correcting the term 'pruina' (frost) to 'pruna' (burning coal)</note>.

**Pulsating** <note>original: "pulsatiles"</note>: A term applied to arteries and veins, perhaps referring to "pneumatic" <note>meaning vessels that carry the 'pneuma' or vital spirit</note>.

**Pusta**: The digestion of discharge, named after the pus itself.

**Rottenness** <note>original: "putredo"</note>: The primary matter of generation <note>the alchemical belief that new life or substances must begin with the decay of the old</note>; or better, **Putrefaction** <note>original: "putrefactio"</note> is taken to mean the corruption occurring during an alteration.

**Pygmies** <note>original: "Pygmei"</note>: Called "little men" or subterranean spirits, whom they also call "torches" or "sparks." It is claimed they are not born and have no parents, but are generated from the corruption of the earth in the same way that beetles are born from rotted horse dung.

**Pyromancy** <note>original: "Pyromantia"</note>: The art of predicting the future from fire, occurring when the stars of the fire manifest themselves openly to humans, so that knowledge of the truth being sought may emerge, removing any doubt or suspicion. An example is what is said of the boxwood tree and its leaves being thrown into the fire, with each of them...

*Pygmies*, little men or subterranean spirits are so called, whom they also call "torches" or "sparks." They are said not to be born, nor to have parents, but to be generated in a much different way from the corruption of the earth, just as beetles are generated from putrefied horse manure.

*Pyromancy*, the art of presage from fire is so called, when the "fire-stars" <note>igneous phenomena</note> make themselves plain to men, so that from there a notice of what is sought may break forth, with any doubt or suspicion removed. # CHEMICAL DICTIONARY

<page-num>164.</page-num>

->### CHEMICAL DICTIONARY ###<-

...hangs down is *pompholyx* <note>original: "pompholyx"; a white zinc oxide also known as "philosopher's wool" or "white nihil," which collects at the top of a furnace</note>; on the other hand, the part that adheres to the walls is *spodion* <note>original: "spodion"; a heavier, darker furnace residue</note>. Young students ought to observe these diligently. The *Arabs* identify two types of Tutty <note>original: "Tutia"; an impure form of zinc oxide used in alchemy and medicine</note>:
1. Native, found in mines, being white, green, and yellow; also found on the shores of the Indian Sea.
2. Factitious <note>man-made or artificial</note>, created in furnaces, which is discussed here.

Spodion <note>ashy residue</note> is made:
1. From the burnt root of a cane.
2. From truly burnt ivory.
3. From the bones of elephants, dogs, and other animals. This *spodos* <note>Greek for "ashes"</note> is burned until it is light and airy; it ought to be made from burnt ivory. *Antispodium* <note>a medicinal substitute for spodium</note> is made from certain astringent pebbles, according to *Dioscorides* <note>a famous Greek physician and botanist</note>.

*Pyrites* and *Marcasite* are the same thing according to the Greeks and Arabs. It is called *pyrites* because a great deal of fire is struck from it <note>from the Greek "pyr" meaning fire</note>; and soldiers use it to ignite their bombards <note>early forms of cannons or firearms</note>. It is also called *pyrites* because it often has a fiery color.

Various species of *Pyrites* are found in the mines of Germany:
1. *Pyrites* of a silver color, which contains silver and copper; sometimes only silver, sometimes only copper, sometimes silver and copper together, sometimes silver and black lead <note>likely galena or lead ore</note>, sometimes several metals, and sometimes it is found to be entirely sterile. However, that specific pyrite seems to contain more quicksilver <note>mercury</note> than the other species.
2. *Pyrites* of a nearly golden color; this is properly called *pyrites*, and is a "copper pyrite" related to the first type.
3. *Pyrites* of a thoroughly golden color, which seems to contain more sulfur; sometimes the following fourth species is mixed with it.
4. *Pyrites* similar in color to *galena* <note>lead ore</note>, but it differs entirely in its material. Gold and silver are smelted from it, and it is found in great abundance at Reichenstein <note>a mining town in Silesia, now Złoty Stok, Poland</note>, where it is remarkably fine...

...while [some] contains more gold and less silver, or gold alone; but the pyrites of *Rauris* <note>a valley in the Austrian Alps famous for its gold mines</note> contains more silver and less gold.

5. *Pyrites* of an ash-gray color, found in a very fine form at *Reichenstein* <note>modern-day Złoty Stok in Poland, historically a major site for gold and arsenic mining</note>; it is mixed with the preceding type so that gold can be smelted from it as well. However, this species is found in a slightly different manner at *Altenberg* in *Silesia* <note>a historical region in Central Europe</note>, from which gold and silver are smelted together.

6. *Pyrites* of an iron color, which by its nature has the appearance of a mass of iron; it is found in certain iron mines, though not in all.

Pyrites has a remarkable power to disperse, cleanse, and produce heat. *Dioscorides* <note>a Greek physician and botanist of the 1st century AD</note> teaches how it should be burned and washed for medicinal use in Book 5, Chapter 89. You may see more regarding the various kinds of pyrites in *Rulandus* <note>Martin Ruland the Elder, author of a famous alchemical lexicon</note> from page 393 to page 396.

*Pyrites* is also the "parent of juices" <note>original: "parens succorum"; in early chemistry, 'juices' referred to liquid minerals, acids, or bitumens</note> [see page 327]. *Pyrites* fertile in various metals [see page 397]. *Pyrites* from which fire is drawn <note>original: "Pyritum è quibus ignis elicitur"</note> [see page 397]. Of a *silver* color [see page 398].

*Pyramis* <note>literally "pyramid"</note> (called by others a cone-shaped crucible) is a metallic vessel, hollowed out in the shape of a pyramid, so that its upper part is wide and its lower part tapers to a sharp point. It is used so that melted minerals can be shaped into a *regulus* <note>the metallic button or mass that settles at the bottom of a crucible after smelting</note>.

---

### Q.

**Qu**andros is a stone or gem found in the brain and head of a vulture, white in color. It is said to fill breasts with milk and to be effective against harmful accidents.

*Quanli* is lead.

*Quars* <note>an early term for Quartz</note> is a gall-stone <note>original: "petra fellis"; likely referring to the yellowish, stone-like appearance of the mineral</note>.

Quartation <note>original: "Quartatio"</note> is the separation of gold and silver when they are mixed together. It is called "quartation" because enough silver is added to the gold and silver mixture so that there are three parts of silver to one part of gold, making four parts in total <note>this ratio allowed the silver to be dissolved by nitric acid, leaving the gold behind, a process that required at least 75% silver to work effectively</note>.

<sig>M</sig>
<meta>catchword: Quinta (Fifth)</meta>

<keywords>pyrites, Reichenstein, Silesia, Rauris, gold refining, Pyramis, Quandros, Quartation, silver separation</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the dictionary entries under the letter Q, moving from the definition of Pyramis (a cone-shaped crucible) into various definitions of the "Fifth Essence" and other chemical terms beginning with Q.</meta>

# CHEMICAL DICTIONARY
<page-num>166</page-num>

**Fifth Essence** <note>original: *Quinta essentia*; also known as the Quintessence, this was considered the "fifth element" or the purest, most medicinal core of any substance, purged of all impurities</note> is a mystery, exalted to the parity and most excellent powers of the ethereal nature. It is usually called "heaven" and "celestial substance," in which a most pure and sincere *crasis* <note>from the Greek word for "mixture," referring to the specific balance of qualities or humors</note> resides, along with a substantial root. It is as if it were a flow from the ether or a ray from the firmament, dispersed through the lowest depths by the voice of the Creator, and a radiance <note>original Greek: ἀπαύγασμα (*apaugasma*), meaning an effulgence or reflection</note> of the world soul.

**Fifth essence of plants** is that which is extracted from vegetables and their parts. This is primarily produced from the extracted juice. The foremost place here is held by the fifth essence of wine, which Lullius <note>Ramon Llull, a 13th-century Majorcan polymath and alchemist</note> calls "Vegetable Mercury." Others call it the "water of the first being," the "heaven," and the "key of the Philosophers."

**Fifth essence of animals** is that which is extracted from the parts of animals by means of natural and vegetable mixture. Thus, the practice differs little from the preparation of plant [essences].

**Fifth essence of minerals** is that which is prepared from minerals.

The fifth essences of metals and gems are superior among these.

**Quebricum** is arsenic.

**Quebrit** and **Quibrith** is sulfur. <note>these terms derive from the Arabic word *kibrit*, meaning sulfur</note>

**Quianos**, that is, a "vein of the earth." Copper-like.

**St. Quirinus’s plague** <note>original: *Quirini sancti plaga*; likely a reference to a specific type of skin disease or ulcerated wound named after the martyr St. Quirinus</note>, a disease described by Paracelsus <note>Theophrastus von Hohenheim, the 16th-century founder of iatrochemistry (chemistry used for medicine)</note> in Volume 2, page 357.

**Quality** is what we call a "complexion," whether hot or cold. Thus far, four qualities have been proclaimed by the mouths of all and assigned to the four elements, although they are nothing less than the elements themselves; nor are those [abstract] qualities, but rather everything generated from the elements is either cold or hot. If it is cold, it possesses within itself a certain fiery and indivisible humidity; hence, wherever there is humidity, there must also be cold, and not otherwise...

# CHEMICAL DICTIONARY
<page-num>167</page-num>

...judgment must be made concerning the hot and the dry. For dryness belongs only to heat, nor can it happen that the cold be dry, or the hot be moist. These complexions <note>In ancient and medieval medicine, a "complexion" was the specific balance of the four qualities—hot, cold, moist, and dry—that determined the nature of a substance or body.</note> arise from **Ares** <note>A Paracelsian term referring to the spiritual force or "inner worker" that differentiates the elements into specific forms and species.</note>, nature so disposing it that each parent—the male, hot element of fire—possesses a dry sperm, which is air.

Truly the cold mother, Earth, possesses a menstruous seed <note>The "menstruum" in alchemy refers to a solvent or the maternal, receptive principle that nourishes the "seed" of a metal or mineral.</note>, which is water, so that we see these also joined together—the water of its earth—forming one tiny globule, not unlike the air, which is always continuous with its fire, forming one sphere. That sphere of fire and air is larger than the first [of earth and water], but smaller than the firmament; for this reason, it is called the "middle globe" and the middle ethereal region of the elements.

From these two parents, every offspring of elemental nature is generated, under a complexion that is either hot with dry in itself, or dry resolved, or under a complexion that is cold with moist in itself, or with moist coagulated. From this it happens that very often many things are judged to be cold by the eye or by what is manifest, which are actually hot in their hidden nature.

An example for us is **crystal**, appearing cold and dry, yet in the hidden center of its nature it is moist, because it is nothing other than coagulated moisture. Likewise **petroleum** <note>literally "rock oil"; in this period, it referred to various naturally occurring flammable mineral oils</note>, which by its nature is a "resolved dry" substance, even though it appears moist to the eye. Therefore, it is of no small importance for the natural philosopher <term>physico</term> <note>from the Greek *physis* (nature); a scholar who studies the laws and substances of the natural world</note> to properly know the nature and complexion of things, both from their parents and from the external accidents <note>In philosophy, "accidents" are properties that can change without changing the essential nature of the thing, such as its shape or temperature.</note> acquired in their composition. 

These things are also prone to change in artificial solutions and congealings. In short, the labyrinth of degrees among the qualities is so vast that no one, however practiced, can pass through this way to the true goal of restoring health in medicine without the greatest deception. This is even grasped from their own treatises, where what is affirmed in one place is most openly denied in another. Then at last, they flee to the sacred anchor of distinction <note>original: *sacram distinctionis anchoram*; a "sacred anchor" was a classical metaphor for a last resort or a final, unshakable argument used when all else fails</note> as if to an asylum...

<summary>This page explains the elemental theory of "complexions," arguing that substances have hidden qualities (like a "hot" nature inside a "cold" crystal) that differ from their outward appearance. It warns that the complexity of these qualities creates a "labyrinth" that makes medical practice and alchemical study difficult and prone to contradiction.</summary>
