<meta>This page continues the discussion on the qualities and "complexions" of the elements, contrasting the Paracelsian view with traditional Greek (Galenic) medicine. The text concludes the letter Q with definitions for "Quartation" and "Quintessence" before beginning the letter R.</meta>

# CHEMICAL DICTIONARY
<page-num>163</page-num>

...one must have recourse, by which their madness shall be demonstrated. Therefore, the intellect of the physicist and physician is to be judged most fortunate, who, from physical confusion, has known how to turn toward the spagyric <term>Spagyric: A term coined by Paracelsus referring to the alchemical process of "separating and recombining" to create pure medicines.</term> union of things, an infallible path. Thus far it has been spoken concerning qualities, so that the reader may see that the mind of Paracelsus concerning these is one thing, and the opinion of the infidels or Greeks <note>The author refers to "the Greeks" to mean the traditional followers of Aristotle and Galen, whose theories of the four humors dominated medicine before the alchemical revolution.</note> is another.

**Quartation**, is said to be the highest trial of gold, namely in this way: that nine parts of silver are mixed with one part of gold by melting through fire. Thereafter, both are dissolved together by Stygian water <note>original: *aqua stygia*; here referring to nitric acid or a similar corrosive mineral acid.</note> or *aqua fortis* <term>Aqua fortis: Literally "strong water," a historical name for nitric acid, which dissolves silver but leaves gold intact.</term>. All the silver flows into the water, while the gold alone settles at the bottom like a powder, of a brownish and blackish color.

**Quintessence**, is said to be a certain spiritual matter, extracted bodily from all herbs, plants, and all other things having life, and brought to the highest degree of subtlety by separation from all elements and impurities; likewise the powers and virtues, and every incorruptible medicine, hiding within any things whatsoever. It is otherwise called the specific form of things, which, if it is removed from the species, approaches its own genus and proceeds higher until it rests in the center. This is most elegantly denoted by Paracelsus with these three characters: **2. 4. 0.** That is, through the casting away of the confusion, inequality, and impurity of the four elements, one arrives at the doubled quintessence resting in the decade <note>The number ten, representing a return to unity and perfection in mystical mathematics.</note> created under union.

**The fifth being** of any element by itself alone, is the animal produced from that element alone.

### R.

**Ragadiæ**, are nodules or abscesses of the genitals.
***Rana*** <note>literally "frog"</note>, is an abscess under the tongue.
***Rabira, Radira***, is tin.
***Rabiel, Roliel***, is dragon's blood <term>Dragon's blood: A bright red resin obtained from certain plants, used as a pigment and for medicinal properties.</term>.
***Rachi or Raco***, is Mercury.

<meta>This page continues the "R" section of the Chemical Dictionary, transitioning from the previous page's discussion on the Quintessence into specific alchemical terminology and apparatus beginning with the letter R.</meta>

# CHEMICAL DICTIONARY
<page-num>169</page-num>

*Racri Ranac* is **sal ammoniac** <note>original: *sal armoniacus*; ammonium chloride, a volatile salt central to alchemical reactions.</note>.
*Ramigi*, also known as *Ramigiri*, is **colophony** <note>original: *colophonia*; a solid form of resin obtained from pines.</note>.
*Ramag* is **ash**.
*Rafar* or *Rastis* is **tin**.
*Rastol* or *Rafoes* is **copper** <note>original: *æs*; while the Latin term can mean bronze or brass, in this context it usually refers to copper.</note>.
*Rastul* is **salt**.
*Rafaheti* or *Rusatagi* is **burnt copper** <note>original: *æs ustum*; copper oxide, often used as a pigment or for medicinal preparations.</note>.
*Realgar* is **red arsenic** <note>original: *auripigmentum rubeum*; literally "red gold-pigment," a toxic mineral used for its color and chemical properties.</note>.
*Recha* is **marble**.
**Receptacle** (*Receptaculum*) is a large, rounded vessel used for receiving the fluids that drop down during distillation. However, the type of receptacle varies according to the nature of the substance being distilled. For if the substances are vaporous, gaseous, and "spiritual" <note>In alchemy, "spiritual" refers to highly volatile substances that easily turn into gas.</note>, the vessel must be large; otherwise, it will burst into pieces with a great boom and a crack from the immense force of the spirits, and not without danger to the person standing nearby. If, however, the substances are hot and thin, the vessel should be shaped with a long neck. But if the substances are of a medium nature, a medium-sized receptacle is required.
**Iron dross** (*Recrementa ferri*) is **slag** <note>original: *sinder*; the stony waste matter separated from metals during the smelting of ore.</note>.
*Rel* or *rebus* is **acidic matter**.
**Quenching** (*Restinctio*) is a process of refinement where white-hot materials are cooled in an "exalting" liquid <note>A liquid meant to increase the potency or purity of the material.</note>, whereby they reach a state of nobility among the best of their kind; this is the primary and principal purpose for which quenching was invented.
**Retort** (*Retorta*) is a rounded vessel whose neck emerges gradually from the "belly" or body and slowly transitions into a long, bent tube.
**Resolution by medicine** (*Resolutio per medicinam*) occurs when substances that were mixed are forcibly separated by adding a "resolvent" <note>A substance that breaks a compound into its component parts.</note>, which segregates any part of the mixture. Following the teachings of Geber <note>Geber is the Latinized name of Jabir ibn Hayyan, an 8th-century polymath considered the father of chemistry.</note>, we call this substance a "medicine," although if it is a liquid, it could be called a "solvent water." This process happens just as vinegar or similar substances cause the separation of curds in milk. Therefore, the work itself could also be called **empyriasis** <note>original: *empyriasis*; a term related to separation through heat or chemical action.</note>. In some cases, it is closely related to **coagulation**, as the two often occur together.

# CHEMICAL DICTIONARY
<page-num>170</page-num>

**Roots of Raparum** <note>literally "turnip roots," though here identified with a specific plant</note> are the roots of the **flammula** <note>Lesser Spearwort (*Ranunculus flammula*), a plant known for its acrid, "burning" juice</note>. For by its own nature, it is nothing but flower and flame.

**Reverberation** (*Reverberatio*) is a process of ignition that reduces bodies into a finer **calx** <term>calx: the powdery "ash" or oxide left after a metal or mineral has been thoroughly burnt</term> by means of a live fire that is reflected and bounced back onto the substance. It is performed as either closed or open.

**Closed reverberation** (*Reverberatio clausa*) occurs when the substances to be reverberated are burnt in a sealed reverberatory furnace. In this type of reverberation, the degrees of fire can be managed precisely <note>original: *ad amussim*; according to a level or rule</note>, so that the heat can be increased or decreased—a level of control that is not possible in common furnaces.

**Open reverberation** (*Reverberatio aperta*) occurs when the material is burnt in a reverberatory furnace with all the vents and openings left open. This reverberation is extremely violent, and we use it to break down hard and stubborn substances.

**KING** (*REX*) <note>In alchemical allegory, the "King" usually represents gold or the perfected state of matter.</note>.

**Rinar** is **filings** <note>original: *limatura*; small shavings of metal produced by a file</note>. **Rian** means the same.

**Robes** is **vinegar** <note>original: *acetum*</note>.

**Ron** is a **pound** <note>original: *libra*; a standard unit of weight</note>.

**Rosa** <note>literally "Rose"</note> is **tartar** <note>original: *Tartarum*; the potassium bitartrate deposit found in wine barrels, vital as a flux in alchemical experiments</note>.

**Rota** or **Rotingenius** is **colophony** <note>original: *Colophonia*; a brittle, distilled resin obtained from pine trees</note>.

**Rob** is a **thickened juice** <note>original: *succus inspissatus*; a syrup or fruit concentrate used in medicinal preparations</note>.

**Rubeum** <note>literally "red"</note> refers to **false gold** or **tinted copper**.

**Rutabulum** or a **Hook** (*Uncus*) is a long iron rod, having a spoon on one end and a scraper on the other, used in the operations of **assaying** <term>assaying: the chemical process of testing an ore or metal to determine its purity or composition</term>.

**Synopic red ochre** (*Rubrica synopica*) <note>a high-quality red earth pigment originally from Sinope</note>, which some claim is called *lepinum*. The Greeks call it *magram* or *mogan*, and the Latins *synopida*. **Dioscorides** <note>a Greek physician and botanist of the 1st century AD</note> selects that variety which is heavy, dense, the color of liver, free of stones, consistent in its own color, and which spreads out extensively when diluted. Sometimes small tablets of a yellowish-brown color are brought from **Byzantium** <note>Istanbul</note>, marked with Turkish letters, which merchants call **sealed earth** (*terra sigillata*). Formerly, **Lemnian earth** (*terra lemnia*) <note>a medicinal clay from the island of Lemnos</note> was marked with the image of a goat, because goat’s blood was mixed with it.

**Lemnos**, the island in which that earth was dug, is situated... <note>The text ends mid-sentence. The catchword "citur" indicates the next page begins with the completion of the Latin word "subjicitur" (is situated or lies below).</note>

...is called by the Turk, which is why it can easily be transported to **Byzantium** <note>modern-day Istanbul</note>. If the color of such **lozenges** <note>original: *pastillorum*; small medicinal cakes or tablets</note> brought from Byzantium is less red than **red ochre** <note>original: *rubricæ*</note> ought to be, the cause is perhaps that when the earth is diluted to be formed into lozenges, it changes somewhat. Furthermore, it is now dug from deeper down and thus is less scorched by the heat. The Turks highly value such lozenges for curing the **plague**. The Arabs use **Armenian earth** <note>original: *glebam Armeniacam*; a type of medicinal clay</note>, which is not much different from **sealed earth** <term>sealed earth: *terra sigillata*, a famous medicinal clay from the island of Lemnos, pressed into cakes and stamped with an official seal to guarantee its purity</term>, except that no seal is ever found impressed upon the Armenian earth. Whether this earth is eventually signed and made into lozenges with Turkish letters, or whether it is **Sinopic red earth** <note>a high-quality red pigment traditionally sourced from Sinope on the Black Sea</note>, or **Lemnian earth**, or **Armenian**, it does not matter much, as all such varieties have the same medicinal powers. It should be investigated at Byzantium whether it was brought into Thrace from Lemnos, Armenia, or Sinope.

**German, made from yellow ochre.** <margin>A remarkable metamorphosis; art has learned to mimic nature, making red ochre from yellow ochre in color—and it is not as [the author] thinks, who writes that yellow ochre comes from red.</margin>
**Artificial, for craftsmen,** which is made from yellow ochre burnt by art in new earthenware vessels.

<meta>This page continues the alphabetical entries of a chemical and alchemical dictionary, moving from the medicinal earths (Rubrica) discussed on the previous page to terms beginning with the letter R, such as Relolleum, Realgar, and Rebis, heavily drawing on the theories of Paracelsus.</meta>

<page-num>172</page-num>

**Relolleum** <note>a term coined by Paracelsus referring to the specific power or virtue inherent in a substance</note> is threefold:
*   **Incorporated**
*   **Innate**
*   **Momentary**

1. **Incorporated** substances are all **Mercurials** <term>Mercurials: substances containing or derived from alchemical Mercury, representing the principle of fluidity and transformation</term>, namely Mercury by itself, the Mercury of sulfur, and the Mercury of salts.

2. **Innate** substances are of three kinds: **Consolidating**, **Purifying**, and **Strengthening**.

3. **Momentary** Relolleum is Death; for it is an unforeseen power accompanied by a very swift **tincture** <note>original: *tinctura*; in alchemy, a substance capable of deeply penetrating and changing the color or nature of another</note>. *Paracelsus, page 871.*

**Frog spawn** (*Ranarum sperma*) is prepared by reducing the spawn through solar distillation, and then by distillation through an **alembic** <term>alembic: a classic alchemical still consisting of two vessels connected by a tube, used for distilling volatile substances</term>. *Paracelsus, 879.*

**Reverberated** substances <note>materials subjected to intense heat and flame in a reverberatory furnace</note> are of the second degree. **Calcined** substances <note>reduced to powder by heat</note> are of the third degree. **Sublimated** substances <note>heated until they turn to vapor and solidify again at the top of a vessel</note> are of the fourth degree. *Paracelsus, 890.*

**Realgar**, according to its proper meaning, is the smoke of minerals <note>usually referring to arsenic sulfide</note>, but taken metaphorically, it refers to a corrupt nature within the human body from which ulcers primarily arise. It is fourfold, corresponding to the number of the elements:
*   **Realgar of water**: foam floating upon the waters.
*   **Realgar of earth**: arsenic.
*   **Realgar of air**: *tereniabin* <note>a type of "manna" or sweet dew gathered from plants, thought to fall from the sky</note>.
*   **Realgar of fire**: a Saturnine <note>lead-related</note> conjunction.

**Rabeboja**, the root of the **greater spearwort** <note>original: *flammulæ majoris*; a plant in the buttercup family known for its acrid, "flaming" juice</note>.

**Rebis**, the excrement of the bowels. **Rebis**, or the **Rebis-stone**, is **Hair**. However, **Rebus** is the ultimate matter of things <note>The translator notes that while *Rebis* often refers to the "Double-Thing" or hermaphroditic stage of the alchemical work, here it is used for bodily waste and hair</note>.

**Rebisola** is a secret remedy derived from urine used for **jaundice** (*icteritiam*). If it is boiled and skimmed, and allowed to rest for three days, it yields small "rebisola" stones.

**Rebona**, the same as **mummy** <term>mummy: *mumia*, a substance believed to contain the vital essence of life, often derived from preserved bodies or bitumen</term>.

**Reduc** is a powder by which calcined metals and minerals are melted, returning again into a metallic **regulus** <term>regulus: a pure "button" of metal that settles at the bottom of a crucible after smelting</term>.

**Relolleum** is a virtue arising from a **complexion** <note>the specific balance of qualities or "temperament" in a substance</note>; for example, in St. John's Wort (*hypericone*), the primary virtue lies in its heat and its ability to heal. Acciden- <meta>catchword: talie (referring to "accidental")</meta>

...such is truly the healing virtue <note>original: *cherionia*; a Paracelsian term for an occult power that preserves or heals</note> and hidden power, in that it resists worms and decay.

**Royal cement** (*Regale*) is the substance with which gold is purified.
**Resin** is the name given to yellow and beautiful pitch.
**Resin of gold** is the saffron <term>saffron: in this context, "crocus," a metallic oxide named for its yellow-red color</term> extracted from gold.
**Resin of the pine**, that is, of the fir tree.
**Resin of the earth** is sulfur.
**Potable resin of the earth** is sulfur sublimated into a liquor, or reduced to a balsam or oil.
**Repellents** <note>original: *repercussiva*</note> are medicines that drive back the flows of humors.
A **reverberatory furnace** (*reverberatorium*) is a furnace in which materials are calcined <note>reduced to powder through heat</note> by the action of flame.
A **rillus** is a tool used by goldsmiths into which they pour melted metals to form long ingots.
**Rosea** <note>literally "the rosy one"</note> is the same as **erysipelas** <term>erysipelas: a bacterial skin infection known historically for its bright red rash</term>.
**Redness from black** (*rubedo de nigro*) refers to redness derived from black talc.

# S.

**Sagani** are the spirits of the four elements.
**Salamanders** are spirits inhabiting fire, or "fiery men."
**Saldini** are the same as Salamanders; beings whom the celestial influence sustains, and who are nourished by the element of fire. Such was that pagan woman named **Zifer**, who, as soon as she became ill, would throw herself into the fire and was refreshed and recovered, sitting and rejoicing in it as if in a bath.
**Aluminous salt** is that which is extracted from plumose alum <term>plumose alum: a feathery or fibrous form of aluminum sulfate or asbestos</term>.
**Anathron salt** is a salt extracted from stone moss <note>original: *usnea lapidea*; likely a type of lichen growing on rocks</note>.
**Crystalline salt** is the salt that is boiled down from human urine.
**Colcotharine salt** is that which is made into an alkali from the **colcothar** <term>colcothar: the brownish-red iron oxide left after the distillation of sulfuric acid from green vitriol</term> of vitriol.

**Congealed salt** (*Sal congelatum*) originates in hot springs, fountains, and medicated waters.
**Resolved salt** (*Sal enixum*), that is, dissolved salt.
**Sallena** is a species of stone salt <note>original: *sal petræ*; saltpeter</note>.
**Salt of mercury** is the name given to spirit of wine <term>spirit of wine: high-proof alcohol or ethanol, named "mercurial" for its volatile and transformative nature</term>, which intoxicates.
**Stone salt** (*Sal petræ*) is that which forms in the vaults of cellars in oblong cones, resulting from dissolved lime or from the foam of salt; it is a water-constricting substance.
**Nitre salt** (*Sal nitrum*) is boiled out of earth in which urine has been cast for a long time, such as in sheepfolds, or the stables of horses, cattle, and other animals.
**Practical salt** (*Sal practicum*) is made from a mixture of stone salt and sal ammoniac <term>sal ammoniac: ammonium chloride, a salt used in alchemy to "spiritize" or volatilize solids</term> in equal parts. It is placed in an unglazed earthenware pot and suspended by a cord in a wine cellar. Through a process of dissolution, the salt penetrates the exterior and adheres to the outside of the pot; this must be scraped off every ten or twelve days like frost.
**Cleft salt** (*Sal scissum*) or **entali**, and **cleft alum**, are the same thing.
**External dissolved salt** is a condition when someone, upon eating anything, believes it to be salt, and everything seems salty to them.
**Saltz**, **sultz**, or **Selenipum**, is saltiness or brine made from salt dissolved by the cold or humidity of a pantry or wine cellar.
**Sabon** or **Sabena** is soap, or the lye from which soap is usually made.
**Sadir** is dross <term>dross: the stony or metallic waste separated from metals during smelting</term>.
**Saffarum** is a species of salt.
**Sagda** or **sagdo** is the color of a leek <note>original: *coloris prassii*</note>. Just as jet <note>original: *gagates*</note> attracts straw and threads, and a magnet attracts iron, so the *sagda* attracts wood; it adheres so strongly to wood that it cannot be removed unless it is cut away. It is found firmly fixed to the planks of ships, from which it may be gathered that the sea is its homeland. It is a gemstone the size of a bean. **Solinus** <note>Gaius Julius Solinus, a 3rd-century Latin grammarian and compiler</note> says that among the Chaldeans, *Sagda* is commonly found. **Pliny** <note>Pliny the Elder, author of the *Natural History*</note> says that in Samothrace there is a species of this gem that is black, similar to wood, and weightless.

<meta>catchword: Sagim</meta>

**Congealed salt** <note>original: *Sal congelatum*</note> is formed in hot springs, fountains, and medicinal waters.
**Enixum salt** <note>original: *Sal enixum*</note>, that is, dissolved or neutralized <term>Sal enixum: a term often used in early chemistry for neutral salts, specifically potassium bisulfate, formed during the distillation of nitric acid</term>.
**Sallena** is a species of saltpeter.
**Salt of mercury** <note>original: *Sal mercurii*</note> is a name for the spirit of wine <note>alcohol</note>, because it intoxicates.
**Saltpeter** <note>original: *Sal petræ*</note> is that which grows in the vaults of cellars in long cones; it is formed from dissolved lime or from the foam of salt, a binding water <note>original: *aqua arctans*</note>.
**Nitre salt** <note>original: *Sal nitrum*</note> is boiled out of earth into which urine has been poured for a very long time, such as in sheep pens, horse stables, and the stalls of oxen and other animals.
**Practical salt** <note>original: *Sal practicum*</note> is made from a mixture of equal parts saltpeter and sal ammoniac in an unglazed earthenware pot. This is hung by a cord <note>original: *filo*; OCR 'silo'</note> in a wine cellar. Through dissolution, the salt penetrates to the outside and sticks to the pot; this must be scraped off every ten or twelve days like frost.
**Split salt** <note>original: *Sal scissum*</note>, also known as **entali**, and **split alum** are the same thing.

**Saffarum** is a species of salt.

Just as jet <note>original: *Gagates*</note> attracts straw and threads, and the magnet attracts iron, so *sagda* attracts wood. It clings so strongly to the wood that it cannot be removed unless it is cut away. It is found attached firmly to the planks of ships, from which it can be inferred that the sea is its native home. It is a gemstone the size of a bean. It is said that among the Chaldeans, *sagda* is commonly found. *Pliny* says that in Samothrace, there is a variety of this gem that is black, wood-like, and weightless.

<meta>This page continues the 'S' section of the chemical dictionary, specifically detailing different types of salts and their production methods, following the general definition of salt on page 175.</meta>

-># CHEMICAL DICTIONARY <-
<page-num>176</page-num>

**Indian Salt** <note>original: *Sal Indicum*</note>, named after its place of origin, is Mercury, used for dissolving and coagulating.

**Artificial salt** <note>original: *Sal factitium*</note> is that which is boiled or made by art, and it is of two kinds:

1. **Sea salt** <note>original: *Marinum*</note>. Dioscorides <note>A Greek physician and pharmacologist (c. 40–90 AD) whose work was a standard medical text for 1,500 years</note> describes this as that which becomes white, uniform, and dense, such as the Cyprian variety <note>original: *Cypriacum*; salt from the island of Cyprus</note>, etc. This sea salt is made by filling ponds with sea water and allowing it to be dried by the burning sun, through a kind of "boiling" process, often with rain or fresh water mixed in. For no salt is made without the addition of fresh water, as Pliny <note>Pliny the Elder (23–79 AD), author of the encyclopedic *Natural History*</note> asserts in Book 2, Chapter 106. Those preparing salt mix fresh water with the salty water to wash away the intensity of the bitterness <note>original: *acrimoniæ*</note>; this is reportedly still done in several places in **Spain** and **France**. It is also produced and dried in this way at the Tarentine lake <note>near Taranto, Italy</note> and in **Sicily**, at the Cocanican lake.

2. **Salt boiled by art** from the natural brine <term>Brine: *muria*, a highly concentrated solution of salt in water</term> of springs, just as salt is now boiled at Halle in **Saxony**, and in other places throughout **Thuringia** and **Saxony**. One variety, however, surpasses the other in quality based on the nature of the brine, as we see in a certain place in the March <note>Likely the Margraviate of Brandenburg</note>. In the regions near **Lisbon**, by the sea, there are great marshes where sea water stagnates in many places. In these areas, a type of "lake" sea salt is formed along the shores; as the waves recede, the sun works to "boil" this sea water into salt. The inhabitants soon collect it using a specific technique, creating little compartments <note>original: *cistulis*</note> so that the salt is not carried away again when the tide returns. They pile it into great heaps on the shore, where it is further dried by the hot sun, and pieces of it reach an immense size—as if they were alum or the ice of rivers. However, these pieces are pale, black, or green in color. This salt is then carried away by ships to various maritime nations and is ground using a specific technique until it resembles coarse sand, which is used for salting fish. That "lake salt," when boiled a second time, becomes the finest and whitest salt.

Dioscorides adds the method for burning salt, both the mined variety <note>original: *fossilem*; literally "salt that is dug up"</note>...

as well as the artificial kind, which they call burnt salt, *saltaculum*, and *cotula* <note>These are obscure or archaic terms for varieties of prepared salts used in early chemistry</note>. He also mentions "salt foam" <note>original: *spumae salis*; often referring to salt spray or crusts</note>, which to him is nothing other than the foam of the sea, or the scrapings found on thin rocks in maritime areas on the shore washed by the sea. It is a kind of coagulated substance, having the taste of salt and a strong, fishy sea odor (others call it salt foam or *alcyonium* <note>Historically, *alcyonium* referred to a sponge-like or coral-like sea growth once thought to be solidified sea foam</note>). Another kind arises spontaneously from sea water, left as foam on the furthest shores and cliffs. All of this is thickened by dew and is sharper when found on the cliffs. The commentator on **Nicander** <note>Nicander of Colophon, a Greek poet and physician from the 2nd century BC who wrote on poisons and their salt-based antidotes</note> defines salt foam as that which occurs when people make salt by mixing fresh water with salt water to wash away the intense bitterness with sharp force. Note also that there are infinite species of salt if you consider location, country, color, mineral source, quality, and other factors.

1. **Cappadocian Salt** <note>original: *Sal Capadocium*</note>

There is another species of salt called **Alkali salt** <note>original: *Sal alkali* or *alkal*</note>, which is artificial and made from the **Kali** herb <note>Glasswort or saltwort; plants high in sodium, used for making soda ash</note>, or *ufusen* <note>An Arabic-derived term for a similar plant</note>, which they say is made by burning the *anthyllis* herb <note>Possibly *Anthyllis hermanniae* or a similar saline-loving plant</note> in a pit.

There is also "shining salt" called **nitre** <note>original: *nitrum*; historically used to refer to both saltpeter and sodium carbonate</note>, as well as **Alexandrian salt**, and **Anderona salt** (*baurach* <note>From the Arabic *borāq*, the root of the modern word "borax"</note>), and **aphronitrum** <note>literally "foam of nitre," a surface crust found on salt deposits</note>, for nitre is a species of salt.

**Native Sandarac** <note>original: *Sandaraca nativa*</note> is a metallic substance and a purple earth; it is found in the same mines in which native orpiment <note>original: *auripigmentum*; a yellow arsenic mineral</note> is found. ...with sulfur and quicksilver <note>mercury</note> made weak by nature. In short, **Sandarac** is a red metallic earth, whether purple or fiery, smelling of the stench of sulfur.

The best are found among the Medes. Those which are of a dark blue color <note>original: *cyanei*</note> are judged to be the males. They proclaim the Oriental and Jewish varieties to be the best; of these, one species is of a transparent color, like a serene sky, in which a yellowish color seems to prevail. Another species has dark clouds inclining toward redness, while another has small white clouds by contrast.

**Sapphire** is said to make a person chaste, pious, and stable. We use Sapphire for strengthening the heart and for cooling the internal body, according to the verse:

->Sapphire cools the body's inner heat,<-
->And makes the weary vows of Venus <note>original: *Cypriæ* — an epithet for Venus, the goddess of love, implying the stone dampens lust</note> languid.<-

**Saraffi** is gypsum <note>plaster of Paris</note>.
**Sarca**,  }
**Sayrsa**, } is **Iron**.
**Sarra**,  }

**Sarfar** is also **Iron**.

**Sard** <note>original: *Sarda*</note> is a gemstone which is also called the sardine stone, and is now called **Cornelian** <note>original: *Darneolus*</note>. According to *Pliny, book 37, chapter 7*, drinking vessels were once made from it. Its name is often interchanged with **Sardonyx**. It takes its name from Sardis, where it was first found, though the most highly praised is found in the heart of the rock in Babylonia. *Albertus* <note>Albertus Magnus, a 13th-century scholar and bishop</note> claims that the sard sharpens the wit and makes men joyful because it purges the blood and produces good spirits. Ruland <note>The author of this lexicon</note>, following *Pliny, book 37, chapter 6*, lists 22 species of **Sard** on page 423.

**Sardonyx** is a transparent red; it has the appearance of a human fingernail <note>from the Greek *onyx*, meaning nail</note>. When placed in the hands, the onyx appears in the upper parts, and the sard in the other part.

**Saturn** <term>Saturn: the traditional alchemical name for the metal lead</term> is lead.

**Sarnicum** or **Sacen** is "of the Dragon" <note>likely referring to *Sanguis Draconis* or Dragon's Blood, a bright red resin, or perhaps a form of arsenic</note>.

**Saura**, called *Lacertus* or *Lacerta* [the Lizard] by the Latins, is of many species. Among these, the most beautiful is green with a white belly, found near thickets and hedges; in their bellies, if cut open with a reed, is found the gemstone **Saurites**, which is said... <meta>catchword: to</meta>

<summary>This page defines various gemstones (Sapphire, Sard, Sardonyx) and substances (Iron, Lead, Gypsum) used in alchemical and medicinal practices, detailing their physical properties and symbolic virtues. It highlights the use of Sapphire for its cooling properties and the Sard for its ability to improve mood and intellect.</summary>

# CHEMICAL DICTIONARY
<page-num>181</page-num>

...[it is said] to be effective against poisons; and now, by a new experiment, the bones of a green lizard are found to be beneficial for the "comitial" disease <note>original: *comitialibus* — a historical term for epilepsy, so named because an assembly (comitia) would be abandoned if a member had a seizure</note>. But the bones ought to be collected in this way: Place a green lizard, captured while still alive, into some closed vessel filled with the best salt; the salt will consume its flesh and all its intestines within a few days. Thus you will easily be able to collect the bones, and those bones are as beneficial for that disease as the hoof of an Elk <note>original: *Alcis* — elk hooves were a traditional remedy for epilepsy in early modern medicine</note>.

**Stones** <note>original: *Saxa*</note>. Certain species of **Stones** are dug out from mines.

**Saxifragium**, any stone that breaks and dissolves tartar <note>stony deposits or "calculi" such as kidney stones</note> within a human being.

**Sbesten**, is quicklime <note>original: *calx viva*</note>.

**Schehendenigri**, is cannabis, or its seed.

**Schistos**, or **Schistum**, is a species of hematite <note>bloodstone</note>, but paler and of a saffron color, and brittle; by its nature it is fissile <note>easily split</note>. By its growth and the alternating path of its clinging veins, it resembles a comb, much like ammoniac salt. Hematite with reddish veins is brittle and blood-colored; its power is to strongly bind <term>astringent</term>. Regarding how the **Schist** stone is burned with gold for "lichens" <note>skin eruptions or sores</note>, see *Pliny, book 33, chapter 4.*

**Scobs**, is potash <note>original: *cinis clavellatus*</note>, that is, alkali.

**Scobs**, is the dross <note>original: *scoria*</note> of any metal.

**Scorax**, is the gum of the Olive tree.

**Scorith**, is Sulfur.

**Scorpius constrictus**, is prepared iron.

***Taberzet* salt**, is the whitest tartar of all <note>original: *Sal taberzet* — a term likely derived from Persian meaning "sugar-candy," here used to describe highly refined tartar</note>.

***Tabari* salt**, is salt alembrot <note>original: *sal alembrot* — a "salt of art" or "salt of wisdom" made from mercury and ammonium chloride, used as a universal solvent</note>.

***Samech***, is tartar. Salt of tartar, a remedy for all wounds.

***Sandaracha***, is red orpiment, more thoroughly cooked by nature <note>original: *auripigmentum rubeum* — today known as realgar, a red arsenic sulfide</note>.

**Sapphire-like matter**, is a liquid in which there is no impurity or corruption, but is a liquid separated from everything impure <note>original: *Saphirea materia*</note>.

**Calcined blood**, that is, like lime, sharp and white <note>original: *Sanguis calcetus*</note>.

**Soap of wisdom**, is common salt prepared into a liquid <note>original: *Sapo sapientiæ*</note>.

***Saxifraga***, or ***Saxifragia***, is the name given to all things that expel stones, calculi, or sand <note>from the kidneys or bladder</note>.

***Saxifragus***, is a pale crystal, previously called yellowish <note>original: *citrinolus*</note>.

***Scacurcula***, is the spirit of the bone from the heart of a stag <note>The "bone of the heart" in a deer is actually a piece of hardened cartilage that was highly prized as a heart tonic in early medicine</note>.

***Scajolæ***, are the spiritual powers of the mind and soul—the properties, virtues, and powers which are fourfold according to the number of the elements. They are the four wheels of the fiery chariot by which Elijah was snatched up into heaven; they take their origin from the soul and mind of man, from which they depart and to which they are again reflected. They are, specifically: fantasy, imagination, speculation, and innate faith. They are also, and especially, those four chief articles of our entire Christian faith: Baptism, true faith in Jesus Christ, the taking of the Sacrament of the Lord’s Supper, and charity toward one’s neighbor, which displays the mature fruits of faith. Through all of these we attain not only a long life, but even an eternal one. Read more extensively on these in Paracelsus in *The Eucharist*, and in the expositions in his book *On the Lord’s Supper*, and you will hear things that satisfy a soul eager for piety and truth.

***Scirona***, is autumn dew.

***Scuma***, the same as scale <note>original: *squama* — the dross or metallic flakes that come off metal when hammered</note>.

# CHEMICAL DICTIONARY
<page-num>183</page-num>
<header>CHEMICAL DICTIONARY</header>

***Selenite stone*** <note>original: *Selenites lapis*; a variety of gypsum known for its transparency</note> is considered to be a **specular stone** <note>original: *lapide speculari*; a transparent mineral, such as mica or clear gypsum, used in antiquity for window panes and mirrors</note>. However, the **Arabian Stone** is properly the specular stone, especially that species which resembles ivory. — *Pliny.*

If **Selenite** is a specular stone, it is properly that species which is most white, from which windows are made using polished wood in place of lead.

**Dioscorides** says that Selenite is called ***Aphroselium*** <note>original: *Aphroselium*, Greek for "moon-foam"</note> because it is found to reflect the image of the moon at night; and the bright specular stone does this, for the shining moon is clearly seen in it as if in a mirror. But whether it increases or decreases with the moon, I do not know. This stone seems to be a solidified moisture. — *Pliny, book 37, chapter 10.*

***Selfas*** is a fern.

***Semafor Femar*** is the brightness of the **Sedge** <note>original: *Carici*; likely referring to a type of marsh grass or reed</note>.

***Separator*** <note>original: *Seperatoriũ*</note> is a transmitting vessel that is somewhat uniform and oblong, with an opening the size of the little finger into which material is poured. It is provided with a bottom sharpened like a needle for the sake of emitting the material again, and has two attached handles; it is suitable for separating liquids from one another.

***Separation*** of the Elements.

***Separation by departure*** <note>original: *Separatio per abscessum*</note> occurs when parts of different natures withdraw from one another through their mixture and are carried to distinct regions, standing apart from each other. This is twofold: departure and dilution.

***Separation by abstraction*** is when different parts are pulled away from one another by means of some instrument.

And this happens in two ways:
| | |
|---|---|
| By **Washing** <note>original: *Ablutione*</note>, & | |
| By **Withdrawal** <note>original: *Subductione*</note>, | whether the things be liquid or dry. |

***Separation by dilution*** is performed at the side by means of a long **trough** <note>original: *lintrem*; literally a "small boat" or "skiff," used here for a specialized laboratory tray</note> with a low edge and one end tapering to a point, which can be turned by hand and shaken from side to side, and pushed by the other wider side.

->Se-<-

# CHEMICAL DICTIONARY
<page-num>186</page-num>
<header>CHEMICAL DICTIONARY</header>

***Separation by dilution*** <note>original: *Separatio per dilutionem*</note> occurs when earth, heavily diluted with liquid, is separated in such a way that the heavy parts settle at the bottom, the light parts rise to the top, and the intermediate parts occupy the middle space. Since this kind of washing is most effective for muddy or earthy materials—or those reduced to such a consistency through pulverizing, calcining, and other methods—it was rightly called ***perisyria*** <note>original: *περισυρία*; a Greek term meaning "a stripping away" or "sweeping around," referring to the removal of impurities</note> by the ancients. This process is assisted by abstraction, depletion, and sometimes filtration and similar functions. For once substances are dissolved, agitated, and moved into their respective layers, they must soon be drained off or poured through a hair filter <note>original: *filtrum setaceum*</note>; otherwise, they will become mixed together again.

***Septamen*** is **serapinum** <note>a gum-resin derived from a plant in the fennel family, often used in ancient medicine</note>.

***Septentrio*** <note>literally "The North"</note> is **aqua fortis** <note>nitric acid</note>.

***Serex*** is **sour milk**.

***Sericon*** is ***Minium*** <note>red lead</note>.

***Sericiacum*** is ***Arsenic***.

***Serapinus*** is ***gum arabic***.

***Serpent of the spirit of chastity*** <note>original: *Serpens spiritus castitatis*</note> is **Mercury**.

***Fugitive slave*** <note>original: *Servus fugitivus*</note> is **Mercury**. **Hermes** <note>referring to Hermes Trismegistus, the mythical founder of alchemy</note> names it so because of its "fugitive" or volatile humidity.

***Sidia*** is a **file** <note>original: *lima*; a tool for smoothing or grinding</note>.

***Sifantis*** is **silvery earth**, or *simphus*.

***Sigia, or***  }
***Sigra,***      } These are **liquid Storax** <note>a fragrant resin used in incense and medicine</note>.

***Seal of Hermes*** <note>original: *Sigillum Hermetis*</note> is the **mouth of a glass vessel** <note>the origin of the term "hermetically sealed," where the glass neck of a flask is melted shut</note>.

***Sirza*** is an ***Eschara*** <note>originally a medical term for a scab or sear from a burn; here likely referring to a crust or residue in a crucible</note>.

***Flint stone*** <note>original: *Silex lapis*</note> is the stone we use to strike fire. It is called *Silex* as if the fire "leaps forth" <note>original: *saliat*</note> from it. This name is also appropriate for **pyrite**. According to **Pliny**, pyrite is a genus (a general category) rather than a species; indeed, to him, every stone from which fire can be struck is a "pyrite" because of its effect. **Rulandus** <note>Martin Ruland the Elder, a famous alchemical lexicographer</note> on pages 43 to 435 records various species of flints.

***Siliqua*** is a weight of **four grains**.

***Simmitium*** is **Ceruse** <note>white lead, often used as a pigment</note>.

# CHEMICAL DICTIONARY
<page-num>187</page-num>
<header>CHEMICAL DICTIONARY</header>
<sig>N 4</sig>
<meta>catchword: Solutio</meta>

**Sinapisis** is **Armenian bole** <note>a reddish medicinal earth or clay historically sourced from Armenia</note>.
**Sinopis** is **Minium** <note>red lead pigment</note>, named after the city of Sinope.
**Sypho**, & }
**Syringe**, } A **water squirt** <note>a device for spraying or injecting liquids</note>.
**Siricon of lead** is the **ash of lead**.
**Siringae** is **lime** <note>original: *Calx*; specifically quicklime or calcium oxide</note>.
The **Emerald** <note>original: *Smaragdus*</note> was dedicated to **Mercury** by the ancient astrologers.

The **Emerald** holds third place among gemstones, because no other color has a more pleasant appearance: for it surpasses the watered grass, the friendly herbs, and all foliage in its greenness. Furthermore, it alone among gems fills the eyes with its sight without exhausting them: it first restores a vision obscured by looking at other things and then focuses the sight. *Pliny & Solinus, chapter 12.* He lists the different species of Emeralds.

The **Emerald** is an enemy to poison and poisonous creatures. They say that if the eye of any poisonous animal looks directly at it, the animal is struck blind. It protects against Epilepsy, heals leprosy, strengthens the sight and memory, and restrains sexual desire; it is said that if worn on a finger, the stone will break during sexual intercourse.

**Emery stone** <note>original: *Smyris lapis*</note> is extremely hard and rough. Ring-engraving sculptors and other craftsmen use this stone for polishing, and nowadays they cut other stones with it through a specialized technique. *Dioscorides* <note>a Greek physician and botanist</note> says it is useful for eroding and cauterizing, and is suitable for ulcerated gums and as a tooth-powder.

**Solatur** is **quicksilver** <note>mercury</note>.
**Solelasar** is **alkali**.
**Solutum** is that which is **melted** or **liquefied**.

**Solution** <note>original: *Solutio*</note> is the first part of chemical practice, by which the structure of solidified things is loosened and thinned. This process is divided into many different types. However, among craftsmen in the practice of this discipline, the name is used more familiarly and frequently for one specific type than for another, and sometimes a specific kind is designated by this general term alone.

<meta>This page continues the "S" entries of the chemical dictionary, following from the properties of the emerald on page 187. It moves from definitions of "Solution" and specific mineral substances like "Sorus" into alchemical theory regarding "Spirit" and the "Spagyric art."</meta>

# CHEMICAL DICTIONARY
<page-num>188</page-num>
<header>CHEMICAL DICTIONARY</header>

*Solution* <note>original: *Solutio*</note>, is twofold:
} Liquation <note>melting</note>, and Segregation <note>separation</note>: or
} Calcination <note>reduction to powder by heat</note>, and Dissolution.

*Sun-follower* <note>original: *Sol sequium*; in this context, a chemical name for sulfur</note>, is sulfur.

*Sorus*, or *fory* <note>an ancient name for a mineral related to vitriol or copperas</note>, is of the same genus as *Melanteria* <note>a black mineral pigment or metallic salt</note>; the ancients believed *sorus* and melanteria to be nearly the same color. Indeed, both turn into *white vitriol* <note>original: *calchantum leucon*</note>, which fullers <note>workers who clean and thicken cloth</note> use for dyeing. However, *sorus* contains a heavy poison that induces nausea, which melanteria does not. The best is the *Egyptian* variety, which appears blacker when crushed, is full of many small holes, is somewhat fatty and astringent, and has a powerful, poisonous smell when inhaled or tasted.

*Spagyria*, or the *spagyric art* <note>a term popularized by Paracelsus for alchemy, emphasizing the separation of components and their subsequent recombination</note>, is that which teaches how to separate the pure from the impure, so that after the dregs are cast aside, the remaining virtue <note>essential power</note> may perform its work.

*Spar* <note>original: *Spara*</note>, is also found in lead mines near Barhoel <note>likely Bakewell</note> in the County of Derby in England; it is a white stone similar to gypsum, effective against the stone <note>kidney or bladder stones</note>.

*Spatula*, or *stopper* <note>original: *obex*</note>, is a broad instrument made in the fashion of a spatula, which serves in composite furnaces for increasing or decreasing the heat.

*Formal Specific* <note>original: *Specificum formale*</note>, is that which represents a species through its formal properties. And although material virtues concur, the formal ones excel, and those are primarily considered during the laboratory work. It is also found as a tincture and an oil.

*Yellow Mirror* <note>original: *Speculum citrinum*</note>, is yellow Arsenic.
*White Mirror* <note>original: *Speculum album*</note>, is Arsenic that has been precipitated <note>original: *descensum*</note>.
*Indian Mirror* <note>original: *Speculum Indicum*</note>, is iron filings.
*Mirror of the Moon* <note>original: *Speculum Lunæ*</note>, is *Affroselinum* <note>literally "moon-froth," likely a name for selenite or a specific white mineral</note>.

*Sphere* <note>original: *Sphæra*</note>, } of the Sun, and
} of the Moon, is the Quintessence <note>the "fifth essence" or purest spiritual core of a substance</note>.

# CHEMICAL DICTIONARY
<page-num>189</page-num>
<header>CHEMICAL DICTIONARY. 189</header>

...and in its consistency it maintains a specific watery quality. In such a substance, it carries the power of the thing.

**Spirit** <note>original: *Spiritus*</note> fluctuates between water and oil and approaches a liquid state. Therefore it is also called "oil," especially in the case of minerals. And some spirits are more oily, while others are more watery. At other times, their exhalations are also called spirits, such as metallic fumes, as well as those of antimony <note>original: *stibium*</note>, tin, flower of salt, of wine, etc. *The Blast of the Trumpet* <note>original: *Clangor Buccinae*, a well-known alchemical treatise</note>.

The principal **Spirits of Chemistry** are seven.
**Stinking spirit** <note>original: *Spiritus foetens*</note> is sulfur.
**Spodion flower** <note>original: *Spodion flos*</note> is copper rust <note>verdigris</note>.
**Spodius**, }
**Succudus**, } Is **Tutty** <note>zinc oxide found in the flues of smelting furnaces</note>.
**Spodium** is the ash of gold.
**Spodos**, there are five kinds:
1. **Spodos** is a soot of ash-colored heat, heavier than *Pompholyx* <note>a light, white zinc oxide residue often called "philosopher's wool"</note>.
2. **Yellow**, which is generated in the furnaces where metals are separated.
3. **Ash-colored subterranean** [spodos].
4. **Black subterranean** [spodos].
5. **Green subterranean** [spodos].
**Groom** <note>original: *Sponsus*</note> is Mercury <note>the "husband" in the alchemical marriage of metals</note>.
**Foam** <note>original: *Spuma*</note> is Ash.
**Foam of silver** is *Litharge* <note>original: *Glette*; a lead oxide produced in the refining of silver</note>.
**Foam of the sea** is *Halcyonium* <note>a substance found on the seashore, believed by ancients to be a mixture of sea-foam and bird nests, used for skin ailments</note>.
**Foam of saltpeter** is *Aphronitrum*, or *Baurach* in Arabic <note>likely borax or a form of natron</note>.
**Spittle of the sea** <note>original: *Sputa maris*</note> is sulfur.
**Spittle of the mushrooms** <note>original: *Sputam boleterum*</note> is Magnesia, or thick gold.
**Sponge**, or the sea fungus, which attracts and preserves water, is a **Zoophyte** <note>a creature that appears to be part plant and part animal</note>; it is neither animal nor shrub, but possesses a certain third nature.

**Avicenna** <note>a famous 11th-century Persian physician and philosopher</note> also defines it thus:
> **Sponge** is a marine body, loose and rare; that which it clings to, it is not separated from, and it possesses souls.

**Solution** <note>original: *Solutio*</note> is twofold:
| | |
| :--- | :--- |
| **Solution** | Melting, and Separation: or |
| | Calcination, and Dissolution. |

**Sun-follower** <note>original: *Sol sequium*; usually referring to the heliotrope or marigold, but here used as a code name</note> is sulfur.

**Sory** <note>original: *Sorus* or *sory*; a mineral substance containing metal sulfates</note> is of the same kind as *Melanteria* <note>a yellow-black vitriolic earth</note>, and of almost the same color. The ancients believed sory and melanteria to be the same thing, and indeed both are transformed into **white vitriol** <note>original: *calchantum leucon*</note>, which fullers <note>workers who cleaned and thickened cloth</note> use for dyeing. But in sory there is a heavy, poisonous stench that causes nausea, which melanteria does not. The best is the *Egyptian* variety, which appears blacker when rubbed, has many gaping holes, is somewhat fatty and astringent, and has a great, poisonous smell when inhaled or swallowed.

**Spagyrics** <note>original: *Spagiria*</note>, or the spagyric art, is that which teaches how to separate the pure from the impure, so that after the dregs are cast aside, the remaining virtue may perform its work. <note>The term "spagyric" comes from the Greek words for "to tear apart" and "to bind together."</note>

**Spar** <note>original: *Spara*</note> is also found in lead mines near *Barhoel* <note>likely Bakewell</note> in the County of *Derbyshire* in *England*; it is a white stone similar to gypsum, effective against kidney stones.

**Spatula**, or stopper, is a wide instrument made in the shape of a spatula, which serves in composite furnaces to increase or decrease the heat.

**Formal Specific**, is that which represents a species at least through its formal properties. And although material virtues may concur, the formal ones excel, and those are the ones primarily considered in the laboratory work. It is both a tincture and an oil.

**Yellow Mirror** <note>original: *Speculum citrinum*</note> is yellow Arsenic.

**White Mirror** <note>original: *Speculum album*</note> is Arsenic collected by descent <note>a method of distillation where the vapor is forced downward</note>.

**Indian Mirror** <note>original: *Speculum Indicum*</note> is iron filings.

**Specular stone**, Moon-stone, Moon-foam <note>original: *Affroselinum*</note>. <note>These are names for Selenite or translucent mica.</note>

**Sphere**, { of the Sun, and
{ of the Moon, is the Quintessence.

**Spirit** <note>original: *Spiritus*</note> is a dissolving water produced from a simple and sharp thing, possessing the nature of a fiery breath. Its most powerful part is the fiery breath, though mixed with a vaporous part. So—

# CHEMICAL DICTIONARY

<page-num>189</page-num>
<header>CHEMICAL DICTIONARY.</header>

...and in its consistency, it obtains a specific watery quality. In such a substance, it carries the force of the matter.

**Spirit** <note>original: *Spiritus*</note> fluctuates between water and oil, and approaches the nature of a liquid. Therefore, it is also called "oil," especially in reference to minerals. Some spirits are more oily, while others are more watery. At other times, their exhalations are also called spirits, such as metallic fumes, as well as the fumes of antimony <note>original: *stibium*</note>, tin, the flower of salt, the spirit of wine, etc. Mentioned in *The Sound of the Trumpet* <note>original: *Clangor Buccinæ*; an influential 15th-century alchemical treatise</note>.

There are seven principal **Chemical Spirits**:
1. **Stinking spirit** <note>original: *Spiritus fœtens*</note> is sulfur.
2. **Flower of Spodion** <note>original: *Spodion flos*</note> is verdigris <note>original: *ærugo æris*; the green pigment or rust that forms on copper</note>.
3. **Spodius** or **Succudus** is **Tutty** <note>original: *Tutia*; an impure form of zinc oxide collected from the flues of smelting furnaces</note>.
4. **Spodium** is the ash of gold.
5. **Spodos** <note>a Greek-derived term for various types of slag or metallic dust</note> has five kinds:
   1. **Spodos** is a soot of ashy heat, heavier than **Pomphylix** <note>original: *Pomphylix*; also known as "white nothing," the lightest zinc oxide dust</note>.
   2. **Yellow Spodos**, which is produced in the hearths where metals are separated.
   3. **Gray subterranean Spodos**.
   4. **Black subterranean Spodos**.
   5. **Green subterranean Spodos**.

**Groom** <note>original: *Sponsus*</note> is Mercury.
**Scum** <note>original: *Spuma*</note> is Ash.
**Scum of silver** <note>original: *Spuma argenti*</note> is Litharge <note>original: *Glette*; lead monoxide, a byproduct of separating silver from lead</note>.
**Sea foam** <note>original: *Spuma maris*</note> is *Halcionium* <note>a medicinal substance found on the sea, possibly dried sea-moss or kingfisher nests in ancient lore</note>.
**Scum of nitre** <note>original: *Spuma nitri*</note> is *Aphronitrum* or Arabic *Baurach* <note>likely Borax or Natron</note>.
**Sea spittle** <note>original: *Sputa maris*</note> is sulfur.
**Mushroom spittle** <note>original: *Sputam boleterum*</note> is Magnesia, or thickened gold.

**Sponge** <note>original: *Spongia*</note>, or *marine fungus*, which attracts and preserves water, is a **Zoophyte** <note>original: *Zoophyton*; a term for organisms, like sponges or corals, that were thought to be intermediate between animals and plants</note>; it is neither animal nor shrub, but possesses a certain third nature.

->*Avicenna also defines it thus:*<-

> **Sponge** is a marine body, loose and rare; that which it clings to, it is not separated from, and it possesses souls.

# CHEMICAL DICTIONARY

<page-num>190</page-num>
<header>CHEMICAL DICTIONARY.</header>

Dioscorides <note>A 1st-century Greek physician and botanist</note> identifies two kinds [of sponges], namely:
{ **Male**, and
{ **Female**.

The **Male** consists of dense and tiny tubes, white in color; the harder examples of this species were called **Tragos** <note>original: *Tragos*; Greek for "goat," referring here to a rough or shaggy texture</note> in former times.

The **Female** consists more of round holes.

Some authors add a third species, which contains hard stones and many cavities. Within these female sponges in the sea, "kernels" <note>original: *nuclei*</note> are sometimes found—though rarely—shaped like a small apple or a peeled almond. These are of great importance in medicine for treating worms in the stomachs of children. Pliny <note>Pliny the Elder, a Roman naturalist</note> in Book 32, Chapter 11, teaches that all sponges are born on rocks, reefs, shells, small fish, and mud. Furthermore, he claims they possess a form of intelligence <note>original: *intellectum*</note>, because when they sense someone trying to tear them away, they contract, making them much more difficult to remove. They do the same when struck by waves; for they are said to hear the sound and contract in response. He also suggests that sponges feed and are nourished by shells, and when torn away, they emit a blood-like juice and can regrow from the root, etc.

**Stuc**, or }
**Stoac**,   } This is **coagulation**, or freezing <note>original: *congelatio*</note>.

**Tin** <note>original: *Stannum*</note> is, without a doubt, a metal considered by the Arabs to be more precious than our white lead <note>The author distinguishes between *plumbum candidum* (white lead/tin) and *stannum*</note>, just as among us **Bismuth** <note>original: *Bisemuthum*</note> is something more precious than lead. It appears that tin is one thing, and "white lead" is another. Chemists always understand tin to be our **Zinn** <note>The German word for tin</note>, and they attribute it to **Jupiter**, calling tin "Jupiter" <note>In alchemy, every metal was associated with a planet; Jupiter is the traditional ruler of tin</note>. When Pliny in Book 34, Chapter 16 says that "black lead" <note>original: *plumbum nigrum*; usually referring to lead</note> has a double origin—firstly, that it comes from its own vein, or that it is born with silver—he clearly teaches that the liquid of black lead in the furnaces which flows out first is called *stannum*. This is despite his earlier teaching that white lead is smelted from black pebbles marked with white spots. Furthermore, he teaches that tin is adulterated with white lead, or with various proportions of white and black lead.

**Tin** <note>original: *Stannum*</note> is categorized as { **Rich**, **Average**, or **Poor**.

***Stibium***, or ***Stimmi***, is **Antimony**. It is also called **Black Sulfur**. By the Germans it is called ***Spiefglass*** <note>modern German: *Spießglanz*, or Antimony glance</note>. They hold that Antimony is the substance of "dead lead." Sometimes it is mixed with silver, so that it cannot be easily separated. If this is melted frequently, it turns into lead. Its primary power is to restrict and to cool.

***Stipte***,  }
***Stiptera***, } This is **split alum** <note>a fibrous or schistous form of alum</note>.

***Stomomutos*** is the **scale of copper** <note>flakes or dross produced when heating copper</note>.

***Stomoma***, or "the edge," is a pure vein of **steel** from the Narisci <note>an ancient Germanic tribe</note>, or the Vogtland region, near Fichtelberg. Ruland <note>Martin Ruland the Elder, a famous physician and alchemist</note> on page 449 lists 13 different species.

**Western Star** <note>original: *Stella Occidens*</note> is **sal ammoniac** <note>ammonium chloride</note>.

**Star of the Earth** <note>original: *Stella terrae*</note> is **Talc**.

***Stercorum ezeff*** is **litharge** <note>lead oxide</note>.

**Layer**    }
**upon**     } This is well known to chemists <note>original: *Stratum super Stratum*; a method of layering materials in a crucible or vessel</note>.
**Layer**,    }

***Squillinum aziza*** is **horse dung**.

***Stupio*** is **tin**.

**Subduction** <note>original: *Subductio*</note> is an abstraction <note>a drawing off of spirits or liquids</note> through the lower parts.
It is of two types: { **Filtration**, and the **Clepsydra** <note>originally a water-clock; here a vessel for slow dripping or siphoning</note>.

**Sublimation** <note>the process of heating a solid until it becomes a vapor and then solidifies again</note> is of two types: { One by **Distance**, the other by **Surface**.

**Sublimation by distance** occurs when, during the sublimation, an airy interval remains between the material being sublimated and the "dead head" <note>original: *caput mortuum*; the useless residue left in the bottom of a flask</note>. In this process, not only volatile substances <note>original: *halabilia*</note> are raised, but also vaporous ones, sometimes within a very large vessel <note>original: *mundi*, literally "world," often used for a large spherical flask</note> in which the elevated vapors remain suspended in the middle.

# CHEMICAL DICTIONARY

<page-num>192</page-num>

<header>CHEMICAL DICTIONARY.</header>

**Refinement** <note>original: *Subtilatio*</note> *is a dissolution that separates the more subtle parts from the coarse.*

*Refinement is of two types:* {
**Short-duration** <note>original: *μικροχρόνικη* (mikrochronike)</note>,
**Long-duration** <note>original: *μακροχρόνικη* (makrochronike)</note>.

**Short-duration refinement** *occurs when bodies are dissolved in a short space of time.*

*It consists of:* {
**Elevation**, <note>rising up, often through heat</note> or
**Descension**. <note>sinking or flowing downward</note>

**Long-duration refinement** *is that which dissolves things over a longer period of time.*

*It consists of:* {
**Exaltation**, <note>the process of increasing a substance's potency or "lifting" its grade</note> and
**Digestion**. <note>the slow application of gentle heat, mimicking natural maturation</note>

**Juice** <note>original: *Succus*</note> *is an essence extracted directly from a body, having a chyle-like consistency* <note>resembling the milky fluid produced during digestion</note>. *Juice is very close to the "magisteries"* <note>term for a substance prepared to its highest power</note> *and carries quite a bit of corporality with it; indeed, if compared to a "mystery"* <note>a highly refined alchemical quintessence</note>, *it could be called a "body," as it is not yet fully elaborated but is a ruder essence, and not infrequently the raw material for mysteries. It contains some moisture, more or less according to the nature of the thing. Since this moisture is vaporous or exhalable, it can easily happen that a thinner juice is gradually thickened through digestion until it congeals; hence it is called congealed or thickened juice. The ancients called these Rob* <note>thickened fruit juice</note>, *Syrups, decoctions, and sapa* <note>concentrated grape juice</note>; *also tinctures, from whose fermentation a potential extract is made.*

**Amber** <note>original: *Succina* or *Succinum*</note>. *Ruland* <note>Martin Ruland the Elder, a well-known physician and author of a 1612 alchemical lexicon</note> *lists 7 species of "heavy" amber on page 451.*

*Of liquid or transparent amber, 13 species, page 451.*

*Of thick amber, 11 species, page 452.*

*Of mixed amber (combined with other juices), 10 species, page 453.*

**Oil of Amber** *is an effective remedy for:*
{
**Epilepsy**,
**Paralysis**,
**Cold diseases of the head**, <note>in the humoral system, "cold" diseases were those caused by excess phlegm or lack of "vital heat"</note>
**And many other diseases.**

<meta>This page continues the "S" section of the chemical dictionary, moving from the definition of "Juice" (Succus) into the major entry for "Sulfur" and its various alchemical interpretations.</meta>

# CHEMICAL DICTIONARY

<page-num>195</page-num>

<header>CHEMICAL DICTIONARY.</header>

**Sulfur**, **Chybur** <note>original: *Chybur*, an Arabic-derived synonym for sulfur</note>, a part of the **Philosophers' Stone**, the principle and father of metals, hot and dry in the fourth degree <note>the highest level of intensity in the traditional system of elemental qualities</note>. It is nothing other than the fatness of the earth thickened by a temperate boiling <note>original: *decoctionem*</note> until it has been hardened and made dry. Sulfur is generated from the purest dryness of the earth, or from an earthy substance in which fire predominates in every way. It is, as it were, a hot and dry vapor, the cause and father of metals.

Or, **Sulfur** is earth cooked through by heat and changed into the substance of Sulfur, while the watery, earthy, and fiery parts pass through it. *Ruland, from page 456 to 459.* <note>Referencing Martin Ruland the Elder's *Lexicon Alchemiae* (1612)</note> The species of Sulfur.

**Sulfur** is the formative principle, partly airy and partly fiery, participating in an ethereal nature, through which power exists and life dwells in things. For this reason, it is also called the **balsam of nature**. Thus, **Salt** is the terminator <note>that which gives boundaries or solidifies</note>, and **Sulfur** is the informer <note>the giver of "form" or essence</note>, possessing a shaping power <note>original: *vim plasticum*</note>. **Mercury** is the oily vapor that is acted upon by them; the Chymists leave the "essential form" for the Natural Philosophers <note>original: *Physicis*</note> to consider. Others describe these principles thus: **Mercury** is the living, mobile, and spirited species that can be thinned into a breath; **Salt** is the dry, preserving, and sharpening species; **Sulfur** is the burning, hot, and purest species. You will note here the peculiar meaning of the word "Mercury," which you cannot exchange with quicksilver <note>liquid metal mercury</note> as you might elsewhere. Likewise, "life" here is not used in the sense of Galen or Aristotle <note>referring to biological life/soul</note>, but in a Chymical sense, which regards the temperament <note>original: *crasin*</note> from which faculties and powers arise. And this Sulfur is one thing, while the sulfur of the common people <note>brimstone</note> is another.

**Sulfur**, one of those three first principles of all things <note>the *Tria Prima* of Paracelsus: Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury</note>, is that oily substance that burns.

**Red sulfur** is arsenic.

**Sumbi** is { **Scaly/husk-like**, <note>original: *acerosus*</note> and
{ **Saffron-colored powder**. <note>original: *crocus*; usually refers to a metallic oxide like Crocus Martis</note>

**Surfaces** <note>original: *Superficies*</note> is the white of eggs.

<meta>catchword: Simar,</meta>

<summary>This page defines Sulfur not just as a mineral, but as a primary alchemical principle representing heat, oiliness, and the "formative" power of nature. It distinguishes the philosophical Sulfur from common brimstone and mercury from quicksilver.</summary>
<keywords>Sulfur, Chybur, Tria Prima, formative principle, balsam of nature, Mercury, Salt, arsenic, Sumbi, egg whites</keywords>

<meta>This page contains entries for the letter 'S' in the chemical dictionary. It serves as a foundational reference for Paracelsian terminology.</meta>

<page-num>194</page-num>

-> # CHEMICAL DICTIONARY <-

<header>CHEMICAL DICTIONARY.</header>

*Simar* is { Roman Green, or <note>original: *Viride Romanum*; a high-quality pigment</note>
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp; { Verdigris <note>original: *Viride æris*; the green crust formed on copper, used in alchemy and medicine</note>.

**Supreme Medicine**, when the pure is separated from the impure; that is, when the virtue (power) is separated from the physical substance.

**Sortilege**, is a foretelling or divination by spirits.

**Spagyrus**, or **Spagyric**, is the name given to anyone who knows how to best distinguish the true from the false, to set apart the evil from the good, and to segregate, separate, and cast away the binary <note>original: *binarium*; the state of duality or opposition</note> while the unity is preserved, by separating the impure from the pure. <note>The term "Spagyric" was coined by Paracelsus, meaning to "separate and recombine."</note>

**Spara**, is a mineral force from the *Ilech* <note>a Paracelsian term for the internal, formative impulse of nature</note> of the first substance, from the "first being"—that is, from the greater minerals.

**Sparallium**, a uterine clyster <note>an enema or wash specifically for the womb</note>.

**Sperm of Aqua Fortis**, is its dregs or sediment <note>original: *fœculentia*. Aqua Fortis is nitric acid</note>.

**Sperniolum**, or **Sporniola**, is gore; it is a liquid made of frog spawn, a viscous and mucilaginous matter that congeals in waters.

**Animal Spirit**, is the power of the soul <note>original: *vis animæ*. In this era, "animal" refers to the soul (anima) rather than beasts</note> through which the body approaches, by an acquired likeness, a proportion that is equal to and incorruptible like that of the heavens.

**Squinantia**, is an abscess of the neck <note>commonly known as "Quinsy," a complication of tonsillitis</note>.

**Stannar**, is the mother of metals, a hidden smoke or vapor from which metals are generated.

**Burnt Stellio**, is cinnabar <note>original: *Stellio adustus*. "Stellio" is literally a spotted lizard, used here as a code name for the mineral cinnabar</note>.

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp; { Antimony,

**Styptics**, are medicines that are constricting and drying.

**Struma**, is a goiter or scrofula <note>a swelling of the neck glands</note>.

**Succubus**, is a female nocturnal spirit that deceives men while they sleep, as if they were having an affair with Venus <note>engaging in sexual intercourse</note>. A male spirit that imposes itself on women is called an **Incubus**. **Ephialtes** is a nocturnal suppression <note>commonly known today as sleep paralysis or a nightmare</note>.

**White juice** <note>original: *Succus albus*</note> is quicksilver <term>quicksilver: liquid mercury</term>.

**Suffo** is sowbread <note>original: *panis porcinus*; the plant Cyclamen, used in traditional medicine</note>.

**Sibar**, also called **stella etuta**, is the same as quicksilver.

**Vitriolated sulfur** is sulfur extracted from vitriol <note>vitriol: a sulfate mineral</note> by boiling it in common water, upon which the sulfur floats.

**Somna** is the operation of the stars upon a human being, which occurs at night during sleep.

**Sylo** is a name for the whole world.

**Sydia** is the Moon.

**Sylphs** <note>original: *Sylphes*</note> are called pygmies <note>In Paracelsian philosophy, these are elemental spirits; while Sylphs are usually air spirits, they are here categorized alongside other small nature spirits</note>.

**Wild men** <note>original: *Sylvestres*</note> or **forest-dwellers** <note>original: *sylvani*</note> are men of the air and spirits of the air. These wild men inhabit groves and deserted forest places; they are much taller and more robust in stature than we are, but they do not know how to speak. At one time, such a being was captured by German merchants, who did not keep him very strictly, offering him food and drink. However, he refused to eat or drink, nor would he speak. Overcome by the greatest fear and continuous anxiety, he died after the third day. It is held that they cannot live for long among men, being like a monster born against nature among natural living things—especially among those endowed with reason.

**Disordered Syphita** <note>original: *Syphita prava*</note> is a disease commonly called **St. Vitus' Dance** <note>a disorder characterized by rapid, uncoordinated jerking movements</note>.

**Strict Syphita** <note>original: *Syphita stricta*</note> is the visionary disease of sleepwalkers <note>original: *ambulonum*</note> that occurs at night during sleep.

**Sirens** are sea monsters born from Nymphs.

**Sympathy** is nothing other than a certain harmony of Nature, arising from the preservation of a power <note>original: *virtutis*</note> as it passes from one thing to another. **Antipathy**, however, is a shared feeling between two or more things that is mutually discordant and repelling. Such a state exists in this Macrocosmic nature between this or that type of Air, whether it be a harmonious continuation or a discordant dissension, concerning the pestilential...

...and I call it healthy. This must also be held regarding Water,
and no less regarding Earth. For there are certain Earths that are
**antidotes** <note>original: *Alexipharmacæ*; substances used to ward off or cure poisoning</note>; others are the strongest of poisons.
They say that Crete and Ireland <note>original: *Hibernia*; Ireland was famously believed to be free of snakes and venomous creatures</note> do not nourish poisonous things;
in the territory of Cyrene <note>an ancient Greek and Roman city in modern-day Libya</note>, nothing is more fertile than venomous
animals; in Africa, crawling beasts are both frequent
and monstrous.

In **Microcosmic Nature** <note>original: *Natura Microcosmica*; the Renaissance concept that the human being is a "little world" reflecting the laws and structures of the greater universe or Macrocosm</note>, there is one kind of participation
of feeling of one toward another, such as equals with equals;
another is the resistance between peoples and nations. And
this is the kinship of Nature, or mutual repugnance, either
of the same species among themselves, or of different ones.
Among the same species is the friendship and enmity of man
with man, or lion with lion; or it is between different
species. Such is the harmony of the Dolphin with Man,
but the hatred between man and the viper. There is also
*Sympathy* and *Antipathy* among animals: In some
there is a sharper kind of fight, which drives others away from
food, even if they do not eat the same thing; such is the case
of the dog and the wolf, or when one animal is the food
of another; such as the fox and the hen, the cat and the
mouse, the kite <note>a bird of prey</note> and the chick.

They say the lizard is a friendly animal to man, while the
Crocodile is most hostile to man; the Elephant is
affectionately disposed toward man; yet he truly hates the mouse
so much that he even shrinks from the fodder in which he has
spied a mouse. The dog is wonderfully associated with man;
between wolves and men, there is perpetual contention.

There is also a struggle or friendship among inanimate things:
as you may perceive by the eye in the strings of a **lute** <note>original: *testudinis fidibus*; literally "strings of the tortoise," as lutes were traditionally associated with tortoise shells. This refers to sympathetic resonance, where plucking one string causes another to vibrate</note>.

There is also a mutual antipathy of poisons: as between
the Scorpion and **aconite** <note>original: *aconitum*; a highly toxic flowering plant also known as monkshood or wolfsbane</note>.

There is also a mutual *Sympathy* and *Antipathy* among
vegetables. There is an ineffable familiarity between oil and **myr-**

...so much so that the branches and twigs of the Myrtle creep pleasantly through oil <note>The author refers to the ancient observation that certain plants thrive in the presence of specific substances</note>, and the roots of both are joined in a friendly way by a mutual embrace; nor does any other plant rise up more beside oil than the Myrtle. Great is the fellowship of asparagus with the reed, but the grapevine does not love the laurel. There is also a **Sympathy** and **Antipathy** of vegetables with this or that earth.

The Poplar, Willow, and Smartweed <note>original: *Persicaria*</note> love the waters. Clover, Satyrion <note>a type of orchid believed to have aphrodisiac qualities</note>, and Cowslip <note>original: *herba Paralysis*; literally "paralysis herb," used to treat tremors</note> love the meadows. Oregano and Ground-pine love the mountains. Vegetables also have their certain times for producing.

There is also a concord and discord of minerals and of metals. Quicksilver <note>original: *Argentum vivum*; Mercury</note> is a friend to gold; it does not allow other metals to pass through or be supported by it, but it yields to gold so that it sinks down. All other metals float upon it, except for gold; it draws that alone to itself and embraces it. Who is ignorant of the stupendous antipathy of Sulfur and Niter <note>original: *Nitri*; Saltpeter. When combined, these form the basis of gunpowder, representing a violent reaction or "antipathy"</note>?

Furthermore, in certain natural bodies, there are peculiar **Magnetic virtues** <note>In this context, "magnetic" refers to an occult power of attraction or healing, not just literal magnetism</note> for curing defects and diseases. A scorpion heals its own inflicted wound; a dried toad extracts the poison of an Anthrax <note>a malignant boil or "coal" sore</note> or a pestilential Carbuncle. A frog cures the bite of venomous creatures. There is also a magnetic and specific virtue in Crabs for the bite of a rabid dog; in Moles for whitlows <note>original: *Parenychiam*; an infection of the fingertip</note>; in the human skull for Epilepsy; in snakes suffocated in oil for malignant ulcers; and in the blood of swallows for Epilepsy. Eggshells from which chicks have hatched are effective against the Stone <note>original: *Calculum*; kidney or bladder stones</note>, and the blood of turtledoves against Apoplexy and Paralysis.

***Arched Tile*** <note>original: *Tegula fornicata*</note>, is an earthen vessel open to air vents and a door, oblong in shape for covering crucibles <note>original: *catillos*; small cup-like vessels used in assaying or melting metals</note>, so that the work is not polluted by embers, and shaped so that the heat can work the material more temperately. Some connect this tile with a base; others make the base separate from it: which is left to anyone’s discretion.
